## Mutation gates for ops, trash and watch each week, and a release that waits for the CI and builds with no right to write, 2026-11-22 (specs.md §26, §27; threat model G21)

Two items done while the maintainer was away, who said to go on: TODO.md's gates for `jobs`, `ops`, `trash` and `watch`, as proposed there, and the threat model's gap G21, as proposed there but its `toolchain` line, which the maintainer's rule of 2026-11-22 leaves out. Commits `e5fe28e`, `362aabe` and the one that records this, on `worktree-agent-abebeea235ec075e0`, on `beta`'s `25a5687`.

- **Measured** with gremlins v0.6.0 and `test/ci/mutation.sh` as it stands (`--timeout-coefficient 20`, each test run with one CPU), on `25a5687`, the machine loaded by other work throughout (killed / lived / not covered / timed out, efficacy, mutator coverage): `ops` 653/123/669/0, 84.15 %, 53.70 %, in 39 min with 6 workers, gremlins' first run of its tests 27 s; `trash` whole for the first time, 476/100/58/3, 82.64 %, 90.85 %, in 1 h 23 min with 8 workers, the first run of its tests 50 s, so 17 minutes for a mutant that times out; `watch` 943/199/294/5, 82.57 %, 79.53 %, in 1 h 3 min with 6 workers, through the new gate, which it passes, its code that of `1be6613`, a test added since. On `1be6613` (2026-11-21) `ops` had given 83.68 % and 53.56 %, `watch` 82.66 % and 79.53 %; `trash` 85.37 % and 90.71 % on its first 226 mutants, `trash.go` not reached.
- **What lives**: in `ops`, 66 of the 123 are a condition negated, most an `err != nil` after a step made `err == nil`, so that the operation returns once the step succeeds, a sync or what follows skipped, which its own tests do not look behind (`disk.go` 37, `linkguard.go` 22, `view.go` 19, `repair.go` 14); its not covered are mostly the reprise, `recover.go` 254, which the tests of `jobs` and `trash` run. In `trash`, 33 of the 100 are a logical operator inverted, `trash.go` 47 and `restoreto.go` 23; `conflicts.go:159:13`, `s.apply && summary.Stayed == 0` made `||`, lives again: a dry run of `conflicts resolve` would resolve the conflict in the catalog, which no test of `trash` sees. In `watch`, as on 2026-11-21, mostly the comparison of a walk to the catalog, `scan.go` 53 and `verdict.go` 47, then `watcher.go` 28, `capacity.go` 17, `notify.go` 12 and `cache.go` 11; one mutant of the same code went from killed to lived between the two runs, 944/198 then 943/199, what a loaded machine can do.
- **No mutant named equivalent** in the three: those looked at are tests missing, not changes no input tells apart. In `ops`, `create.go:39:12` and `disk.go:324:12` make a `chmod` to the mode a folder already has, which moves its change time, which Atoll's own looks compare; `disk.go:112`, `||` made `&&` in the check that another mount is not at the disk's place, lives as no test changes one of the mount's id, device and inode alone.
- **The gates** (`test/ci/gates.txt`), as proposed in TODO.md: `mutation ./internal/ops 83 53`, `mutation ./internal/trash 82 90`, `mutation ./internal/watch 82 79`, each at its measure rounded down to a whole percent; `test/ci/mutation-gate.sh` and `test/ci/mutation.sh` unchanged, as they read any package's gate. `ops` stays at 83, the 2026-11-21 measure's, though it gives 84.15 % now: 84 would leave one mutant of margin. Tried: each of today's logs passes its gate (exit 0), and the `trash` log with five of its killed made lived, 81.77 %, fails it (exit 1).
- **The weekly run** (`.github/workflows/mutation-weekly.yml`): Sundays at 04:23 UTC on `beta`, and on demand, never on a push; a job for each of `ops`, `trash` and `watch`, at once, `fail-fast` off, each `bash test/ci/mutation.sh <package>` in the CI's image, 330 minutes at most, under GitHub's 6 hours; the summary, gremlins' log and JSON as an artifact of its own, as the nightly run does. The nightly `mutation.yml` keeps the pure core, and its dispatch still mutates any package named, gated when it has a gate.
- **`jobs`**: no gate and no weekly run. gremlins runs every test of the package against each mutant, 28 minutes a run with one CPU on 2026-11-21, so its 1,895 mutants reached would take days; a tool that runs only the tests that reach a mutant is what it needs (TODO.md).
- **The release** (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.goreleaser.yaml`), in three jobs, the workflow given no permission and each job only its own (§26):
  1. `ci`, `actions: read`: lists through the API the runs of `ci.yml` on the tag's commit (`head_sha`), keeps those a push or a dispatch made, the commit checked again in `jq`, and goes on for one that ended in success, every job of it passing; while one is queued or running, it waits, a minute at a time, three hours at most; otherwise it stops and says to run the CI on the tag, then the release again. A pull request's run does not count: it tests the merge of its head into its base, another commit.
  2. `build`, `contents: read`: the checkout with its history, Go (the latest patch of `go.mod`'s version), syft, then `goreleaser release --clean --skip=publish`, with no token; `.goreleaser.yaml` now says `release.disable`, so GoReleaser builds, packs, writes the SBOMs, `SHA256SUMS` and `dist/CHANGELOG.md`, and publishes nothing. A step writes the notes, the header GoReleaser's `release.header` held and the changelog, to `dist/notes.md`; `SHA256SUMS` is checked and its digest given as the job's output; the archives, packages, SBOMs, `SHA256SUMS` and notes go to the artifact `dist`, kept 7 days.
  3. `draft`, `contents: write`, `id-token: write` and `attestations: write`, with no checkout: downloads `dist` (`actions/download-artifact` v8.0.2, pinned by `9000827`, which fails on a digest that is not the upload's), checks `SHA256SUMS` against the build's output and every file against it, each archive, package and SBOM listed there; attests the archives and packages (`actions/attest-build-provenance`, as before); then `gh release create --draft --verify-tag`, titled `Atoll <tag>`, a prerelease when the tag has a suffix, with the archives, packages, SBOMs and `SHA256SUMS`. A draft of the tag from an earlier run has its title, notes and files replaced (`gh release edit`, `gh release upload --clobber`); a published release stops the job, untouched.
- **Images by digest**: every container image of `ci.yml`, `fuzz.yml`, `mutation.yml` and `mutation-weekly.yml`, of `test/ci/run.sh`, `test/tla/run.sh` and `test/mediaservers/run.sh`, and of the `Makefile` (`test-qemu`, `release-snapshot`), pinned by the digest of its multi-platform index that its tag named on 2026-11-22, the tag in a comment: `golangci/golangci-lint:v2.15.0` `sha256:fbfcfd12…`, `eclipse-temurin:21.0.13_9-jre-noble` `sha256:b4f9d2a0…` (the tag's rebuild of 2026-11-20; this machine had its build of 2026-11-11, `977602aa…`), `debian:13` `sha256:9c6e3814…` (built 2026-11-04) and `goreleaser/goreleaser:v2.20.3` `sha256:3019e0e6…`. README.md's command for a snapshot named GoReleaser v2.20.0, the Makefile v2.20.3: it names the digest now. The media servers' images stay at `latest`, as what that test watches is the vendors' images changing.
- **No `toolchain` line** in `go.mod`, as the maintainer confirmed on 2026-11-22 Go 1.27.4 as its minimum with none (the entry of 2026-11-20, "Go 1.27.4, the modules and the tools"): a release takes the latest patch of 1.27, a fix of the standard library at once, and the binary records it. The threat model's row says so.
- **The ruleset** that keeps tags `v*` to the maintainer is a setting of GitHub's that no file makes: its steps are in README.md, "Protecting the tags `v*`, on GitHub", for him (TODO.md).
- **Found**: the API lists no run of any workflow on `main`, none of the repository's 56, so none on `b6c1104`, #23's merge: a tag there is refused until the CI is dispatched on it (TODO.md).
- **Found** too: the CI fails on `beta`'s `25a5687`, its `race` job, a `DATA RACE` in `TestPlexScansWithinABudget` (`internal/notify`), on the push and on the pull request; the release's check refuses that commit (TODO.md).
- **Verified**, each in Docker:
  - actionlint 1.7.12, with its shellcheck, on the six workflows: no error. shellcheck v0.11.0 on the scripts changed: nothing new, the notes it gives were there before (`SC2329` on the functions `test/ci/run.sh` calls by name, `SC2125` in `mutation.sh`).
  - The release's `build` job and the checks of its `draft` job, in GoReleaser's image by its digest, on a repository made inside the container from this tree, with an `origin` as a checkout has and a local tag `v0.0.1-test.1` that never left it: `goreleaser release --clean --skip=publish`, with no token, built in 1 min 7 s the two archives, the two packages, their SBOMs, `SHA256SUMS` listing those six, and `dist/CHANGELOG.md`; then the notes step; then the six files, `SHA256SUMS` and the notes alone, as the artifact holds them, checked against `SHA256SUMS` and its digest, each listed there; the tag read as a prerelease. GoReleaser needs the `origin` remote even with nothing to publish.
  - The `ci` job's step, its text cut from `release.yml`, in `debian:13` by its digest with jq 1.7 from Debian's archive, `gh` answering from files and `sleep` doing nothing, against nine answers of the API: three real ones of the repository, saved through `gh api` (`3f7a10e`, passed: it goes on; `dedb9c2`, cancelled, and `25a5687`, failed: refused), and six made up (another commit's runs, none, a pull request's success alone: refused; a dispatch's success beside a push's failure: it goes on; a run going then passed: it goes on after two waits; then failed: refused). Each as expected.
  - `make model-check`'s script in the Java image by its new digest: the eleven instances, each as it expects, `Copy` 3,862,283 states.
  - `go test` of `TestThePolkitRuleLetsAtollStartAMountUnitAlone`, which reads `.goreleaser.yaml`, and of `conformance/models` (the fingerprints: no section they hold changed).
  - `make release-snapshot`: this machine has no `make`; its `docker run`, as README.md gives it for Windows, run in this worktree, built every file but read no git there, as the Makefile says of a worktree (version `0.0.1-snapshot.none`); it was not run from a clone.
- **Not verified**: no run on GitHub of `release.yml` or `mutation-weekly.yml`; `gh release create`, `edit` and `upload`, and the attestation, were not run, as they publish; the runner's jq is 1.7.1, the test's 1.7; how long `trash` takes on GitHub's four CPUs.
- **Decided without the maintainer, to confirm**:
  - the gates at the levels TODO.md proposed, `ops` at 83 though it measures 84.15 % now, `trash` at its first whole run's 82 and 90, `watch` at 82 and 79, which it meets again on `25a5687` by about six mutants; no mutant named equivalent;
  - a workflow of its own, `mutation-weekly.yml`, rather than a second schedule of `mutation.yml`: its own group of concurrency, so that a run of hours never holds the nightly one, a job per package at once, Sundays at 04:23 UTC on `beta`, 330 minutes a job;
  - only a push's or a dispatch's run of `ci.yml` on the very commit counts, never a pull request's; the release waits up to three hours for one going rather than refusing at once; `ci.yml` found by its file name;
  - the draft made with `gh` by the release's last job rather than by GoReleaser, whose free version cannot publish what another job built: `release.disable` in `.goreleaser.yaml`, `--skip=publish`, no token given to the build, and the notes' header moved from `.goreleaser.yaml` into `release.yml`, word for word, the repository's name taken from the run;
  - a draft of the tag already there has its title, notes and files replaced, `gh release edit` and `upload --clobber`, rather than deleted and made again as GoReleaser's `replace_existing_draft` did; a published release stops the job;

  - each image pinned by the digest its tag names today, `eclipse-temurin`'s rebuild of 2026-11-20 rather than the build of 2026-11-11 this machine had run, the model checked again on it; GoReleaser's image, which no workflow uses, pinned too, in the Makefile and README.md; `test/qemu/README.md`'s commands to type kept with their tags; the media servers' images at `latest`;
  - the ruleset's steps in README.md's "Releasing", where the release is told, rather than `deploy/README.md`: a tag ruleset on `v*`, active, the repository's admin role alone allowed past it, creations, updates and deletions restricted, force pushes blocked.

## Six low gaps of the threat model: G11, G13, G14, G17, G19 and G20, 2026-11-22 (specs.md §14, §15.1, §15.13, §21.1, §21.2, §25, §26)

The view, the watcher, the units and the catalog's files, as `docs/threat-model.md` lists them, with the maintainer away: each with a test that failed first, no contract changed (no schema, layout, configuration key, API, `--json` schema, exit code, state, reason or alert kind). Commits `43b5828` (G13), `ef80047` (G11), `cb73c50` (G17), `7304065` (G19), `a2bb2cb` (G20, G14) and the one that records this, on `worktree-agent-a0f1d41dc0c8f85da`, on `beta`'s `25a5687`.

- **G13, a file the view makes was a setuid file of `atoll`'s for a moment.** `ops.(*Disk).CreateUserFile` created it with the mode asked, setuid and setgid included, then gave it to the caller and set the mode again. It is now made 0600, given its owner, then its mode, through its handle, as a copy's `.part` is (§14.2); one whose owner the driver refuses stays `atoll`'s without setuid and setgid, as a replica that could not take its source's owner (§14). Test first: `TestAFileTheViewMakesIsPrivateUntilItIsTheCallers` looks at the file at its `fchown` and its `fchmod` through the fault layer, and found it `ugrwxr-xr-x` before the owner was set; §21.2.
- **G11, a folder presented the first disk's mode alone.** A folder of Atoll's own view now presents the first disk's entry with its mode narrowed by every other folder at its path on a disk that presents, each as it would present alone, so that no caller is given by the permission bits what one of them may refuse it there (`view.Present`, `narrow`). With one owner and one group everywhere, that is the bits all of them give, class by class; where an owner or a group differs, nothing says which groups an account is in, so each class keeps the bits that every class a caller of it may fall in on that disk gives: the first's owner is there of the group or of the others, and a caller of the first's group or others may be that disk's owner. The sticky bit of any is presented; setuid, setgid, the owner, the group, the size and the time stay the first's, as mergerfs presents them, and a `chmod` or `chown` through the view already reaches every disk. A folder whose disks disagree is thus stricter through the view than through mergerfs, which §21.2 now says. Vectors first: 11 cases added to `conformance/vectors/view_node.json` and 3 changed (`TestPresentVectors`), `TestTree` updated; in qemu, `TestViewAsAnotherAccount` lists a folder 0755 on one disk and 0700 on the other, as a walk recorded them, and the kernel refuses it the listing (`EACCES`), where it lists one 0755 on both.
- **G17, any account's process could pass for the view's or Atoll's own.** The watcher now tells the process behind an event by its real account first, the first field of `Uid` in `/proc/<pid>/status` (`view.ParseProcStatus`, pinned by `conformance/vectors/proc_status.json`, which gained the field and two cases), which must be one of the accounts Atoll takes as its own, `ops.TrustedOwners` as the daemon passes it, root, its own and the catalog's owner; then by its executable as `/proc/<pid>/exe` names it, ` (deleted)` left out: Atoll's own, its command `mount` for the view, or one named `mergerfs` with the view's mount among its arguments. Its `comm` is read only where `/proc` does not let the watcher read the executable: the kernel lets a process read another account's executable only when it may trace it, which the daemon, run as `atoll` without `CAP_SYS_PTRACE`, may not of root's mergerfs, whose events the old check by `comm` told as the view's. The real account, not the effective one, as mergerfs moves the effective, saved and filesystem ids of a thread to its caller's (`setreuid(-1, caller)`) and keeps the real one, root's. A process gone by the time `/proc` is read, or of any other account, is a user's, as before. Tests first: `TestOnlyTheViewAndAtollOfTheirAccountsMakeTheirEvents`, 12 cases: a shell named `mergerfs` by a link, whose `comm` says so, and a copy of a shell named `mergerfs` or `atoll` run as 65534 are a user's; `TestEventsSayWhoMadeThem`'s stand-in for mergerfs is now a copy of a shell under that name, not a link to one; `TestWatcherOnEachDriver` in qemu, a real mergerfs as root.
- **G19, the units' PATH, and mergerfs's branches through a link.** Both units set `Environment=PATH=/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin`, the system's own folders, whatever systemd's default or a `DefaultEnvironment=` says (`TestTheUnitsSetTheirPath`, and in qemu the `PATH` of each running service, in `checkRunsAsShipped`); `deploy/README.md` lists it with the hardening. The runtime branches are now read and written only through a handle on `view.mount`, opened `O_PATH|O_NOFOLLOW`, the top of its mount (`STATX_ATTR_MOUNT_ROOT`), whose type the mount table, read by that mount's id, gives as `fuse.mergerfs`, with `lgetxattr` and `lsetxattr` on `/proc/self/fd/<n>/.mergerfs` (`view.ReadBranches`, `WriteBranches`). Test first: `TestTheBranchesAreMergerfssAlone`, where a plain folder holding a `.mergerfs` with the attribute, a link named `.mergerfs` and a link at the mount point were read and written through; the positive way against a real mergerfs in qemu (`TestBranchModes`, `TestDaemonRunsForGood`, `TestTheDaemonUnderSystemd`). §15.13, §21.1, §26.
- **G20, a catalog elsewhere was readable by every account.** Each folder made for the catalog or its backups is now 0750 whatever the umask, given its mode through a handle on it, one there already left as it is (`fsx.MkdirAllClosed`); the database, at `atoll init` and `catalog rebuild` (`catalog.Create`), and each backup's part before `VACUUM INTO` fills it, are made empty 0640 (`fsx.CreateClosed`), and SQLite makes the write-ahead log and the shared memory with the database's mode, as measured. Tests first: `TestTheCatalogIsClosedToOtherAccounts`, under the masks 0000, 0022 and 0077, found the database and its log 0644 under the shell's 0022; `TestWhatTheCatalogMakesIsClosed`. §25, §26, `deploy/README.md`.
- **G14, ACLs, documented.** §14 and §21.2 now say what a replica and the view without ACLs mean: the mode's group bits are an ACL's mask, so a file's group has what the mask gives, which can be more than the ACL's entry for it, and an account or a group the ACL named has what the mode gives the others or the group; and to keep the library's access in modes, owners and groups. No alert: one for a file that holds an ACL would be a condition of its own, a new kind in §23's closed list and in the values of `atoll_alert`'s label, which this work was not to add, and would take a read of each file's `system.posix_acl_access` at each walk. §14 is in `conformance/models/fingerprints.json`: `Replication.tla` models no attribute, and is unchanged; the fingerprint is updated and `make model-check` passes.
- **Verified**, in the CI's image: `gofmt`; `go vet ./...`, with `-tags conformance` and `-tags loopdev`; `go test ./...` (`-timeout 60m`, 1111 s); `go test -race` of `view`, `watch`, `catalog`, `fsx`, `ops`, `daemon` and `cmd/atoll`; `make conformance`; `golangci-lint run ./...`, with `--build-tags conformance` and `--build-tags loopdev`, no issue; `make model-check` (`test/tla/run.sh`), every instance as it expects. In qemu, on Linux 6.12 (Debian 13) and 7.3.5 (mainline), both runs of each guest, the shell's and systemd's: `TestView.*` (`TestViewAsAnotherAccount` with its folder of two modes, `TestViewWrites`, `TestViewMatchesMergerfs`, `TestViewXattrs`, `TestViewWritesInParallel`, `TestViewBrowsesNoDisk`, `TestViewMovesAFileThatRunsOutOfSpace`), `TestReadonlyMount.*`, `TestBreakGlass.*`, `TestWatcherOnEachDriver`, `TestBranchModes`, `TestDaemonRunsForGood`, `TestRemountAsTheDedicatedUser`, and every `*UnderSystemd` test, the units' `PATH` checked in each, all passing; on 6.12 twice, once before G20 and once on the tree as committed, and a third time with `VERBOSE=1` for `TestViewAsAnotherAccount` and `TestWatcherOnEachDriver`, which shows both run, not skipped, the type `ntfs` aside, which 6.12 has not. Not verified: a daemon of the account `atoll` telling root's mergerfs by its `comm` was not watched for in qemu, where no test looks at the origin of the events the daemon under systemd hears; the docker test reads it so of a process of 65534 that root there may not trace.
- **Decided without the maintainer, to confirm**:
  - G11: the owner, the group, setuid and setgid of a folder are its first disk's, as mergerfs presents them, and only its permission bits are narrowed, the sticky bit kept from any; where owners or groups differ the mode can fall to 0000 for all but root, the safest, rather than an alert for folders that disagree; a `failed` disk's folder narrows it too, as the view lists what it holds; a folder whose row lacks its mode or owner narrows as it would present alone, 0755 of the view's own owner, which is strict when that owner is another;
  - G13: a file whose owner the driver refuses loses setuid and setgid, as a replica does, rather than keep the mode asked as `atoll`'s; a folder the view makes is left as it was, made with its mode then given its owner and mode by `Adopt`, which refuses a folder that holds anything by then;
  - G17: the real account, not the effective or the filesystem one; the accounts trusted are `ops.TrustedOwners`'s, those of the work folders and of F061, for the view's mergerfs too, so a mergerfs run by another account reads as a user's, the safe way; where the daemon may not read a trusted process's executable, its `comm` is taken, for mergerfs alone, never for Atoll's own;
  - G19: the `PATH` leaves `/usr/local` out, which systemd's default holds, as a `smartctl` put there would run with the daemon's capabilities: one installed there by hand needs a drop-in; and `PATH` in the units rather than absolute paths found at start, which keeps a daemon run by hand working with its caller's `PATH`;
  - G20: 0750 and 0640, the group's read the unit gives `/var/lib/atoll`, rather than 0700 and 0600, as a member of the group `atoll` reads the catalog (`deploy/README.md`); a strict umask such as 0077 is opened to those modes, as the configuration is written 0640 whatever the mask; a catalog, or a folder, there already keeps its mode;
  - G14: documentation alone, the alert left to the maintainer.
- **Left** (TODO.md): G14's alert; a scenario of the modes `atoll init` gives, as the runner's `expect_system` compares no mode; until a pass takes it in, a folder given an extended attribute through the view presents the look of one disk, as before, not the strictest of its folders.

## The threat model's gaps G7, G9, G10 and G12, the local API and the network, 2026-11-22 (specs.md §23.1, §24.4, §25.1)

Four low gaps of `docs/threat-model.md`, made while the maintainer is away, as he said to go on: each the mitigation its row proposed, with no contract change (no SQL schema, layout, configuration key, API path, response schema, `--json` schema, exit code, state, reason or alert kind). On `worktree-agent-acf8888db8f376f40`, cut from `beta`'s `25a5687`, in the commit that records this. Each test was written before its code; those of G7 and G12 did not build before it, and `TestTheMetricsAnswerOnlyTheirAddress` failed, 200 where 421 was wanted.

- **G7, which account answered a guard over the socket**: the socket's server reads, for each connection it accepts, who connected, as the kernel recorded it (`SO_PEERCRED`), through the wrapper that caps the connections (`placedConn.NetConn`, `fsx.PeerOf`, `withPeer` as the server's `ConnContext`); the log line of a recorded answer, "the local API answered a guard", says `uid` and `pid` beside the disk, the guard, the answer, `up_to` and the remote address, which is empty there. The events are unchanged. Tests: `TestPeerOf` (fsx: a socket's peer, through one and two wrappers, nothing for TCP, nil or a wrapper that gives itself), `TestTheLogSaysWhoAnsweredOverTheSocket` (an answer through a real socket of the daemon's server: the log names this process's account and number, no event holds them).
- **G9, the API's token**: (a) a §24.4 warning at `api.token_file` for a token under 32 bytes, its last line break left out as the API compares it, and for a file that holds none, which refuses every request over TCP; from `atoll rules check` and in the daemon's log at its start and each reload, as every warning; the token taken all the same, never quoted (`tokenWarnings`, `cmd/atoll/secrets.go`). Tests: `TestAShortTokenIsWarned`, scenario `rules-check-short-token`; the token of `rules-check-secret-files` is now 64 bytes, so that it still tests modes alone. (b) A 401 over TCP is sent 250 ms after its request, the wait ending with its client; the right token waits for nothing (`apiRefusedWait`, `withToken`). The 32 places of `api.listen` give 128 tries a second at most; a place is taken at the accept, before any request, and a connection that sends nothing holds one 10 s already, and an idle one 60 s, so the wait fills none faster than today. Test: `TestARefusedTokenWaits` (two refusals wait 250 ms at least; with a wait of 30 s, the right token is answered within its 10 s; a client that gives up after 200 ms over a real connection ends the wait).
- **G10, `metrics.listen` and DNS rebinding**: `value.Listen.NamedBy`, pure: a `Host` names a listen address when it is that address in any form `api.listen`'s grammar takes, with its port, 80 when left out, or for a loopback address `localhost`, in any case of its ASCII letters, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]`; nothing is resolved. `metrics.listen` answers 421 with `{"error": …}`, which does not say the address, to any other `Host` (`namedOnly`). `api.listen` answers any `Host`, as before. 59 vectors, `conformance/vectors/listen_host.json`, with the flag `misdirected`, added to the loader's list and to the vectors' README; `FuzzListenHost` checks against `net/netip` that a `Host` named is the address or a loopback one with its port, and that `String` is always named. Tests: `TestListenHostVectors`, `FuzzListenHost` (30 s, 5.5 million runs, nothing found), `TestTheMetricsAnswerOnlyTheirAddress` (the server as the daemon starts it: 200 for the address, `localhost`, `LOCALHOST` and `[::1]` with the port, 421 for a name, no port, another port, `localhost` with no port and `127.0.0.2`; `api.listen` with its token answers another name). `api/openapi.yaml` says the 421 under `/metrics`, and the 401's quarter of a second; `deploy/README.md` says a scrape names the address.
- **G12, `100.64.0.0/10`**: §25.1 says the range is trusted as Tailscale's by the address alone. Once `api.listen` or `metrics.listen` listens on an address of it, the daemon lists the machine's interfaces and warns in its log when none whose name begins with `tailscale` holds it, naming those that do, or when they cannot be listed; it listens all the same (`carrierNATDoubt`, `warnCarrierNAT`, `machineInterfaces`). Test: `TestAnAddressOfCarrierGradeNATIsDoubted` (ten cases of interfaces, the log line, interfaces that cannot be listed, none listed for a loopback address, and the machine's own list holding 127.0.0.1).
- **Verified**, in the CI's image: `gofmt`; `go vet ./...`, and with `-tags conformance` and `-tags loopdev`; `go test ./...` (`-timeout 60m`, `jobs` 1143 s); `go test -race` on `cmd/atoll`, `internal/config`, `internal/fsx` and `internal/value`; `make conformance`, 655 scenarios; `golangci-lint run ./...`, with `--build-tags conformance` and with `--build-tags loopdev`, 0 issues each; `FuzzListenHost` for 30 s. Not run: the loop-device tests and qemu, which none of this touches; the mutation gate of `internal/value`, which runs each night; a real Tailscale or carrier-grade NAT interface, which the container has not, so the warning of G12 is tested on interfaces given to it, and the machine's own list only for its loopback.

Decided without the maintainer, to confirm:

- G7: the log alone, `uid` and `pid`, not the group; on a recorded answer alone, as the log had it, not on a refusal or a dry run; what the kernel recorded at the connection, so a process that passed its connection on, or ended and left its number to another, is still the one named.
- G9: 250 ms, each refusal on its own, not a queue of every refusal, which would let a client with no token hold the 32 places longer; the wait before the 401 is sent, the connection kept open after it. The warning wherever `api.token_file` is set, `api.listen` or not, and for an empty token too; 32 bytes counted, not their entropy.
- G10: 421 Misdirected Request rather than 403, as RFC 9110 has it for a request to an authority the server does not answer, and 403 already says a loose token file in this API. A name is never taken, MagicDNS's included: a scrape that names the machine by a name now gets 421 and must name its address; taking the machine's own names would need a resolver or a list in the configuration. An empty `Host`, as HTTP/1.0 may send, is refused too; `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]` are taken for any loopback address listened on, no other address of `127.0.0.0/8`. `api.listen` left answering any `Host`: it asks for its token, which a rebound page has not, and a phone or Home Assistant may name it by a name.
- G12: at the start alone, as a changed `api.listen` takes effect at the next start; after the listen succeeded, as one that failed already says the API is off; the prefix `tailscale` as written, in its case; Tailscale's IPv6 range, its own, gets no check.

## Two pools' daemons told apart, a rename that no longer waits for a failed disk, and Jellyfin and Emby within a budget, 2026-11-22 (specs.md §15.12, §15.13, §21.5)

Three items of TODO.md, as the maintainer asked ("après tu continue sur ça").

- **Two pools of one account**: a disk command or a guard's answer told every daemon of Atoll's executable that acted as one of Atoll's accounts, so on a machine with two pools under one account, it told the other pool's daemon too, which read its own configuration again for nothing, and counted it, which hid that this pool's ran none. A daemon is now told only when its configuration is the command's, the same file by device and inode: its `--config`, or the default, read through `/proc/<pid>/root`, or `/proc/<pid>/cwd` for a relative path. `TestOnlyADaemonOfThisConfigurationIsTold` starts five processes with the daemon's arguments: its path, a path relative to its folder and a link to it are told; another configuration and the default are not. `fsx.SameFile` is new.
- **A rename that waited for good for a failed disk** (§15.12, step 3): `folderLike` counted a `failed` disk, which no pass walks, as a disk not looked at that may hold the model of a new parent, so with one in the configuration a rename whose parent no disk present holds waited for good, its copy left behind. A failed disk is now none to wait for, as for a copy (the maintainer, 2026-11-22): the parent is made with no model, 0755. `TestAParentWhoseHolderFailedIsNotWaitedFor`, which waited before; §15.12's fingerprint updated, `Replication.tla` unchanged as it leaves folders out.
- **Jellyfin and Emby within a budget** (§21.5): as Plex's scans are since 2026-11-22, all the requests of one notification, a hundred folders each, are sent within 10 s and 1 s more a request, the rest tried again later: a server that answered each slowly held the daemon's turn 10 s a request. The base of both budgets is one variable now, `notifyBase`. `TestMediaBrowserTellsWithinABudget`.

Decided without the maintainer, to confirm:

- A daemon whose configuration this command cannot look at, as one of a process it may not look into, is none of this pool's: it is not told, and the command says no daemon runs, as before a non-root command could not signal another account's daemon either.
- The budget of Jellyfin and Emby, 1 s a request of a hundred folders, ten times Plex's for a scan, as each request names a hundred.
- The notifications stay in the daemon's turn, as the maintainer chose for Plex on 2026-11-22.

## The maintainer's answers of 2026-11-22 (specs.md §10, §14.2, §17.2, §21.2, §21.5, §27)

- **Confirmed**: every choice the entries of 2026-11-20 to 2026-11-22 listed as to confirm, each saying so now. Seven had a question of their own, each answered with the option put first ("Confirmer"), the rest together ("Tout confirmer"):
  - an NTFS journal, `$LogFile`, absent, unreadable or invalid makes the disk `readonly`, `hibernated`, as one Windows left in use does;
  - in the reprise of `view.move_on_enospc`, a trash with no room never removes the copy at its path: it stays, the move planned, tried again at the next start;
  - a copy edited through the library's name, then that name deleted, between the link and the look, goes back to its path: the deletion undone, the edit kept;
  - a `failed` or retired disk is not away for a copy's folder model: no copy waits for it, and a folder it alone held is made 0755;
  - the copy looks for its folder's model before it copies, at the cost of a disk woken now and then, rather than copy again at each pass;
  - the floor of `make cover` at the measure of 2026-11-21, 84.4 % and 88.9 %, never lowered from there;
  - Plex's scans bounded and asked within a budget, in the daemon's turn, rather than the notifications moved out of it.

## The audit's two other findings, F020 and F050, and the tools' three, 2026-11-22 (specs.md §9, §21.5)

The rest of the audit by scrutineer of 2026-11-22 on `main`, from its list of findings: eight in all, the three of the entry below among them.

- **F020, an id from a name on a disk moved the catalog's counter to its end** (medium): real on `beta`. `HighestOpID`, `HighestLinkID` and the trash a rebuild lists again took any id a name of the work folder gave, up to 2^63 - 1. One folder `trash/<date>/9223372036854775807/`, or a part or a link of that id, which anyone who may write the work folder can make, moved `sqlite_sequence` to SQLite's largest id at `atoll init` or `catalog rebuild`, past which it gives no other: the new catalog could record no operation or file. `catalog.MaxID`, 2^53 - 1, is now the highest id a catalog gives, the largest integer a JSON number holds exactly; the highest ids and the trash listed again leave out a name past it, the trash's told of as no entry and left, and a counter is never moved past it, nor an entry listed at one. The names Atoll writes and reads are unchanged (`conformance/vectors/work_names.json` still reads 63 bits), so is the layout of format version 1. Tests first: `TestNoIDPastThoseACatalogGivesIsTaken` (ops), `TestNoCounterPastTheIDsACatalogGives` (catalog), and the scenario `catalog-rebuild-ids-past`, which failed on the build before at `atoll init`, its counter 9223372036854775807.
- **F050, what Plex answers set how many scans the daemon asks for** (medium): real on `beta`. The answer of `/library/sections` was read up to 4 MiB, but then each library folder that holds a folder that changed was asked for a scan, one request after the other, in the daemon's turn: a hostile answer of thousands of libraries that each hold `/` made thousands of requests a folder, each up to 10 s, the passes waiting. Plex's scans are now planned before any is asked: more than 1,024 library folders in the answer, or a folder in more than 8, asks for none, an error tried again later; and the plan is asked within 10 s and 100 ms a scan. Tests first: `TestPlexAsksForABoundedNumberOfScans` and `TestPlexScansWithinABudget`.
- **Three findings of the tools, none a defect**: `generic-api-key` at `conformance/frozen/v1.json:16` is the SHA-256 of the migration `0012_ops_by_key.sql`; `use-of-unsafe-block` at `internal/fsx/power.go:68` is the `SG_IO` ioctl of ATA CHECK POWER MODE, its header passed as `x/sys` passes one, the buffers it points to kept alive over the call; `string-formatted-query` at `internal/catalog/view.go:201` builds its query from the table names `files` and `entries` alone, every value bound. So that a scan does not raise them again, as the maintainer asked: the view's queries are now written whole for each of its two tables, and a table it does not know is an error (`TestNamesBelowReadsOnlyItsTables`, which a crafted name read before); and the ioctl says why it needs `unsafe`, with semgrep's `nosemgrep` on its line.

Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22 ("Confirmer"):

- The bound of ids, 2^53 - 1, rather than a lower one or SQLite's own: it leaves 9 × 10^15 ids, and keeps every id exact in `--json`.
- The bounds of Plex: 1,024 library folders and 8 libraries a folder, both far past a home's server; and a budget, not a turn of the notifications outside the daemon's loop, as Jellyfin and Emby already send a hundred folders a request.

## The audit by scrutineer of 2026-11-22: three findings, two fixed on beta before, one fixed (specs.md §15.13, §25.1)

The maintainer ran scrutineer on `main` (`b6c1104`, #23's merge) and exported three findings, each rated low; their `DupCheck` names two more, F020 (the catalog's id counters) and F050 (the requests to Plex), that the export did not hold. Each was checked against `beta`.

- **F030, `fsx.Dir.Names()` listed a handle once**: a second listing of one handle gave an empty folder and no error, while its comment said the opposite. Fixed on `beta` by `2a6dfa9` (2026-11-20) before the audit: each listing seeks to the start, under a lock, and `TestNamesGivesEveryNameEachTime` lists one handle twice through the real and the fault layers. Nothing to do.
- **F060, `/v1/disks/{id}` read the catalog outside the turn of one answer at a time**: fixed on `beta` by `4e4015f` (2026-11-20), the route behind `one()` and `checks()`. The audit was right that the test of the turn wrapped an answer of its own and could not see a route that left it: `TestEveryAnswerThatReadsTheCatalogWaitsItsTurn` now holds the turn and asks each route that reads the catalog, `/v1/status`, `/v1/disks/{id}`, `/v1/trash` and `/v1/guards`, which must each wait, and `/v1/disks`, which must not. With the route taken out of the turn by hand, it fails.
- **F061, the daemon told by what a process says it runs**: open on `beta`. `tellDaemon` counted any process whose executable was Atoll's and whose arguments were `daemon`, so any account that may run the executable could start one: `disk add`, `disk remove`, `disk fail` and `disk replace` then said the daemon was told, with exit 0 and no `daemon-not-told`, when none ran, and `guard confirm` and `guard reject` dropped the line that says how long the answer holds. A process is now told only when it acts as an account Atoll takes as its own, the accounts of `ops.TrustedOwners` (§10): root, the one the command runs as, and the catalog's owner, which is the daemon's. Its effective account is read from `/proc/<pid>/status`. Tests first: `TestOnlyADaemonOfThePoolsAccountsIsTold` starts this test binary with the daemon's arguments and tells it only when its account is one of those given; `TestEffectiveUID` reads the `Uid` line.

Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22 ("Tout confirmer"):

- The accounts are `ops.TrustedOwners`'s, as for the work folders and the secret files, the account that runs the command among them: a process of that account deceives only that account's own command.
- A daemon that acts as an account owning no catalog, as one of an account that writes a catalog of root's through its group, is not told any more: the command says no daemon runs. The unit's `StateDirectory=atoll` gives `/var/lib/atoll` and the catalog to the daemon's account when it starts, so the catalog's owner is the daemon's there, as §10 says.
- What stays: the `atoll` account itself, the daemon's, can still start a process that is counted, as it can do worse; and on a machine with two pools of one account, a command of one counts the other's daemon too, as `--config` is not compared (TODO.md).

## Mutation gates by package and a floor of coverage, every mutant that lived in the pure core killed or named, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §27)

The maintainer's decision of 2026-11-21 ("Seuil par paquet"): a gate per package, 95 % efficacy and 85 % mutator coverage for each pure package; `names` and `integrity`, whose switches stay switches, at the coverage measured, never lowered; the mutants that change nothing named and left out; and the floor of the `cover` job at its measured level. Commits `789ac24`, `7e8c340`, `358672e` and the one that records this, on `worktree-agent-a8b70a62853584dea`, on `beta`'s `1be6613`.

- **What lived, measured again** on `1be6613` (gremlins v0.6.0, killed / lived / not covered / timed out, efficacy, mutator coverage): `glob` 127/22/6/15, 85.23 %, 96.13 %; `value` 220/17/31/8, 92.83 %, 88.43 %; `names` 150/9/32/9, 94.34 %, 83.25 %; `rules` 295/2/50/18, 99.33 %, 85.59 %; `config` 307/25/57/0, 92.47 %, 85.35 %; `integrity` 29/1/13/3, 96.67 %, 69.77 %; `solver` 843/64/140/4, 92.94 %, 86.63 %. 140 mutants lived, not the 123 of 2026-11-20.
- **A timeout hid some** (`test/ci/mutation.sh`): gremlins stops a mutant at three times its first run of the package's tests, a run that takes its build from go's cache, where each mutant builds its package and the test binary again. On a loaded machine, mutants the tests let live were counted timed out, in neither score: as the script stood, the same tree gave `glob` 90/15/6/59, `value` 219/17/31/9, `config` 306/24/57/2 and `solver` 806/36/140/69. The script now passes `--timeout-coefficient 20`, at which what times out is a loop the mutant made endless; the 92 timed out of `glob` on 2026-11-20 were not all such loops.
- **Killed by a test** (80), each checked by applying the mutant by hand to a copy of the tree, and no production code changed: `glob` 18, a Go test of each syntax error's offset and words and 4 cases in `glob.json` (nested braces at the start, a class that ends in `-`, a range of one character, too many expansions after a literal); `value` 13, 14 cases in `listen.json` (RFC 5952's runs of zero groups, an empty part, a group of five digits or not hex on either side of `::`) and a Go test of `Window.Next` in a zone made from TZif data whose clock jumps over the window's start two nights in a row; `config` 16, 6 cases in `config.json` (bare keys at the ends of ASCII, two unknown keys and two relative `path_map` keys each reported, an address with no authority, an empty user), 4 in `config_edit.json` (an empty file, one of a byte order mark alone, keys left out between keys written, a key named `disk` in another table), and Go tests of `ErrEditRefused`, of the words of checks no vector tells apart, and of a format on the wrong kind of value; `solver` 33, 12 cases in `plan.json`, 4 in `propagation.json`, 1 in `metadata.json` and 2 in `trash_purge.json`, and Go tests: no list of results for a plan with nothing to say, `percent`'s shares with no capacity or a room below zero, `TrashUsed`'s count of the bytes known.
- **Named equivalent** (60), in `test/ci/mutation-equivalents.txt`, each with the line of code it changes and why no input the code can be given tells it from the original: `config` 9, `glob` 4, `integrity` 1, `names` 9, `rules` 2, `solver` 31, `value` 4. The 12 of `names`, `integrity` and `rules` are those named on 2026-11-20, argued again (`regex.go:316:11` and `521:71`, found then with every mutant reached, are counted not covered by gremlins, so not listed). Most of the others are a boundary at a value that cannot occur (a `/` that cannot lead a checked path, a `]` right after a `[`, two positions that never repeat), an equal value set again, or a capacity hint.
- **After**, the gate run of every pure package (`WORKERS=10`, 1520 s, on a machine other work loaded): `glob` 149/4/6/11, 100 % with its 4 named left out, 96.23 %; `value` 234/4/31/7, 100 %, 88.48 %; `names` 150/9/32/9, 100 %, 83.25 %; `rules` 295/2/50/18, 100 %, 85.59 %; `config` 323/9/57/0, 100 %, 85.35 %; `integrity` 29/1/13/3, 100 %, 69.77 %; `solver` 877/31/139/4, 100 %, 86.72 %. No mutant lives that is not named.
- **The gates** (`test/ci/gates.txt`, one file for all of them): `mutation <package> <efficacy> <coverage>`, 95 and 85 for `config`, `glob`, `rules`, `solver` and `value`, 95 and 83.25 for `names`, 95 and 69.77 for `integrity`. `test/ci/mutation-gate.sh` reads gremlins' log of a package, leaves the named mutants out of the efficacy, and fails a package under either gate; it fails too when a named mutant is not in the log, or its line no longer reads as the list says, and says it of one gremlins counted killed, left out all the same. `test/ci/mutation.sh` runs it after each package: its summary has the named count and the gate, `lived.txt` only what is not named, and its exit code is 1 when a package fails its gate. The nightly `.github/workflows/mutation.yml` runs the script, so the gates; a package a dispatch names that has no gate is only measured.
- **The floor of coverage** (`test/ci/cover.sh`, `make cover`, the CI's `cover` job): `cover unit` and `cover merged` in `test/ci/gates.txt`, the totals as the table prints them, to a tenth; a total under its floor fails the job after the table, said in the log and the run's summary. Measured here, on `1be6613` with these tests: the unit tests 84.4 %, the scenarios 46.6 %, either 88.9 % of 30,181 statements, so the floors are 84.4 and 88.9. The 84.7 % and 89.3 % of 2026-11-20 counted 29,584 statements; the packages measured then read the same within half a point (`catalog` 94.1 %, `trash` 91.6 %, `watch` 89.6 %, `fsx` 89.0 %, `ops` 88.3 %, `jobs` 87.0 %, `cmd/atoll` 52.1 % and 86.8 %), and what came between is mostly the view's writer, 1495 statements at 73.2 %, which the loop-device tests run. A first run failed on its own: under the load of the machine `jobs` took past `go test`'s 10 minutes (807 s once raised), as `cover.sh` sets no timeout; the measure is that of a run with `GOFLAGS=-timeout=60m`.
- **`jobs`, `ops`, `trash` and `watch`**, measured on `1be6613` as the pure core was, with no gate (TODO.md): `ops` 636/124/659/0, 83.68 %, 53.56 %, in 3369 s with 6 workers: its own 19 tests reach half its mutants, the reprise of `recover.go` hardly at all (246 of its mutants not covered), which the tests of `jobs` and `trash` run; most of what lives turns the `err != nil` after a step into `err == nil`, so that the operation returns as soon as the step succeeds, a sync or what follows skipped, which the tests of `ops` alone do not look behind: in `disk.go` (39), `linkguard.go` (21), `view.go` (19) and `repair.go` (14). `jobs`, a dry run alone (`--dry-run`, which runs the tests once for their coverage and no mutant): 1895 mutants its tests reach and 220 they do not, 89.60 % mutator coverage; that one run of its tests took 28 min 18 s with one CPU on the loaded machine, past `go test`'s 10 minutes, which the dry run had to raise (`GOFLAGS=-timeout=90m`); each mutant that lives would cost as much, and one that times out 20 times that, so a run of them all would take days: gremlins, which runs every test of the package for each mutant, cannot measure `jobs`. `trash` in part, stopped after 30 minutes with 6 workers, its first run of the tests 1 min 58 s, so 39 minutes for a mutant that times out: of the 226 mutants of `conflicts.go`, `folders.go`, `linkguard.go`, `repair.go` and the start of `restoreto.go`, 175 killed, 30 lived and 21 not covered, 85.37 % and 90.71 % so far, `trash.go` not reached; among those that live, `conflicts.go:159:13`, `s.apply && summary.Stayed == 0` made `||`, would resolve a conflict in the catalog on a dry run, which no test of `trash` sees (the scenarios of `conflicts resolve --dry-run` may: gremlins does not run them). `watch` 944/198/294/5, 82.66 %, 79.53 %, in 3566 s with 6 workers, its first run of the tests 32 s: what lives is mostly in the comparison of a walk to the catalog, `scan.go` (53) and `verdict.go` (46), then `watcher.go` (28), `capacity.go` (17), `notify.go` (12) and `cache.go` (11); in `scan.go`, among others, each term of the check that the mount and the root did not change under the walk (`scan.go:357`), which only a disk swapped during a walk trips, as the loop-device tests do and gremlins does not; in `verdict.go`, the conditions that set a row of the catalog against what the walk found (hard links, a row without its mode or owner).
- **Verified**, in the CI's image, the machine loaded by other work the whole time: `gofmt`, `go vet ./...`, and with `-tags conformance` and `-tags loopdev`; `go test ./...` (`-timeout 60m`, `jobs` 815 s); `make conformance`, 645 scenarios; `golangci-lint run ./...`, with `--build-tags conformance` and with `--build-tags loopdev`, 0 issues each; each of the 80 mutants killed applied by hand to a copy of the tree, each failing a test; `bash test/ci/mutation.sh` on the whole pure core (`WORKERS=10`), exit 0, every package meeting its gate; the gate tried on logs edited by hand: it says so of a named mutant counted killed, and fails on one not in the log and on a line of code changed; `make cover` failing under floors of 84.7 and 89.3, then, run again, the same totals passing at 84.4 and 88.9.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Confirmer"):
  - the timeout coefficient 20, not gremlins' 3, in the script and so each night: the pure core took 1520 s with 10 workers here, `solver` 764 s of it;
  - a named mutant is left out of the efficacy alone; the mutator coverage stays gremlins' own, a named mutant counted as reached, as the tests reach it, so that the gates of `names` and `integrity` are the figures gremlins prints, 83.25 % and 69.77 %; left out of both, they would be 82.42 % and 69.05 %;
  - each entry of the list carries its line of code, which the gate compares with the file, so that a line moved or changed fails the gate until its mutant is argued again; an entry gremlins does not report fails it too; one it counts killed is said and left out all the same, as a kill may come from a loaded machine (2026-11-20);
  - each figure compared as it is printed: gremlins' to two decimals, the coverage's to a tenth;
  - the floor of coverage at what this tree measures, 84.4 % and 88.9 %, not the 84.7 % and 89.3 % of 2026-11-20, which it does not reach since the view's writer came; the CI's run may read a tenth apart from this machine's, and fails at once if it reads under: its first run is to read; `cover.sh` keeps going without a timeout of its own, as before;
  - one file of gates for the mutation tests and the coverage;
  - a function's own result pinned rather than its mutant named where only its callers could not tell them apart: a plan with nothing to say holds no list of results (nil), and `TrashUsed` has no key for a disk whose trash holds no byte;
  - the words of errors and warnings pinned in Go tests, the vectors keeping to keys, as on 2026-11-20; a few vectors on inputs a caller would rarely give, each one the function's documents allow: the disks listed out of their positions' order, two pending copies on cache disks for one resident file, a key named `disk` in another table, an empty file to edit;
  - `Window.Next`'s three days pinned by a Go test in a zone of no real place, which no vector can name;
  - `jobs`, `ops`, `trash` and `watch` measured and left without a gate (above);
  - a lesson in LESSONS.md: a mutant timed out may be one that lives.

## `$LogFile` read too: a volume Windows left in use readonly, `hibernated`, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §10, §22, §30)

The maintainer's decision of 2026-11-21 ("Lire $LogFile aussi"): a data volume Windows had mounted when Fast Startup ran holds no `hiberfil.sys`, so once the mount is confirmed the check reads its journal, `$LogFile`, as it reads `hiberfil.sys`, and a journal left unclean makes the disk `readonly`, `hibernated`. No new reason, key or field: format version 1 kept. Commits `32a3321` and `98f519e` on `worktree-agent-af2dd17a475f217f1`, on `beta`'s `1be6613`, and this record. The sources and the measures are in `docs/research/ntfs-logfile.md`.

- **What is read** (§10, `internal/disks/logfile.go`): `$LogFile` at the top of an NTFS disk, after `hiberfil.sys` and only once it leaves the disk online, through a handle whose mount is the one the check found, by its exact name, following no link, with `O_NOATIME` (`fsx.ReadStart`): its size and its first 128 KiB, enough for two restart pages of a system page of 64 KiB. What runs for long reads it once per mount, as `hiberfil.sys`, in a cache of its own (the cache made generic, `onceAMount`). `EIO` and the other errors of a disk lost make it `missing`, `io-error`; any other error, a `$LogFile` absent or that is no regular file make it `readonly`, `hibernated`.
- **What it says** (`JournalOf`, `conformance/vectors/logfile.json`, 45 cases, their bytes in the README): ntfs-3g's rules, the strictest of the four drivers and the one Windows users meet (`libntfs-3g/logfile.c`, `volume.c`). Restart pages looked for at 0 and each power of two from 512, each header, restart area and client array checked, the update sequence applied, the newer by LSN counting. `empty` (all 0xFF, as `mkntfs` and every driver of Linux leave it) and `clean` (no client in use, or `RESTART_VOLUME_IS_CLEAN`) are `online`; `unclean` (a client in use, the flag clear), `version-2` (which ntfs-3g refuses read-write, "Metadata kept in Windows cache") and `invalid` (no restart page that holds, the newer torn, a page past what was read, a file under 200 KiB, a read cut short) are `hibernated`, each with a message of its own.
- **Measured in qemu, Linux 6.12 and 7.3.5** (`TestAnUncleanLogFileOnEachDriver`, restart pages of 4 KiB written on the device of a volume `mkntfs` made before each mount, no log record after them, Windows not run; each driver mounted it read-write as §22 recommends, read-only when it refused; the type `ntfs` on 7.3.5 alone):
  - every driver answers `$LogFile` by its name at the top, a regular file whose bytes are the device's; the check moved no access time of it and wrote nothing to the journal on any;
  - **ntfs3** refused read-write every journal with a client in use, clean or not, of version 1.1 or 2.0, and the invalid one ("failed to replay log file. Can't mount rw!", its replay finding no log record), mounted each read-only when asked and left it: `hibernated`, `read-only-mount` for the clean one; one with no client in use it mounted read-write and emptied: `online`;
  - **the type `ntfs`**, `errors=remount-ro` or `errors=continue`: version 1.1, clean, in use or not, emptied at a read-write mount (`online`); version 2.0 and the invalid one, superblock `ro` ("LogFile version 2.0 is not supported"), left: `hibernated`, `errors-continue` first under `errors=continue`;
  - **ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g**: version 2.0, clean or not, read-only ("Metadata kept in Windows cache, refused to mount"), left: `hibernated`; version 1.1 in use, and the invalid one, emptied at a read-write mount ("The file system wasn't safely closed on Windows. Fixing.", their default `recover`): `online`; with `norecover`, those mounted read-only and left: `hibernated`; clean ones read-write and left: `online`;
  - so no driver kept a read-write mount over a journal left in use, or one that proves nothing, without emptying it: what the check adds is `hibernated` for a read-only mount, where it said `read-only-mount`, and the same on a driver that would mount it read-write as it is. A journal a driver empties is lost before Atoll looks, which §22 and `deploy/README.md` now say.
- **Tests that failed first**: 23 new cases in `disk_check.json`, 12 of which fail with the check not reading `$LogFile` (an unclean, version 2.0 or invalid journal, one absent, not a file, unreadable or giving `EIO`, `hibernated` before `read-only-mount` and `ignore-case`); the scenario `disk-list-logfile` fails likewise (pattern bytes at `$LogFile`, a folder and a symbolic link there `hibernated`; none, `$LOGFILE`, or ext4, `online`); the 45 cases of `logfile.json` had no reader. Then `TestJournalVectors`, `TestJournalVectorsCoverEveryJournal`, `TestJournalReadsNoFurther`, `FuzzJournalOf`, `TestReadLogFile` (absent, each journal, a folder, a symbolic link not followed, a FIFO, `EIO`, `ENOTCONN`, `ENODEV`, `ENXIO`, `EACCES`, `EMFILE`, the access time kept), `TestJudgeLogFile`, `TestLogFileIsReadOncePerMount`, `TestTheMachineReadsLogFileOncePerMount`, `TestCheckStopsAtTheFirstFailure` (`$LogFile` after `hiberfil.sys`, never after a hibernation or an error), three cases of `TestDiskAddRefusesNameRules`; in `test/loopdev`, `TestAnUncleanLogFileOnEachDriver` and `TestALogFileIsReadOncePerMount`.
- **Spec and records**: §10 (the reason `hibernated`, the files a check reads, what `$LogFile` says), §22 (a volume Windows left in use, what each driver does), §30, `deploy/README.md`; the §10 fingerprint, `Replication.tla` unchanged, a disk being up or gone there; `docs/research/ntfs-logfile.md` (the sources: ntfs-3g's `logfile.c`, `volume.c`, `inode.c`, `ntfs-3g.c` and `lowntfs-3g.c`, Linux's `fs/ntfs3/fslog.c`, `fsntfs.c`, `super.c` and `fs/ntfs/logfile.c`, `super.c`).
- **Verified**, in the CI's image: `go vet` (default, conformance and loopdev tags), `gofmt`; `go test ./...`, `internal/jobs` with a longer timeout, as it took 1819 s on a machine shared with other runs; the race detector on disks and daemon; `make conformance`, 646 scenarios; lint (default, conformance, loopdev), 0 issues; `make model-check`, the ten instances; `TestAnUncleanLogFileOnEachDriver` and `TestALogFileIsReadOncePerMount` in qemu on Linux 6.12 and 7.3.5. The whole of `test/loopdev` ran too, on a machine shared with other runs: on 6.12, 340 tests and subtests passed and none failed before the guest's timeout of 50 minutes stopped it at `TestADeviceThatFailsDuringACopy`, and its run under systemd passed (5 tests); on 7.3.5 it was stopped after 183 had passed, up to `TestARepriseOfAModeOnEachDriver`, `TestAHibernatedVolumeOnEachDriver`, `TestAHibernatedVolumeIsReadOncePerMount` and `TestWhatClearsAHibernation` among them. There `TestALogFileIsReadOncePerMount` failed once, a device read during a check of the same mount: the readahead that the first read of `$LogFile`'s 128 KiB sets going had ended after it. The test now waits for the device to be quiet before it counts (`98f519e`), and passed 5 times in a row on both kernels, beside `TestAHibernatedVolumeIsReadOncePerMount`. The rest of `test/loopdev` past those points was not run with this change.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Confirmer"):
  - read through the mount by name, as `hiberfil.sys`, not from the device: every driver answers `$LogFile` at the top as a regular file whose bytes are the device's (measured), so no second kind of read;
  - ntfs-3g's rules: a journal with a client in use and `RESTART_VOLUME_IS_CLEAN` set is clean, where ntfs3 reads only whether a client is in use; a clean journal of version 2.0 is `hibernated`, as ntfs-3g refuses it read-write;
  - an invalid journal is no proof of a clean one, so `hibernated`, with a message that says it cannot be told; stricter than ntfs-3g where it costs nothing: the newer restart page torn, an update sequence array at an odd offset taken for torn, lengths counted without wrapping;
  - 128 KiB read: a restart page past them is `invalid`, and a journal empty in its first 128 KiB is taken for empty, where ntfs-3g looks further;
  - `$LogFile` absent, or no regular file, is `hibernated`: every NTFS volume holds it and every driver answers it (measured), so its absence tells nothing;
  - `hiberfil.sys` first, then `$LogFile`, read only once `hiberfil.sys` leaves the disk online; the same reason `hibernated`, its messages of its own, and `disk add`'s warning that waits for Windows, unchanged;
  - the conformance build reads a folder with no `$LogFile` as an empty journal, so that a folder stands for a volume a driver of Linux mounted; the scenario uses the runner's pattern bytes, with no runner change for bytes that are not UTF-8;
  - §22 and `deploy/README.md` recommend `norecover` to ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g on a disk Windows also mounts, so that they mount a journal left in use read-only rather than empty it (measured), which the check then reads `hibernated`;
  - nothing changes for the remount after a USB reset, which reads no journal before it mounts: the check after it does.

## A copy known by its inode, its size and its modification time once it moved, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §15.12, §17.1, §17.2, §21.2)

The maintainer's decision of 2026-11-21 ("Inode + taille + mtime"): the look after a move and the table of §17.2 knew a file by its inode alone, so that a file made at a copy's name just before its move to the trash, with the inode number of the copy just removed, as ext4 gives it to the next file made, went to the trash in its place (seen once in the fault sweeps of 2026-11-20, below). Chosen: the inode, the size and the modification time, as the look of the link path compares them; a file that does not match is not the copy, and stays where it is, for the next pass. Commit on `worktree-agent-a560a2654b0b4c787`, on `beta`'s `1be6613`.

- **The look after a move to the trash** (§17.1, step 4, `ops.ToTrash`): the name in the trash must hold the copy by its inode, its size and its modification time, as the look of the link path compares them (`linked`), never by its inode alone. A file that took the copy's name before the move with its inode number, or the copy edited then, is `ErrGone`, resolved at once by the table of §17.2, which puts it back at its path, the operation `undone` with an `op-undone` event, the outcome `changed`. Before, the move was done, and the user's file went to the trash as the copy, purged at the end of its retention.
- **What a reprise decides** (§17.2, `ops.ResolveTrash`, `recoverTrash`, `trashChanged`): a name in the trash that alone holds the copy's inode is `same` only with the size and the modification time the copy's row says, and `other` with others: back at the logical path when that is free, `undone` with an event; left in the trash, `undone` with an event, when the path is taken. Before, both finished, the file in the trash as the copy. Both names leading to that inode are one file, as before: edited in place, the name in the trash goes. With no row of that inode, the trash's name alone keeps its inode to go by, as for a link of the guard.
- **Renames and exchanges** (§15.12, step 4 and the cycle, `ops.RenameFile`, `ExchangeFiles`): the look after the move knew the file at its new name by its inode; now by the identity of §15.1, as their link path and their reprise (`seenRenamed`) already did, so that a move that ends and one a crash stops are judged alike.
- **The move of Atoll's own view** (§21.2, `ops.ResolveTrashNow`, `writer.leave`, `repriseMoveCreate`, `trashLeft`): no row records the copy a move leaves on a full disk, so its move to the trash that fails, the look after it included, is resolved by the table of §17.2 against what the move copied, or the reprise's look right before the move. A file that took the copy's name with its inode number went to the trash and was purged at once, by its inode alone; it now goes back to its path, the move `done`, for a pass to find both, as a copy its trash refuses does.
- **The model** (`conformance/models/Replication.tla`, `README.md`): `TrashLook` knows the trash's name alone by its object and its change count, as Atoll's looks know it by inode, size and modification time; `TrashUnlink`, finding the source's name gone by itself and its link no longer what the copy's row says, goes to `TrashResolve` then `TrashResolved`, the table of §17.2 at once, in two steps; `RepTrash` shares them. Every file a user makes in the model is a new object, so it never meets an inode number taken again: it checks what the rule decides of the copy itself edited through the name it shared with its link, then that name deleted. That takes three user actions, one more than `Link.cfg` allows, whose states are the same with the rule or without it; a new instance, `LinkEdited.cfg`, two disks without `RENAME_NOREPLACE`, writes and deletions, reaches it: 249,072 distinct states, 248,712 with the rule left out, both passing, as no invariant tells a user's version in the trash from one in the library. The fingerprints of §15.12, §17.1 and §17.2 follow; `states.json` is unchanged, as no copy state changes.
- **Tests that failed first**: two vectors of `op_recovery.json`, `same`/`absent` and `same`/`other` with `changed` (`TestRecoveryVectors`); `ops.TestTheLookAfterAMoveKnowsMoreThanTheInode`, a fault hook that writes the copy in place at the move, the same inode with another size, another modification time, or both: to the trash by a rename, by a link with the source written then removed, a file renamed, two files exchanged, 12 cases, and a change of the change time alone, still the copy; `trash.TestAFileWithTheCopysInodeGoesBackFromTheTrash`, a prune by a rename and by a link (4 cases), and `TestARepriseKnowsTheCopyByMoreThanItsInode`, a disk lost right after the move, the file in the trash then written in place: back at its path, or left in the trash when the path is taken (4 cases), and its change time alone, done; `writer.TestACopyLeftWithItsInodeAndNotItsSizeIsNeverPurged`, the view's move with the copy left written in place at its move to the trash, purged before; and four scenarios, `prune-crash-moved-same-inode-size.json`, `-time.json`, `-taken.json` and `prune-crash-linked-edited-then-deleted.json`, each failing on the base.
- **Verified**, in the CI's image, on a machine other runs shared: `gofmt`, `go vet ./...`; `go test ./...`, `jobs` with `-timeout 45m` (1,760 s under that load, past the default 10 minutes); the race detector on `ops`, `trash`, `catalog`, `watch`, `view/writer` and `jobs` (2,478 s); `make conformance`, 649 scenarios; `golangci-lint run ./...`, with `--build-tags conformance` and with `--build-tags loopdev`, 0 issues each; `make model-check`, its 10 instances passing with the states they had before, then `LinkEdited.cfg`; and in qemu, on Linux 6.12.117 and 7.3.5, each passing, the loop-device tests of renames, the trash, the reprise, the link guard, a refresh, the cache and the view's move: `TestRenamesOnEachDriver`, `TestRenamesFoundOnEachDriver`, `TestASwapOnEachDriver`, `TestPruneOnEachDriver`, `TestPruneOfARefusedName`, `TestDeletionsOnEachDriver`, `TestATrashOnAnotherMount`, `TestRestoreStaysOnItsDisk`, `TestPruneByHandlesPastEveryLimit`, `TestADeviceThatFailsDuringAnOperation`, `-ARename`, `-AFolderRename` and `-ARestore`, `TestDeviceFailures`, `TestTheLinkGuardThroughMergerfs`, `TestTheLinkGuardOnTheTypeNTFS`, `TestViewMovesAFileThatRunsOutOfSpace`, `TestAPassRefreshesOnEachDriver` and `TestTheCacheOnRealDisks`.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Confirmer"):
  - a rename and an exchange compare the identity of §15.1 after their move, the inode with the creation time where both have one and with the size and the modification time otherwise, as their link path and their reprise do, rather than the inode, the size and the modification time: "as the link path does", for each move;
  - what stays by its inode alone: an entry of the trash, in a restore, a purge and their reprises, as the `trash` table records its size and no modification time (a column would be format version 2), and nothing but Atoll writes in its trash; a link of the guard and a part in `tmp/`, Atoll's own names; the view's moves for a user (`RenameUser`, `LinkUser`, `PlaceUserPart`), which its caller looked at right before; the name a reprise removes of two that lead to one inode, and the reprise's second look once the source's name went, as no other file can have that inode while a name holds it; and the copy a move of the view left, when its reprise finds it in the trash already. TODO.md says so;
  - with no row of the copy's inode, both names leading to it still read as edited (the name in the trash goes, the copy stays live), as before; the trash's name alone keeps its inode, as the move of the view needs;
  - a file in the trash that is not the copy, its path taken since, stays in the trash, `undone` with an event and no entry, as the table says of `other`/`other`: never listed, never purged, kept;
  - an edit in place that the look after the move finds has an `op-undone` event, as it cannot be told from a file that took the inode number;
  - the copy edited through the library's name, then that name deleted, between a `link` and the look at the source, goes back to its path: the user's deletion undone, the edit kept, for the next pass to read as a modification;
  - the view's move compares the copy it left with what it copied, through a new `ops.ResolveTrashNow`, and the table then puts back a file that took its name, both kept for a pass, rather than that file kept unpurged in the trash;
  - times compared exactly, as the row and the look are of one disk, at its own precision (invariant 9).

## A copy's folder made like the one on another disk, or waiting for the disk away that holds it, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §14.2, §17.4)

The maintainer's decision of 2026-11-21 ("Chercher, sinon attendre"): a copy looked for the model of a folder it makes on the source's disk alone. When the source sat behind at its old path and the disk that holds the real folder was away, it made the folder with no model, 0755 since 2026-11-20 (below), so that a folder kept 0700 there was more open on the copy's disk. Now it looks for the model on the other disks present first; when none holds the folder and the catalog says a disk away does, it waits (`io-error`), as §15.12's step 3 does; 0755 only when no disk holds it. Commit `b36a01b` on `worktree-agent-a33f87fb016085a99`, on `beta`'s `1be6613`.

- **The look** (`internal/jobs/replicate.go`, `folderModel`): the same folder on the source's disk; when that disk holds none, on the first other disk present by position whose copy of the file is at its path, then on any other disk present by position, the target aside. A disk present is one the run can read (`online`, `standby`, `readonly`, `draining`), counted in use for the look, so that a copy beside this one that loses it does not close its handle under the look. None found, the catalog's entries of that folder (`catalog.FolderDisks`) say which disks hold it: one away, a disk of the configuration not `failed` that the run cannot read, `missing` or lost since the run began, makes the copy wait; with none, the folder is made 0755 as before. A look at another disk that fails, or a disk that cannot be reached as it is opened, makes it wait too, that disk lost when the error says it cannot be relied on, never the target (invariant 4).
- **Where it waits**: first before anything is copied or journaled, right after the look at the source, as `io-error`, the job queued again one attempt more, so that a disk away for days costs a look at each run, never a whole copy read again at each pass; then at the rename, under the lock of the target's names, as before, every parent's model found before any parent is made: a wait there undoes the operation and keeps the `.part` for the job to resume, as a source that cannot be read does. The pipeline's order (invariant 7) and the per-path lock are unchanged. Every copy goes through it: `atoll replicate`, the daemon's copies, balance, the cache's transfer, evacuation and `disk replace`. Not in scope, and unchanged: a pass's renames and folder jobs (§15.12, step 3, which already wait), `trash restore` and `--to` (their model is the folder in the trash), Atoll's own view and its move for room (it always has one).
- **Spec**: §14.2 (the look before anything is journaled, the clause of each missing parent, and a bullet for the model) and §17.4 (a folder stands for none only when no disk holds it); the fingerprints of §14 and §14.2. `Replication.tla` unchanged: it leaves folders out, and a wait at the rename has the effect of `CopySourceLost`, the operation undone and the part kept, which the model takes at `keyed` and `copied` only, as it already left out a source that fails at the rename.
- **Tests that failed first**, in `internal/jobs/model_test.go`: `TestACopysParentIsMadeLikeTheFolderOnAnotherDisk`; `TestACopyWaitsForTheDiskAwayThatHoldsItsParent`; `TestACopyWaitsWhenALookAtAnotherDiskFails` (`EIO` and `ENODEV`, the disk lost; `EACCES`, kept); `TestACopyWaitsWhenALookAtAnotherDiskFailsLate` (a look that fails once the part is verified: the part kept, the operation undone, the other disk lost, not the target); `TestACopysModelIsTheFolderTheFileSitsIn`; `TestACopyFromACopyBehindTakesTheModelOfTheFolderItWasRenamedInto`, the decision's own case end to end. Two pass before and after, and pin what does not wait: `TestACopyDoesNotWaitForADiskPresentWithoutTheFolder` and `TestACopyDoesNotWaitForAFailedOrRetiredDisk`. Scenarios: `replicate-parent-model-away-waits` and `daemon-parent-model-away-waits`, both failing on the code before. `daemon-parent-no-model-umask` and `daemon-crash-parent-no-model-umask` (below) made the folder 0755 with the disk that held it away, which now waits: they became `replicate-parent-no-model-umask` and `replicate-crash-parent-no-model-umask`, where the disk the catalog says holds the folder is there and no longer holds it, no walk run since, so that no disk holds it and it is still made 0755 whatever the umask, and given it at the reprise.
- **Verified**, in the CI's image: `gofmt`; `go vet ./...` and `go test ./...`; the race detector on `jobs` (2337 s), `ops`, `catalog`, `fsx` and `watch`, `-timeout 45m`; `make conformance`, 647 scenarios, the two new ones failing against a build of the code before; `golangci-lint run ./...`, with `--build-tags conformance` and with `--build-tags loopdev`, 0 issues each; `make model-check`, its 10 instances passing. In qemu, on Linux 6.12.117 and 7.3.5, the loop-device tests of copies and of folders made with no model: `TestAFolderWithNoModelOnEachDriver`, `TestReplicateOnEachDriver`, `TestAPassCopiesOnEachDriver`, `TestADeviceThatFailsDuringACopy`, `TestCopiesAtOnceOnEachDriver`, `TestATargetFailingWithCopiesAtOnce`, `TestBreakGlass`, `TestBreakGlassWithADiskAway`, `TestTheCacheOnRealDisks`, `TestAFailedMountIsAMissingDisk` and `TestImmediateCopiesOnClose`, all passing. None of them puts the model on a third disk, which only the unit tests and the scenarios do: the look is a `stat` through a confirmed handle, as on the source.
- **Found**: §15.12's step 3 counts a `failed` disk, which no pass walks, as a disk not looked at that may hold the folder: with one in the configuration, a pass's rename whose new parent no disk present holds waits for good (seen with a test not kept). A decision for the maintainer (TODO.md).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Confirmer"):
  - the order of the disks after the source's: those whose copy of the file is at its path first, by position, then the others by position, as §15.12's step 3 prefers the folder the file sits in;
  - a `failed` disk and a `retired` one are not away: no copy waits for them, and the folder they alone hold is made 0755, as neither comes back of itself and waiting would leave the file a copy short for good; a pass's rename does wait for a failed disk (above);
  - what the catalog says is its `entries` of the folder, not the copies below it, which a walk records with their folders;
  - a disk present that the catalog says holds the folder, and that a look finds without it, does not hold it: no wait, 0755;
  - a look at another disk that fails for a reason that is not the disk's, `EACCES`, makes the copy wait too, the disk kept in service, as §15.12 does; and a disk present that cannot be reached as it is opened is waited for, whatever the catalog says;
  - the look before anything is journaled, which the decision did not ask for, so that a copy that is to wait copies nothing: it looks at the source's folder once more for each missing parent, and may open, and wake, a disk in standby to look at the folder there, only when the source's disk holds none;
  - every parent's model found before any is made, so that a wait for a deeper one makes none of those above it;
  - a wait counts an attempt of the job, as a source that cannot be read does, with a warning in the log at each run;
  - the two scenarios renamed from `daemon-` to `replicate-`, as their copy now runs through `atoll replicate`, which walks nothing, so that what the catalog says of the folder stays stale.

## What an earlier rebuild left kept under a name that holds the time, and no name a rebuild gives ever replaced, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §9, §25)

The maintainer's answer of 2026-11-21 ("Garder sous un autre nom"): a rebuild's start removed `<catalog>.rebuild` and its log as what a build stopped before its end left, while after a rename that failed past the swap, or a crash between the exchange and the rename, that name holds the old catalog.

- **Kept, never removed**: a rebuild now moves what it finds at `<catalog>.rebuild`, `-wal` and `-shm` to `<catalog>.rebuild-<UTC time>` beside the catalog, the log first, never replacing a name; standard error says where, the catalog first. A rename that fails puts back what went, and the rebuild exits with 1 having changed nothing (`catalog.KeepLeftover`, `fsx.RenameFileNoReplace`: `renameat2` with `RENAME_NOREPLACE`, or a link then an unlink where the filesystem lacks it).
- **Another rebuild's catalog left alone**: with no catalog yet, nothing held a lock before that removal, so a second `atoll catalog rebuild --yes` would have removed the first one's catalog as it was made. A catalog at `<catalog>.rebuild` whose lock is held is now that rebuild's: nothing is moved, and the command exits with 1.
- **Found on the way, fixed**: `catalog.SetAside` renamed the old catalog and its log to `<catalog>.old-<UTC time>` replacing what held that name, so a second rebuild in the same second, or with the clock set back, removed the first one's old catalog. Every rename of a set-aside now never replaces: a log's name taken leaves everything as it was; the catalog's, once the swap went through, leaves the old one at `<catalog>.rebuild`, which the message says, and which a rebuild run again keeps.
- **Tests first**: `TestKeepLeftover`, `TestSetAsideNeverReplaces` (it failed, the earlier catalog replaced), `TestRenameFileNoReplace`, `TestKeptMessage` and the changed `TestARebuildSaysWhereEachCatalogIs`; and the scenario `catalog-rebuild-leftover`, which failed on the build before (the leftover gone at step 5). The runner gained two steps for it, `write_system` and `expect_system`, a file of the sandbox that is no disk's, as no step could put a file beside the catalog; `TestSystemFiles` checks them.
- **The model**: §9 is pinned by `conformance/models/fingerprints.json`; `Replication.tla` models no rebuild, so it is unchanged, and §9's fingerprint is updated.

Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22 ("Tout confirmer"):

- The name: `<catalog>.rebuild-<UTC time>`, its log and shared memory with `-wal` and `-shm` after it, as `<catalog>.old-<UTC time>`'s are. What is kept is not told apart: a catalog not finished and an old catalog get the same name, and the message says it may be either.
