- A name taken where the leftover would go (two rebuilds in one second) stops the rebuild with exit 1, nothing moved: neither removed nor refused for good, as running it again a second later works.
- With exactly one old catalog's log left at `<catalog>.old-<time>-wal` and no catalog there, the leftover is likely that catalog, but it is not renamed to that name: the message of the failed rebuild named both, and joining them stays the user's rename.
- `--json` gains no field for what was kept: only standard error says it, as the answer asked, and no schema changes.

## A new file whose write runs out of space moved to another disk, with `view.move_on_enospc`, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §6.1, §9, §17, §19, §21.2, §24; invariant 2)

The maintainer's decisions of 2026-11-21: opt-in, a new key `view.move_on_enospc`, `false` by default, which changes nothing while off; only a new file, one the view made and that is still open through the handle that made it, never one opened again, never one at whose path the catalog holds a file; once the file is copied and verified on the other disk, the copy left on the full disk goes to that disk's trash and is purged at once, as the SSD cache's transfer does, a second exception to invariant 2 and to §6.1's removal rules, which CLAUDE.md and §6.1 now say. Commit `7a4e2ef` on `worktree-agent-a870f152e8d217794`, on `beta`'s `ffaf060`.

- **The move** (§21.2, `internal/view/writer/move.go`, `file.go`): a write through `File.WriteAt` that the file's disk answers with `ENOSPC` or `EDQUOT` drops the file's parts written in parallel, as any failed write does, then moves it, the program's writes held back meanwhile by a lock of the file, which every call on its handle takes shared and the move alone. The target is chosen as a new file's disk is (§12.4), the full disk left out, a cache disk never, among the disks whose room past `min_free` exceeds the file's size plus the write, and the caller must be allowed to make the name there. Then, in order: the move and the `create` of its part journaled; the copy into `<workdir>/tmp/<op id>.part`, each chunk of 4 MiB hashed, the full disk's copy looked at again; the modification time, owner, mode and `user.` attributes; `fsync`; the re-read without cache against the hashes; under the writer's lock, the checks made again and the part renamed without replacing to the file's path (`ops.PlaceUserPart`), after the folders the disk lacks, made as the view presents them; the `trash` journaled; the part's own handle made the file's; the copy left on the full disk to its trash, then purged; the move `done`; and the write made there, whose answer is the program's. A file from a full SSD cache goes to a data disk (§19).
- **The journal** (§9): `view_ops.kind = move`, a new column `disk2_id`, the disk the file leaves, by `schema/migrations/0023_view_move.sql`, which makes the table again as 0020 and 0022 did, rows, ids and counter kept; `op_id` names the one operation the move runs now, the `create`, then the `trash`, then the `unlink`, pointed at in the transaction that journals each (`catalog.BeginViewMove`, `ViewMoveTrash`, `ViewMovePurge`, `EndViewMove`), so that no command's or daemon's reprise ever resolves it. The frozen contract test passes: a kind and a column added, nothing taken away.
- **What the view presents** (`view.Resolve`, `ListOf`, vectors in `view_resolve.json`): the full disk's copy while the operation the move names is its `create`, and the target's from then on, its copy at its name by then. A pass looks at a move's name on both disks, a deletion on neither (`watch.ViewNames`, `view_names.json`), waits at it on both while it is planned, and takes it in as any other operation of the view. What a move sends to the trash is Atoll's own: `catalog.IsViewDeletion`, which says so of the view's deletions and replacements alone, replaces `IsViewOp` in `ops.OwnFiles`, and `catalog.PlacesOf` keeps the move's operations.
- **A trash with no room, the copy removed at its path** (the maintainer, 2026-11-21: "Purge sur place"): run in qemu, `TestViewMovesAFileThatRunsOutOfSpace` failed on Linux 6.12 and passed on 7.3.5. On 6.12 the small disk had no block left once the program's write ran out of space, so the trash could not make `Atoll/`, the copy left stayed at its path beside the moved one, an older and cut-short version, the move done with its `trash` undone and the full disk still full; on 7.3.5 a few blocks were free, by chance. Now, only when the trash fails for want of room, its folders or its rename answering `ENOSPC` or `EDQUOT`, and a look finds nothing moved, the copy left is removed at its path: the `trash` undone and an `unlink` with no place in the trash journaled with its `purge` event, in one transaction (`catalog.ViewMoveRemoveHere`); then `ops.RemoveLeft`, the trash's checks (inode, size, modification time, change time against what was copied, looked at right before), the unlink, the folder's time put back, its sync; the move done, the copy's row, if a pass recorded one, gone with it. A copy that changed meanwhile, or is gone, stays: the `unlink` undone with an `op-undone` event. §6.1 (item 10 and the removal rules), §9 (that `unlink`), §17, §21.2 (step 8, two rows of the table, the reprise) and CLAUDE.md invariant 2 say so, approved by the maintainer; the fingerprints of §6.1 and §9 again, `Replication.tla` unchanged.
- **The warning** (the maintainer, 2026-11-21: "Oui, avertir"): `view.move_on_enospc = true` with `view.engine = "mergerfs"` gets a warning of no later phase, at `view.move_on_enospc`, the configuration still valid (§24.4; 4 vectors in `config.json`, two failing first).
- **The view's reprise** (`internal/view/writer/reprise.go`), by the operation the move names: its `create` planned with the part at the file's path, by inode, the rename went through: the part's name in `tmp/` goes, and the full disk's copy goes to its trash, unpurged, when it has the target's size and modification time; with others, both stay, the `create` done and the move undone, the full disk's presented. No part there: the part goes, the move is undone. Its `trash` planned: the copy still at its path goes to the trash all the same, unpurged, then the table of §17.2; its `unlink` planned, as a purge's reprise, and one at the copy's path likewise (`ops.LeftAt`): nothing there once the folder is synced, done; the copy there, undone, and it stays; another file, undone with an `op-undone` event. A trash with no room in a reprise leaves the copy at its path and the move planned, with a warning, for the next start: a reprise never removes it at its path. A disk it needs and cannot write to leaves it planned.
- **Tests that failed first**: the 10 new cases of `view_resolve.json` and the 2 of `view_names.json` (`TestResolveVectors`, `TestViewNamesVectors`, failing until `Resolve`, `ListOf` and `ViewNames` knew a move); 4 cases of `config.json`, and the key in the list of every default. Then, each failing with the move turned off in `File.WriteAt` but the one that checks what never moves: `TestANewFileMovesWhenItsDiskRunsOutOfSpace` (`ENOSPC` and `EDQUOT`), `TestAMoveThatFailsLeavesTheFileAsItWas` (the target filling during the copy, a part that does not read back, the target lost, then the reprise), `TestTheParallelPartsGoBeforeAMove`, `TestAFileOnAFullCacheMovesToADataDisk`, `TestAMoveStoppedAtEachStep` (kill -9 at each of the 17 crash points a move passes, then the reprise: one presented copy holding every byte written, the other in the trash or purged, nothing planned), `TestAMoveWhoseLeftCopyChangedKeepsBoth`; `TestWhatNeverMovesForRoom` (the key off, a file opened again, a path the catalog holds, no disk with room, another name, a write with no place, a cache as the only other disk). Catalog: `TestAViewMoveRunsOneOperationAtATime`, `TestAViewMoveUndone`, `TestTheMoveMigrationKeepsTheViewJournal`, and the four new writers in `TestEveryWriterFailsWhole`. Jobs: `TestAPassTakesInAFileTheViewMovedForRoom`. Loop devices: `TestViewMovesAFileThatRunsOutOfSpace`, 40 MiB written through the mounted view past a 24 MiB ext4 disk, which failed on Linux 6.12 in qemu (above). For the trash with no room, each failing with the removal at the path turned off: `TestACopyLeftWithNoRoomForItsTrashIsRemovedAtItsPath` (`ENOSPC` and `EDQUOT` on the trash's folders and on its rename), `TestACopyLeftThatChangedIsNotRemovedAtItsPath`, `TestAReprisesTrashWithNoRoomLeavesTheMovePlanned` (stopped at `move-placed` and at `move-swapped`, a reprise with no room, then one with room), `TestARemovalAtItsPathStoppedAtEachStep` (kill -9 at `move-unlink-journaled`, `unlinked` and `source-synced`, then the reprise); in the catalog, `TestAViewMoveRemovesTheCopyLeftAtItsPath`, and `ViewMoveRemoveHere` in `TestEveryWriterFailsWhole`. The loop-device test now runs on two small disks, so that what it finds no longer depends on the last free blocks: one whose inodes are all taken but the file's, where no folder of the trash can be made and the copy is removed at its path; one made with `inline_data`, whose new folders keep their names in their inode and need no block, where the trash works and the copy is purged from it.
- **Spec and records**: §21.2 says the move, its steps, what it presents, what a failure leaves (a table) and its reprise, in place of "Not in phase 2a"; §19, §6.1 (item 10 and the removal rules), §9 (the kind and the column), §17 (what goes in as `surplus`), §24.1 and `deploy/atoll.toml` (the key); the fingerprints of §6.1 and §9, `Replication.tla` unchanged, as it leaves out the view and the SSD cache.
- **Verified**, while the soak of 24 hours ran, in the CI's image, package by package: `gofmt`, `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/...`; `go test` of `view/...`, `catalog`, `watch`, `config`, `ops`, `jobs` (455 s), `conformance/models`, `schema`, and `cmd/atoll`'s frozen contract and configuration tests; `go vet -tags loopdev ./test/loopdev/`. Once the soak ended, in the same image: `go test ./...`; the race detector on `view/...`, `jobs`, `ops` and `catalog`, with the 45 minutes of `make test-race` (`jobs` takes 706 s under it, past `go test`'s default 10 minutes); `make conformance`, 645 scenarios; `golangci-lint run ./...`, 0 issues, once the tests' stand-ins for kill -9 carried `//nolint:forbidigo` as the jobs tests' do. With the removal at the path and the warning, on `beta`'s `aeb6f69`: `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`; the race detector on `view/...`, `jobs` (845 s), `ops` and `catalog`, `-timeout 45m`; `make conformance`, 645 scenarios; `golangci-lint run ./...`, with `--build-tags loopdev` and with `--build-tags conformance`, 0 issues each; `make model-check`, its 10 instances passing; and `TestViewMovesAFileThatRunsOutOfSpace` in qemu, `COUNT=3`, on Linux 6.12.117 and 7.3.5, both small disks passing each of the six runs: the copy removed at its path on the one, purged from the trash on the other.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Confirmer"):
  - the writer's lock is held for the journal and for the change of names, steps 1 and 5 to 9, not during the copy, so that the view's other operations do not wait for gigabytes to be copied; the checks of the start are made again under it before the rename;
  - the view presents the target from the moment its copy is at its name and its `trash` is journaled, not only once the move is done, so that no lookup finds the name empty between the copy leaving the full disk and the end;
  - the part's own handle, open since it was made, becomes the file's, rather than an `OpenUserFile` on the target, so that nothing can fail once the copy is at its name;
  - refused besides the decisions: a file with another name on its disk, a hard link's or the link guard's, which would keep the old bytes; and a file written once without an offset, whose handle on the other disk would not be at the same place (FUSE always gives one); a file that moved may move again when its new disk fills;
  - the program gets the write's own `ENOSPC` or `EDQUOT` whenever the move does not happen, whatever stopped it, the reason in the log;
  - confirmed by the maintainer on 2026-11-21 ("Taille et mtime"): the reprise compares the full disk's copy with the moved one by size and modification time, not by content, as reading both whole could pass the 90 s systemd gives `atoll mount` to start; the trash keeps the copy either way;
  - confirmed by the maintainer on 2026-11-21 ("Terminer, sans purge"): a `trash` left planned is carried on rather than undone with both copies left, as the program wrote to the target from then on: a copy left at its path would be an older version, which a pass would find in `conflict` and present by its older creation time;
  - in a reprise, a trash with no room never removes the copy at its path, as answer 3 rested on "the trash keeps it either way": the copy stays, the move planned, the target's copy presented, with a warning, and the next start tries again; until then the passes wait at its path on both disks, and record the file once the full disk has room for the trash;
  - an `unlink` at the copy's path left planned is resolved as §17.2 resolves a purge, the path in place of the trash: gone once its folder is synced, done; the copy there, by inode, undone, the copy staying beside the moved one, never removed on an inode alone; another file, undone with an `op-undone` event; the move done either way, so that a pass then finds both copies;
  - the removal at the path only when a look after the failed trash finds the copy still at its path and nothing in the trash; otherwise the table of §17.2, as before; and the folders the trash made before it ran out of room are cleared, as an undone trash clears them;
  - three crash points added: `move-unlink-journaled`, and `unlinked` and `source-synced` in `ops.RemoveLeft`;
  - a copy its trash refuses in a running move, changed since or its name refused, stays beside the moved one, the move done, for a pass to find both, rather than sent to the trash whatever it holds;
  - the move's `create` is journaled with a `replicate` event naming the full disk, as a part written in parallel's is; the reason is `surplus`, as the transfer's; the folder the file lands in keeps the time the new name gives it, as for what the view makes; a `user.` attribute the target refuses is left out with a line of the log, and no `xattr-kept` event;
  - eight crash points of the conformance build added (`move-journaled`, `move-copied`, `move-placed`, `move-trash-journaled`, `move-swapped`, `move-trashed`, `move-purge-journaled`, `move-purged`);
  - done since, as the maintainer asked on 2026-11-21: the warning for `view.move_on_enospc` with `view.engine = "mergerfs"` (above).

## The soak of 24 hours on 200,000 files a disk, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §26)

`TestDaemonSoak` on `5a3fc74`, every branch of 2026-11-20 merged, in qemu without KVM on Debian 13's Linux 6.12.117, `SOAK=24h SOAK_FILES=200000`, from 2026-11-20 at 05:07 to 2026-11-21 at 05:13 UTC. It passed. Its log is `bin/soak5/soak.log`.

- **No fault**: 1720 passes after the first, none held nor partial, no error, and two warnings, expected, as `smartctl` reads nothing of a loop device in the guest.
- **Memory, at each of the ten tenths**: 3.3 to 3.5 MiB of heap in use from the second tenth on, 9 goroutines, 14 open files, with no growth; 38.0 to 50.8 MiB resident; a peak of 171.7 to 173.0 MiB, under the 200 MB (190.7 MiB) at every tenth, of which the Go runtime held 151.7 to 153.0 MiB, SQLite's allocator 10.2 MiB and the binary's pages 16.7 MiB, none of it in transparent huge pages. The first pass, which finds the 400,000 copies of a catalog that knows none, took 6 min 15 s and peaked at 180.4 MiB. The catalog grew from 73.1 to 151.2 MiB, about 3.3 MiB an hour, its events and its journal, as in the soaks before.
- **The passes**: 8.9 s at the median, 4.5 hours of passes in the 24. By tenth, 8.0, 7.9, 8.8, 8.9, 9.0, 9.0, 9.0 and 9.3 s at the median, with 96 to 104 operations a pass, then 16.2 s in the ninth and 8.1 s in the last: what the catalog's growth costs a pass, if anything, is under what the machine's load moves. From 01:12 to 04:02 UTC on 2026-11-21, the passes took 14 to 20 s, 54.1 s at most at 02:24, with 130 to 150 operations a pass and 240 at most, and the ninth tenth ran 134 passes for about 175: the machine built and tested the updates of `ffaf060` from about 01:10, and from 01:38 an agent the move of a write that meets `ENOSPC`, in containers beside the soak. From 04:02 to the end, they took about 8 s again. As on 2026-11-19, a pass that takes longer gathers more of the soak's changes, so it runs more operations. That it was the load alone is not shown, as then: both times, the passes slowed while other work ran on the machine.

## The latest releases of every module and tool, the qemu guest on Linux 7.3.5, 2026-11-21 (specs.md §26, §27)

The maintainer's answer of 2026-11-21 ("mets la dernière version disponible", then "Toutes, les dernières"): the releases kept as too fresh on 2026-11-20, each at its latest, the rule of 14 days waived for them this once.

- **Modules**: `golang.org/x/sys` v0.49.0 → v0.50.0, `golang.org/x/text` v0.43.0 → v0.44.0, `modernc.org/sqlite` v1.61.1 → v1.62.0 with `modernc.org/libc` v1.79.0 → v1.80.1, the version that release of sqlite requires: libc v1.82.6 is out, but modernc's sqlite runs only on the libc its `go.mod` names. Indirect, through OpenTelemetry's exporters: `golang.org/x/net` v0.62.0 → v0.63.0, `grpc-gateway/v2` v2.33.0 → v2.34.0, and the two `genproto` modules they bring. `go mod verify` passes.
- **x/text and the collision keys**: `names.normRelease` follows the module, v0.44.0, so `CollisionKeyVersion` is `go golang.org/x/text v0.44.0 17.0.0`: a catalog made before recomputes every `lpath_key` when it opens, as §15.8 says of keys another version computed, never comparing them. The Unicode version stays 17.0.0.
- **Tools**: govulncheck v1.9.0 → v1.10.0 (`ci.yml`), GoReleaser v2.20.0 → v2.20.3 (`release.yml`, and the image of `make release-snapshot`), syft v1.57.0 → v1.57.2, `actions/setup-go` v7.0.0 → v7.1.0, pinned by its commit `d8cbb80`.
- **The qemu guest**: mainline v7.3.1 → v7.3.5, the latest stable release of Linux 7.3 (2026-11-17), from kernel.ubuntu.com as before (`test/qemu/run.sh`, `MAINLINE`). Debian 13's kernel is the one its archive gives.
- **Verified** before the push: `go build ./...`, `go vet` and `go test` of `names` and `catalog`, while the soak of 24 hours ran, which keeps the machine quiet; the whole by the CI on GitHub, qemu on 7.3.5 included, then the full check once the soak ended.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): libc kept at the version sqlite requires rather than its latest; x/net, grpc-gateway, syft and setup-go taken too, as "la dernière version disponible" reads, though the question named only the others.

## Mutation tests that run the tests, the pure core measured for real, and tests for rules, integrity and names, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §27)

Raising the mutator coverage of `rules`, `integrity` and `names` to the gate proposed (95 % efficacy, 85 % mutator coverage a pure package) found the measure void. Commits `3625ac2`, `eaadc64`, `900355c`, `1d4a503`, `9aecc5d`, `a850975`, `f6ad110` and `1cb7f38`, merged by `16ed29c`.

- **The flaw**: `test/ci/mutation.sh` passed `--test-cpu 1`, which gremlins v0.6.0 hands to `go test` as the one argument `-cpu 1`; `go test` passes it, with the package, to a test binary of the module's root, which has no Go file, and fails before a test runs ("no Go files", checked by hand). Every mutant reached was counted killed: every efficacy measured until then, 100 %, the nightly workflow's included had it run, said nothing. `GOFLAGS=-cpu=1` instead, and `-count=1`, as a coverage run taken from go's cache gave each mutant 0.6 s and timed out mutants the tests kill.
- **The pure core measured for real** (16ed29c, `WORKERS=12`, 413 s), killed / lived / not covered / timed out, efficacy, mutator coverage: `glob` 63/9/6/92, 87.50 %, 92.31 %; `value` 214/16/31/15, 93.04 %, 88.12 %; `names` 150/9/32/9, 94.34 %, 83.25 %; `rules` 290/1/50/24, 99.66 %, 85.34 %; `config` 306/25/57/0, 92.45 %, 85.31 %; `integrity` 29/1/13/3, 96.67 %, 69.77 %; `solver` 843/62/140/6, 93.15 %, 86.60 %. 123 mutants live: tests missing, or mutants that change nothing (TODO.md). The timed-out mutants of `glob`, 92, are loops a mutant makes endless, which no test would let pass.
- **Tests added**, pure, in each package: rules' `Hidden`, `Reach`, `Effective` and `Sources` built from rules (§11.3); every regex the vectors refuse refused by the grammar alone, and 35 refusals with their offset and reason (§29.5); `HashReader` on the vectors through four kinds of reads, a read that fails, a cancel (§16); UTF-16 counts and non-starter runs (§15.8, §29.5). **Vectors**: 9 in `regex.json`, 1 in `names_check.json`, 4 in `short_form.json`, 1 in `nfc.json`, `aux.nfo` in the name corpus; none changed. Measured on those three before and after: `rules` 199/23/125, 89.6 %, 64.0 % → 293/2/50, 99.3 %, 85.5 %; `integrity` 25/0/19, 100 %, 56.8 % → 29/1/13, 96.7 %, 69.8 %; `names` 144/15/32, 90.6 %, 83.3 % → 150/9/32, 94.3 %, 83.3 %.
- **What tests cannot reach**: gremlins counts every mutant in a `case` expression, and in a constant, as not covered, as Go's coverage blocks end at a switch's `{` and resume after each colon: `rules` 50, `integrity` 13, `names` 32. A gremlins told every mutant is reached (a scratch build, not kept) shows the tests kill all of them but three equivalents. `names` and `integrity` stay under 85 % unless those switches become `if`/`else`, a change of production code; `names` stays under 95 % efficacy with its nine equivalents.
- **Equivalent mutants** named: `regex.go:438:10`, `488:9`, `316:11`, `521:71`; `hash.go:72:8`; `check.go:65:23`, `101:49`, `196:46`, `collision.go:43:10`, `162:9`, `compat.go:159:19`, `164:20`, `unicode.go:44:8`, `44:26`, `44:31`. On a loaded machine one run counted `regex.go:521:71` killed though the tests pass with it applied by hand: a kill may come from outside, so a score may be off by a mutant.
- **Verified**: `go test` of the packages, `make conformance`, lint (default, conformance, loopdev), the mutation runs.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the mutation script fixed though the task asked for tests, its numbers being void; `GOFLAGS` rather than a patched gremlins; no production change, so the switches stay switches and the gate cannot pass on `names` and `integrity` as proposed; error offsets and words pinned in Go tests only, the vectors keeping to error or not; `aux.nfo` in the corpus rather than `name_compat.json`; the U+FFFF count tested in Go rather than a vector; equivalent mutants named rather than worked around.
