The maintainer asked, before going away for eight hours, for the fuzzing and the mutation tests run locally, the coverage raised and the README brought up to date. Every branch of the day merged, on `a3d39c0`, a worktree of it left as it was for both runs.

- **Fuzzing** (`FUZZTIME=5m bash test/ci/fuzz.sh`, `GOMAXPROCS=8`): the 19 targets each for five minutes, in 96 minutes, over a billion executions in all; none found an input that fails, so nothing went to `testdata/fuzz/`. The nightly run of `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml` has never run on GitHub: a scheduled workflow runs from the default branch, and `main` holds no workflow yet, so it, the nightly mutation tests, the weekly media-server test and Dependabot start with the pull request from `beta` to `main`.
- **Mutation tests**, first run (`bash test/ci/mutation.sh`, `WORKERS=8`): 100 % efficacy in each package, a measure found void the same morning, as the script made every test run fail before it started (the entry above); the real numbers are in it.
- **Coverage** (`make cover`, the CI's cover job on `bfe885c`): the unit tests 84.7 %, the scenarios 47.6 %, either 89.3 % of 29,584 statements, from 77.3 % and 83.0 % that morning; `catalog` 94.2 %, `trash` 91.6 %, `watch` 89.6 %, `fsx` 89.1 %, `ops` 88.8 %, `jobs` 87.1 % by the unit tests; `cmd/atoll` 52.1 % by them and 86.8 % with the scenarios.
- **The CI** is green in every job on `7dde53b`, `7744288` and `bfe885c` (run 44670625740), its qemu job running the whole of `test/loopdev` on Linux 6.12 and 7.3 and under systemd, so the loop-device tests that the day's fixes of the scrub, repair, ops, watch and the daemon touch ran there.
- **README.md**: where Atoll stands (the first release waits for a soak of 24 hours and tries on real disks); what guards and checks it: the guards within minutes or over a day, the scrub that repairs or moves a corrupt copy and never presents it, the mount check (a disk away missing, never empty; an NTFS volume Windows left hibernated only read; the remount after a USB reset through systemd); each test's `make` target, and the nightly runs once the workflows are on `main`.
- **Left for later on the day**: x/sys v0.50.0 is 14 days old at 19:57 UTC and Linux 7.3.2 at 17:24 UTC, after the maintainer's eight hours; both wait (TODO.md).

## A corrupt copy left in the root taken again by the next scrub, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §15.13, §16, §16.1, §25; invariant 14)

Found by the fault sweeps of jobs: a corrupt copy that a scrub could neither repair nor send to the trash, its disk or the healthy copy's failing then, or the machine stopping, or that a scrub without `--yes` only found, stayed corrupt in `<root>/`, where mergerfs may present it. No scrub read a copy that is not `ok`, and no pass moves a corrupt one, so only `atoll repair` by hand ended it. Invariant 14 asks for such a copy out of the root as soon as it is found. Commit `24909dd`, merged by `a3d39c0`.

- **Each scrub of a disk takes them first** (§16), before the copy its budget stopped in and the copies due, as one found then: looked at as a repair looks at it, settled as one when it changed (§16.1), the healthy copy read whole first, then repaired from it or moved to the trash, or said to be without `--yes` (exit 3). It takes those whose disk can be written to, whose file has another copy `ok` on a disk that can be read, and, in a scrub that writes, with no operation left `planned` at their name. A file whose only copy is corrupt, or a corrupt copy on a disk that may only be read, stays as it is, marked and alerted, and is not listed again. No `corrupt` event and no line in `corrupt`, which says what this scrub found: the copy goes in `repaired`, `quarantined`, `kept` or `conflict` (the schema's descriptions say so; shapes unchanged, format version 1 kept). The budget counts them; a disk with nothing due and none to take is still not opened.
- **The daemon** takes each once in each stretch of `integrity.window`, keeping those it took in memory (a restart takes them again), so that a copy that cannot go costs one read of its healthy copy a night, not one a slice; one repaired or moved brings a pass, which ends the file's `corrupt` alert and makes the copy again.
- **Why the scrub and not a pass**: a pass decides nothing of a corrupt file and cancels its trash jobs (§15.4, §15.12), reads no file whole in the daemon, while the quarantine needs the healthy copy read first, and would go around `auto_repair`; the scrub holds the whole path already.
- **Tests that failed first**: `TestACorruptCopyADiskFailureKeptGoesAtTheNextScrub` (EIO at the first write after the re-read, the disk back), `TestACorruptCopyACrashKeptGoesAtTheNextScrub` (a crash at `journaled`), `TestACorruptCopyFoundWithoutYesGoesWithIt`, each repaired and quarantined; the integrity sweeps of disks and of the catalog without their `atoll repair`; `TestTheDaemonTakesACorruptCopyAgainOnceAStretch`. Beside them: `TestACorruptCopyWithNothingToMendItFromIsLeftAsItIs`, `TestACorruptCopyTakenAgainIsLeftOutOfTheStretch`, `TestTheBudgetCountsTheCorruptCopiesTakenAgain`. No conformance scenario: the runner cannot make rot (`scan-full-changes.json`). The loop-device suite runs in the CI's qemu job.
- **Verified**: `go test ./...`; the race detector on jobs, ops, integrity, trash, catalog and daemon; `make conformance`; lint (default, conformance, loopdev, mediaservers). No fingerprinted section changed; `Replication.tla` unchanged.
- **Found, not changed**: `catalog.CorruptCopies` has no partial index on `state = 'corrupt'`; each daemon slice reads a disk's copies through `copies_by_disk`, as `ScrubDue` does.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the scrub rather than a pass; once a stretch in the daemon, in memory, and at each run of `atoll scrub`; not listed in `corrupt` and no event; a copy with no candidate healthy copy, or on a readonly disk, not listed again, and one whose candidate does not read healthy `kept` (exit 4); a copy that reads right after all `kept`, as for one found now; the scrub without `--yes` and `--dry-run` take them too, reading the healthy copy, so that the preview shows what `--yes` moves; a pass brought only once one is repaired or moved.

## Four flaws found on 2026-11-20 fixed: a links folder read as empty, a second listing, an empty mount point's message, a rebuild's last rename, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §9, §10, §17, §25; invariant 4)

The two flaws the tests of fsx and the trash found, the message seen in qemu, and the rebuild's rename left as it was, each fixed after a test that failed first. Commits `ae56431`, `2a6dfa9`, `e88fc21` and `5cf9887`, merged by `d440d1e`.

- **A links folder that cannot be read** (`ae56431`, `TestALinksFolderItCannotReadIsNoFolderWithNoLink`, `TestAPurgeThatCannotReadTheLinksFolder`): `ops.ownLinks` took a `links/` it could not read, `EIO` included, for one with no link, so a purge went on and the purged data stayed behind its link until the next pass sent it to the trash again. An absent folder still holds no link. A disk's error there loses the disk, the operation left for the reprise. Any other error, another mount over the folder included, fails the look, and nothing of a failed read is kept. A purge purges nothing of an entry whose links it cannot tell (`io-error`, the disk kept). A purge counts the entry's names before its unlink, and a link of the guard reads the folder before it is made. A move that went through before a look failed is resolved as a reprise would, never undone. Tested on a look, `OwnLinksOf`, `PurgeEntry`, `ToTrash` and `MakeLink` under `EIO`, `ENOTCONN`, `EACCES`, `EMFILE` and another mount, and with no folder; and through the trash's purge. §17 says it.
- **A second listing of one handle** (`2a6dfa9`, `TestNamesGivesEveryNameEachTime`): `realDir.Names` read from the handle's position, so a second `Names` gave nothing where the interface says every name. It now seeks to the start, under a lock of the handle, as the disk's top is shared by copies at once. Tested on a real folder of 1,001 names, listed twice, after a name came, and by eight callers at once, through the handle and the fault layer. Nothing visible changes today: every caller listed a handle once.
- **An empty mount point under another mount** (`e88fc21`, `TestNotMountedSaysWhatIsMountedThere`): seen in qemu, a mount point in /tmp read "the mount is at "/tmp": write that path, not one through a symlink". When the entry's mount point lies above the path, by whole components, it now reads "nothing is mounted at that path: it is part of the mount at "/tmp"". The reason is still `not-mounted`, and the vectors, which pin reasons, already had the case.
- **A rebuild's rename after the swap** (`5cf9887`, `TestARenameThatFailsAfterTheSwapSaysTheNewCatalogIsInPlace`, `TestARebuildSaysWhereEachCatalogIs`): once `RENAME_EXCHANGE` put the new catalog in place, a failed rename of the old one to `.old-<time>` was reported as the new catalog "not yet in place". `catalog.OldNotSetAside` now says where each is. The command still exits with 1 and says the new catalog is in place, where the old one and its log are, that a rebuild run again would remove `<catalog>.rebuild`, and the rename that puts them together, in French too. §25 says it.
- **Found, not changed** (TODO.md): a guard link a purge cannot remove for an error that is not the disk's stays with a warning, and the purge goes on, the data behind it until the next pass; a rebuild's start removes `<catalog>.rebuild` and its log, which after that failed rename, or a crash between the exchange and the rename, hold the old catalog.
- **Verified**: `go test ./...`; the race detector on ops, fsx, disks, catalog, trash and jobs; `make conformance`; lint (default, conformance, loopdev, mediaservers); no fingerprint changed.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): every error of `links/` that is not the disk's (`EACCES`, `EMFILE`) makes the look fail and the purge purge nothing, and so does another mount over it; a move that went through before such a look resolved as a reprise would; no scenario for the first two, which the runner cannot make fail; a lock per handle for `Names`, through `os.File.Seek`; the new message's wording, untranslated as no verdict's message is; exit code 1 kept for the failed rename, nothing moved after it, the user told to rename the old catalog; `catalog.OldNotSetAside` a new exported type, with no scenario as only standard error changes.

## Fault sweeps of jobs and ops, and a purge of chunks that ended on a chunk it could not look at, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §14.2, §15.4, §15.12, §16, §16.1, §17.1, §17.2, §19, §21.2)

The maintainer asked for the coverage to go up. The unit tests of `internal/jobs` and `internal/ops` now inject a fault at every call an operation makes on a disk, and at every statement of the catalog, and check what must hold whatever fails. Commits `d5350bb`, `8575e39`, `c928423`, `f770c30` and `29ffa14`, merged by `303aa8f`.

- **The bug**: `ops.Disk.PurgeChunks` took a chunk it could not look at (`EIO`, `EACCES`) for no chunk. The purge ended, the entry left the catalog, and the chunk stayed in the trash with nothing left to purge it. The purge now stays planned for the reprise, as any I/O error of a purge does (invariant 4); an absent chunk is still skipped. The ops sweep fails on the base. No scenario can inject an I/O error yet.
- **The ops sweeps** (`internal/ops/sweep*_test.go`): every `Disk` operation (moves to and from the trash, renames of files and folders, a part placed, a guard link sent to the trash and an exchange, on ext4 and with the rename ntfs-3g refuses; parts, folders below the root, the guard's links, chunks saved, written and purged, metadata, extended attributes, the catalog's copy and the marker; the looks before a write; what the view does for a user) runs once per call it makes, that call failing with `EIO` then `EACCES`. Nothing is lost, no error is a success, and `EIO` stays "disk unknown", never a name gone, taken or refused (invariants 1 and 4).
- **The pass sweeps** (`internal/jobs/sweep_test.go`): a pass of renames, deletions, a folder renamed, a mode and a time; the reprises after a crash at each point; a refresh and a copy on the target and on the source, with their reprises at `mkdir-made`, `copied`, `placed` and `source-synced`; `atoll replicate`; an SSD-cache transfer; an attribute set through the view; an exchange of two names and its reprise. Each call on one disk fails with `EIO` then `EACCES`; the pass ends without an error, then passes and a full scan end as the pass with nothing failing does: the same files, bytes, modes, attributes and rows, nothing planned, the trash holding at least as much.
- **The catalog sweeps** (`catalogfaults_test.go`): a `database/sql` driver that wraps SQLite's fails each statement, begin and commit in turn, a failed commit rolled back; the same cases end the same once the catalog answers again (invariant 12). **The integrity sweeps** (`integritysweep_test.go`): a scrub that repairs or quarantines, and a repair by chunk and whole, with faults on either disk and in the catalog: the healthy copy is never written, the rotten bytes never lost before they reach the trash. **Targeted tests**: `--move-originals`, balance with replication off, the catalog's backup on the disks, a transfer held (dry run, battery, readonly cache), and a file that takes a copy's name just before its move to the trash, which goes back untouched, the operation undone with `op-undone`.
- **Race**: a sweep fails one call in eight under `-race` (build tags `race`/`!race`) and every call without it; jobs under race takes 17 to 22 minutes on this machine.
- **Measured** (`make cover`, unit column): jobs 78.2 % → 87.0 %, ops 74.8 % → 88.4 %; the module 81.0 % by the unit tests, 47.7 % by the scenarios, 86.2 % by either (29,445 statements).
- **Left** (TODO.md): an SSD-cache copy a transfer sent to the trash stays for the retention when a fault comes between its move and its purge (§19), kept rather than purged at once; a corrupt copy the scrub could neither repair nor quarantine, its disk failing then, stays in the root until `atoll repair`, as no later scrub reads it and no pass sends it to the trash (invariant 14 with mergerfs); a disk lost earlier in a pass keeps its own set of the view's attributes, `disk-unavailable` (§21.2); a file re-created at a copy's name just before the rename, with the same inode number, would go to the trash in its place, as the check after a rename and the §17.2 table go by inode alone where the link path also compares size and modification time (seen once, not reproduced in 31 runs).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): a purge of chunks that cannot look at a chunk stays planned, tried again at each run, rather than ending; the sweeps accept the four limits above as they stand; one call in eight under the race detector.

## A stop as a copy moves keeps its change time's record, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §14.2)

Found while checking the merges together: `TestFaultsWithCopiesAtOnce/stopped` failed about one run in ten under the race detector, 5 copies verified for 6. Commit `2de64d2`.

- **The flaw**: the record of the change time the look right after a copy's rename found (flaw 1's limit, `catalog.RecordPlaced`) took the pass's context. A stop at that moment, as SIGTERM or a low battery gives it, cancelled its commit, and the reprise recorded a copy already at its name unverified, which a crash may leave but a stop never should. The commit now runs whatever the stop (`context.WithoutCancel`): one commit, the copy being at its name already.
- **Tests**: `TestAStopAsACopyMovesKeepsItsRecord` (a stop at the rename itself, one copy at a time), which failed first; it, `TestFaultsWithCopiesAtOnce/stopped` and `TestACrashWithCopiesAtOnce` pass 20 times in a row under the race detector.

## Go 1.27.4, the modules and the tools at their latest stable releases, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §26, §27)

The maintainer asked to bring the libraries and containers to their latest stable versions ("mettre à jour les lib et container avec la dernière version stable"). Commits `c046017`, `e574def` and `e8f90bd`, merged by `54617f1`.

- **Go 1.27.4** (go.mod's `go` line, from 1.27.0): its standard library fixes GO-2026-6859 (net/http) and GO-2026-6809 (crypto/x509), which govulncheck found reachable with the CI image's Go 1.27.2; taken at once, though ten days old. No golangci-lint image ships them yet: v2.15.0, the latest, stays, and its `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` makes its go fetch go1.27.4 from the module proxy, checked against sum.golang.org. GoReleaser's image sets `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` with Go 1.27.3, so `make release-snapshot` and README's command pass `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto`.
- **Modules**, each to its newest stable release at least 14 days old: grpc v1.85.0, grpc-gateway v2.33.0, x/net v0.62.0 (past GO-2026-6860 and GO-2026-6866, which Atoll did not reach), proto/otlp v1.11.1, and genproto at the commits they require. No module joins go.mod, no new indirect one. OpenTelemetry v1.47.0, go-fuse v2.12.1, go-toml v2.4.3, rapid v1.3.0, gremlins v0.6.0 and blake3's pin, still the head of its main branch, are the latest.
- **Tools**: TLC in `eclipse-temurin:21.0.13_9-jre-noble`; syft v1.57.0. tla2tools stays 1.7.4 (1.8.0 is a prerelease). Every action is at its latest release at least 14 days old, its SHA checked against its tag.
- **Kept as too fresh**: x/sys v0.50.0 (13 days on the day), x/text v0.44.0, sqlite v1.62.0 with its libc and memory, x/net v0.63.0, grpc-gateway v2.34.0, GoReleaser v2.20.1 and v2.20.2 (their redaction fixes reach nothing Atoll's release uses; eligible on 2026-11-26), govulncheck v1.10.0, syft v1.57.1 and v1.57.2, setup-go v7.1.0. The qemu guest still boots mainline 7.3.1; 7.3.2 to 7.3.5 are out, none 14 days old on the day.
- **Checks** on the merged `beta`, with go1.27.4: `go build`, `go vet`, `go test ./...`, the race detector on jobs, ops, catalog, trash, watch, disks, daemon, integrity, fsx and `cmd/atoll`, `make conformance` (645 scenarios), lint (default, conformance, loopdev, mediaservers), govulncheck ("No vulnerabilities found"), `make model-check` in the new image (ten instances); in qemu, on Linux 6.12 and 7.3.1, the replicate, view, break-glass and remount tests.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the rule itself, latest stable only, a security fix Atoll is exposed to taken at once, anything else only once 14 days old, no new direct dependency; the 14 days counted in hours; Go 1.27.4 as go.mod's minimum, with no `toolchain` line; the golangci-lint image kept with its `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` rather than another image; `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` for the release snapshot; libc kept with sqlite; GoReleaser's and syft's security fixes judged out of Atoll's reach; Temurin's date taken from Docker's official images.

## The merges of 2026-11-20 checked together, and two tests they broke (specs.md §14.2, §15.6)

The branches of the day merged on `beta` one after the other, then every check on the whole: `go test ./...`, the race detector on jobs, ops, catalog, trash, watch, disks, daemon, integrity, fsx and `cmd/atoll`, `make conformance`, lint, and `make model-check` (all ten instances). Two failures came of branches written side by side, each a test's, none the code's:

- **`TestACrashWithCopiesAtOnce/verified`** (`d629e21`), under the race detector: 5 copies verified, not 6. With four copies at once, a crash at any point of one copy can find another between its rename and the commit of its change time, which the reprise rightly records unverified (flaw 1's limit); the test read every copy again by a full scan only at the point `moved`. It does so at each point now; three runs in a row under the race detector pass.
- **`replica-edited-keeping-dates-after-record`** and **`-before-record`** (`fdc284a`), written beside the guard over a day: they set `modifies.mass_guard` at 100 % but not `day_guard`, which a pool of two copies trips at its first modification, as the 63 other scenarios that keep the window away do. 645 scenarios pass.
- **The sweep of the catalog's failing steps** (`c1b78f2`), written beside the guard over a day, flaw 1's limit and the folder with no model, called `ReadModifyGuard`, `WriteModifyGuardEvent`, `FinishCreate` and `MkdirLike` as they were before them; it compiles against the new ones, and every test of every build tag compiles and passes.

## Two disks lost or written wrong, found by the unit tests of fsx and the trash, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §10, §16.1, §17.1, §17.2; invariants 4 and 12)

The maintainer asked for the coverage to go up ("augmente aussi le taux de coverage"). The coverage of `internal/fsx` and `internal/trash` is raised to 89.3 % and 89.7 % by tests that each check what the code must do; they found two flaws, fixed after a test that failed first. Commits `667d2df`, `e9f686a`, `613d1d6`, `bed0328` and `4c5d1e0`, merged by `59e19d8`.

- **A chunk that cannot be read back lost its disk at every run** (`e9f686a`, `TestARepairWhoseChunkCannotBeReadBackLosesNoDiskThatConfirms`): a chunk written in place that could not be read back, on a disk that confirmed, stopped the repair with `io-error` as §16.1 says. But the repair's own look at the copy right after read that chunk again and took `EIO` for the disk's: the disk went `missing`. The reprise of each later run did the same while the sector could not be read, so the copy never reached the trash and nothing else was written there. A read that fails in that look is now `ops.ErrChunkUnreadable`, and the session confirms the disk (§10), in the run and at the reprise. One that confirms is written to as before, the operation `planned` for a reprise that can read the chunk (exit code 4); one that does not is lost.
- **A disk lost while a session opened it still took writes** (`613d1d6`, `TestADiskLostWhileOpenedTakesNoWrite`): a disk whose `tmp/` answered `EIO` as the session opened it was recorded `missing`, and its handle went back to the caller all the same. A transfer and a send to the trash journaled and moved a copy there, and a repair journaled its chunks, on a disk the session had just lost (§17.1). The caller now gets no handle.
- **fsx** (`667d2df`): the fault layer, each call of a folder, a file and a copy in progress failing alone, about the name it documents, reaching nothing underneath, and going through once nothing fails; a closed handle answers every call with an error that keeps its cause; one table gives what each errno says to `IsDiskLost`, `IsUnreadableName` and the 15 other questions; the system disk's helpers; extended attributes as the system calls give them (a value that grew, a name gone since listed, `ENOTSUP`) and on a real file through each handle; a block device's head of a file, a FIFO or `/dev/null` never read and never hanging; a `/sys` tree that loops or leads nowhere an error; `/proc` giving no creation time.
- **trash** (`bed0328`): `conflicts resolve`, which had no test in the package; `restore --to` failing at each step of its copy; restores, purges by slices, a purge of saved chunks, of an entry replaced or of a disk gone; the link guard where it cannot read, make or look; the folders a user removed through the view. The catalog refuses each kind of operation through an SQLite trigger the tests make: nothing moves before its row (invariant 12), and a move whose end it cannot record is left to the reprise.
- **Not covered**: in fsx, what needs ntfs-3g, a block device, `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, an account other than root or a failing `fsync` (test/loopdev's), and Linux before 5.8; in the trash, the `catalog: %w` of reads a trigger cannot fail, and guards nothing reaches. No guard removed.
- **Found, not changed** (TODO.md): a second `Names` on one folder handle lists nothing, as it reads from the handle's position (every caller lists a handle once); `ops.ownLinks` takes a links folder it cannot read, `EIO` included, for one with no link, so a purge goes on and the purged data stays behind its link until the next pass (no data lost, but not invariant 4's reading).
- **Verified**: `go test ./...`; the race detector on fsx, trash, ops and jobs; `make conformance`; lint (default, conformance, loopdev); each fix commit's tests on its own.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): a chunk the repair's look cannot read confirmed in the run and at the reprise, the operation `planned` with exit code 4 for as long as the chunk cannot be read; `ops.ErrChunkUnreadable` a new exported error; no conformance scenario nor loop-device test for either flaw (the runner can make neither an unreadable sector nor `EIO` on `tmp/`), the unit tests pinning them.

## The catalog and the watch past 85 %, and a rebuild's swap that kept the old catalog's log from it, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §9, §15.1, §15.10, §15.13, §16, §17)

Unit-test coverage of `internal/catalog` from 74.9 % to 94.0 % and of `internal/watch` from 79.7 % to 88.7 %, with tests that check behaviour. Commits `51a973c`, `e428a19`, `a09b39c` and `c2ed041`, merged by `19fd9ca`.

- **Found and fixed**: `catalog.SetAside` renamed the old catalog's `-wal` and `-shm` to their `.old-<time>` names before the swap. When the swap failed, they stayed renamed, so the old catalog kept its name without its log, losing commits a checkpoint had not moved into it, as with a catalog of a later version or a damaged one, the cases a rebuild is for. A swap that fails now puts the log back (`TestASwapThatFailsKeepsTheOldCatalogWhole`, which failed first). The CLI cannot make the exchange fail, so there is no scenario.
- **Every read and write of the catalog under a failing step**: a test driver wraps SQLite's and fails one step, a begin, a prepare, an exec, a query, a row or a commit; 127 readers and 92 writers run once per step from a pool holding a little of everything. Each failure comes back wrapped; a reader gives nothing with its error; a writer leaves every table as it was (invariant 12). Seven give a value with their error (`FilePaths`, `GonePaths`, `CopyOf`, `TrashEntries`, `AnswerModifyGuard`, `ConfirmGuard`, `RejectGuard`), which every caller drops (checked). Twenty runs of the sweeps in a row give the same.
- **What had no test**: duplicates and unhashed files (§16), the rules' subjects, the trash listed again and file ids started past the links by a rebuild (§17), the checkpoint of a catalog this version refuses, a guard's hold that cleared itself (§15.6), a copy that caught up with its file, a stale copy in the trash made again (§15.4), the keys of old paths of another version (§15.8), a backup with a row without its file, and backups that cannot be written.
- **watch, with the fault-injecting fsx and no root**: `EIO`, a moved root, an unmount or out-of-range times mid-walk or mid-look make the disk missing, and no copy is concluded gone (invariant 4); the inotify source's overflows, a removed root and folders moved in; fanotify records read through a pipe, and each event's origin; capacity against §13.2's example; the targets of parallel copies and the cache's transfers; `catalog verify` writes nothing. A test of the watch that failed once in a hundred runs, `TestEventsSayWhoMadeThem`, waits for its stand-in mergerfs to run before reading its command line (`c2ed041`; 500 runs in a row pass).
- **Not covered**: in catalog, `Scan`, `LastInsertId` and JSON errors that SQLite's STRICT tables and plain structs never give; in watch, the fanotify marks (`CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, test/loopdev in qemu) and guards a parsed configuration never reaches.
- **Verified**: `go test ./...`; the race detector on catalog and watch; `make cover`; lint as the CI runs it (default, conformance, loopdev, mediaservers).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): a failed swap puts the log back; a failed rename after a successful exchange left as it is, the old catalog at `<catalog>.rebuild`, its log under the `.old-` name, and a message that says the new catalog is not in place, though it is; the seven functions that give a value with their error left unchanged, as their callers drop it.

## A hibernated NTFS volume readonly on every driver, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §10, §22, §25, §30)

The maintainer decided point 3 of `docs/research/ntfs-driver.md` ("Oui, readonly"). Commit `c156ad2`, merged by `de02979`. Vectors and scenarios first: 37 new cases in `disk_check.json`, 19 of which failed before the check read `hiberfil.sys`, and one note changed; the scenarios `disk-list-hibernated` and `daemon-hibernated-ntfs` fail on the base, which copied a file to the hibernated disk.

- **The reason `hibernated`** (§10): an NTFS disk, on ntfs3, `ntfs`, ntfs-3g or lowntfs-3g, whose mount's top holds `hiberfil.sys` under 4 KiB, or whose first 4 KiB are not all zeros, or anything at that name that is not a regular file, is `readonly`. It sits after `errors-continue`, before `casefold` to `ignore-case`, `read-only-mount` and `other-marker`, in the closed list and in the `reason` enums of `api/schemas/daemon`, `disk-list` and `scan`, as an addition: format version 1 kept.
- **The only file a check reads**: once questions 1 to 10 pass and the driver's options leave the disk online; through a handle on the top whose mount id is the one question 2 found, by its exact name, following no link, with `O_NOATIME` where the process may (`fsx.ReadStart`). `EIO`, `ENOTCONN`, `ENODEV`, `ENXIO`, `EROFS` and `ESTALE` make the disk `missing`, `io-error` (invariant 4); any other error makes it `readonly`, `hibernated`, as nothing can be told.
- **Once per mount**: the daemon and the view keep what they read, by mount id, device numbers and `STATX_MNT_ID_UNIQUE` (`fsx.UniqueMountID`, Linux 6.8 and later), and read it again once a check finds no mount of its own at the path; errors are never kept. In qemu, the mount table gave the next mount the same id, which the unique id told apart, and the next check of the same mount read nothing from the device.
- **`disk add`** takes such a disk `readonly`, with the warning `readonly`, a message of its own ("until Windows has shut down fully and it is mounted again", translated), and no marker.
- **§22**: Fast Startup and why it destroys files; what clears it, a full shutdown, Fast Startup off, `powercfg /h off`, or ntfs-3g's `remove_hiberfile` (measured, which loses Windows' session); and that `ntfsfix` does not: measured on ntfs-3g 2022.10.3, it refuses the volume, leaves `hiberfil.sys`, but first empties `$LogFile` and sets flags on the volume, so it writes to it. §22 says never to run it on such a volume.
- **Measured in qemu, Linux 6.12 and 7.3**: ntfs3, and `ntfs` under `errors=continue`, mount a hibernated volume read-write (the check gives `hibernated`, and `errors-continue` first for the latter); `ntfs` under `errors=remount-ro` mounts it with superblock options `ro`, ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g read-only, each `hibernated`; with 8 KiB of zeros, or no file, every driver mounts it read-write and the disk is `online`. ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g move the access time of a file read on a read-write mount despite `O_NOATIME`.
- **Tests**: `TestCheck`, `TestCheckStopsAtTheFirstFailure`, `TestDiskCheckCoversEveryReason`; `TestReadHiberfil` (`EIO`, `ENOTCONN`, `ENODEV`, `EACCES`, `EMFILE`, the access time kept); `TestHiberfilIsReadOncePerMount`, `TestHiberfilCacheAtOnce`, `TestJudgeHiberfil`, `TestTheMachineReadsHiberfilOncePerMount`; `TestReadStart`, `TestUniqueMountID`; three new cases in `TestDiskAddRefusesNameRules`; in `test/loopdev`, `TestAHibernatedVolumeOnEachDriver`, `TestAHibernatedVolumeIsReadOncePerMount`, `TestWhatClearsAHibernation`, with a set of NTFS and daemon tests (the remount after a reset, device failures, the failed mount, replicate, the dirty volume, break glass) on both kernels. `go test ./...`, race on disks, daemon, fsx, jobs, ops, watch, trash, view and `cmd/atoll`, `make conformance`, lint (default, conformance, loopdev) and `make model-check` pass; the §10 fingerprint updated, `Replication.tla` unchanged (a disk is up or gone there).
- **Left**: a data volume Windows had mounted when Fast Startup ran holds no `hiberfil.sys`; its sign, a `$LogFile` left unclean, which ntfs-3g refuses, is neither read nor measured (§22, §30), a decision for the maintainer (TODO.md). Before Linux 6.8 (Debian 12's 6.1) there is no unique mount id: an unmount and a mount between two checks keep the old reading until a check sees the disk unmounted.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the name `hibernated`; its place after `errors-continue`, so the driver's options come first and the file is not read then; the exact name `hiberfil.sys`; anything at that name that is not a regular file counted as hibernated; a lost disk's errors make it `missing`, `io-error`, which can start the remount after a USB reset, other errors `readonly`, `hibernated`; the cache's key and its forgetting, a mount that changes during the read giving `readonly` for that check; `O_NOATIME` with a fallback; `disk add`'s message of its own; the conformance build reading the folder at each check, with no field in the control file; §22's remedies through Windows, not measured with Windows.

## A folder with no model made 0755 whatever the umask, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §9, §14.2, §15.12, §17.2, §17.4)

The maintainer's answer of 2026-11-20 ("Forcer 0755"): a folder Atoll makes below a disk's root that stands for none takes 0755 whatever the unit's `UMask=0027`, which left it 0750 and owned by `atoll`, so that a media server running under another account lost what landed below it with nothing said. Commits `6880918` and `0c4b403`, merged by `08c4607`.

- **Where**: every folder below the root goes through `ops.Disk.MakeRootFolder`, for a copy (§14.2: balance, the cache's transfer, evacuation and `disk replace` included), a pass's renames and folder jobs (§15.12, step 3), `trash restore` and `trash restore --to` (§17.4). With no model, it is made 0755, then given 0755 by `fchmod` through a handle on it, by its name without following a link, while it holds nothing. Only the permission bits are set: the setgid bit a parent passes on stays. The owner stays the one `mkdir` gives: the process's user, its group or the parent's (setgid, `grpid`), on NTFS the mount's. A refused mode leaves the folder as made, with a warning. Its event `mkdir` now holds the mode 0755 and no owner, and a reprise that finds the folder made and not yet given it gives it then; a crash point `mkdir-made` sits between the two. Atoll's own folders below `<workdir>` keep the umask; Atoll's own view always has a model and is unchanged.
- **Measured** in qemu on Linux 6.12 and 7.3 under 0027: `mkdir` leaves it 0750 on ext4, XFS, btrfs, ntfs3 and the type `ntfs`, which keep the 0755 given, after a crash between the two too; ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g give every folder 0777, as their mount says. Under systemd, the daemon's and the view's processes have the umask 0027 (`checkRunsAsShipped`).
- **The conformance runner** takes a `umask` on an `atoll` step and a `mode` on a folder of a tree.
- **Tests that failed first**: `TestACopysParentWithNoModelIsOpenWhateverTheUmask` (and under a setgid folder, 2755), `TestARenamesParentWithNoModelIsOpenWhateverTheUmask`, `TestAFolderWithNoModelTakesItsModeAtTheReprise`, `TestADiskLostAsAFolderIsGivenItsMode`, `TestARestoredFolderWithNoModelIsOpenWhateverTheUmask`, `TestAFolderRestoredToAnotherDiskWithNoModelIsOpenWhateverTheUmask`, the scenarios `daemon-parent-no-model-umask` and `daemon-crash-parent-no-model-umask`; in qemu `TestAFolderWithNoModelOnEachDriver`, with `TestTheDaemonUnderSystemd`. `go test ./...`, race on jobs, ops, catalog and trash, `make conformance`, lint (default, conformance, loopdev) and `make model-check` pass; `Replication.tla` unchanged (it leaves folders out), the fingerprints of §9, §14, §14.2, §15.12 and §17.2 updated.
- **Found, a gap of §14.2 this widens**: a copy looks for its model on the source's disk alone. When the source sits behind (its path not yet renamed) and the disk that holds the real folder is away, the copy makes that folder with no model, now 0755 where it was 0750: a folder kept 0700 on the disk away is more open on the copy's. §15.12, step 3, waits (`io-error`) in the same case. A decision for the maintainer (TODO.md).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): only the permission bits set, so a setgid bit inherited from the parent stays (2755); the event `mkdir` of a folder with no model holds `mode` and no `uid` or `gid`, an addition to what format 1 holds; a folder that holds anything by the time it is given its mode, at once or at the reprise, keeps what `mkdir` gave it, as adopt does; the reprise compares the permission bits alone; a new crash point `mkdir-made`; the runner's `umask` and folder `mode`, additions to its format.

## The limit of flaw 1's fix, closed both ways, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §9, §14.2, §17.2)

The maintainer's answer of 2026-11-20 ("Les deux"). Commit `d2063f5`, merged by `cad750d`.

- **Why**: a write into a new copy that keeps its size, times, mode and owner moves only its change time. Flaw 1's fix took that change time from the look right after the rename, or from the reprise's: the width of a `stat` in a normal run, the whole time Atoll was down in a reprise. Such a write was recorded as the copy, verified, for a scrub to call it corrupt and repair it away.
- **(b) The change time after the rename, recorded**: right after the rename and its look (`moved`), the job records the change time that look found, with the operation's id, in a commit of its own, before the fsyncs and the copy's row (`catalog.RecordPlaced`, the payload's `placed`, `{"op", "ctime_ns"}`, crash point `placed`). A reprise that finds another reads a write since: the row is the part's, and the next look finds the copy `edited`, a modification or a change of metadata. A record is of its operation alone; a new operation, a verification or a part dropped clears it.
- **(a) No record, the copy unverified**: a reprise that finds none (the crash or the disk lost came between the rename and that commit) and a change time other than the part's records the copy with `verified_ns` NULL, with a `WARN` line. The source gets `verified_ns` and the file the hash, as they are what was read. A later `--full` or scrub reads a write there as a `conflict`, never a corruption; `atoll prune` counts it unverified, so `balance --move-originals` keeps the user copy it stands in for until a full scan. A job an earlier version verified, with no `part_look`, finishes unverified too (`ops.RecoveredRow`, `conformance/vectors/create_reprise.json`, 14 cases).
- **The normal run's window stays**, said in §14.2: the width of a `stat`; on a driver without `RENAME_NOREPLACE` (ntfs-3g, lowntfs-3g), from the `link` to the look after the `unlink`, which spans the syncs of the file and of the target folder (found while modelling). On those drivers a power cut that undoes the unlink after the record makes the reprise's own unlink move the change time: the copy is read again once, `metadata` at worst.
- **The model**: objects and rows count what moves the change time alone; a user's `rewrite`, keeping size and dates; the record step `CopyRecord`; the reprise's row as §14.2 says (`RepriseRow`); `ReplicaInSync` looks past a copy a reprise recorded in doubt, which `DoubtUnverified` keeps unverified. New instance `FlawReplicaRewritten` (85,696 states, pass). Without (b), TLC's trace is a rename, its record, a rewrite, a kill and the reprise; without (a), the same with the disk lost before the record. All ten instances pass. Fingerprints of §9, §14, §14.2 and §17.2 follow.
- **Tests that failed first**: `TestRecoveredRowVectors`; `TestAWriteThatMovesOnlyTheChangeTimeWhileAtollIsDown` (placed, target-synced, source-synced), `…BeforeItsRecordLeavesTheCopyUnverified`, `…OnADiskLostAfterItsRename` (EIO before the record and after it), `…BeforeItsRowIsAnEdit/placed`; `TestACrashAtEachPointOfACopy` and `TestAPassKilledDuringACopy` (moved, placed); `TestAWalkLeavesAPlannedCopyAlone`; `TestAMoveOfAnOriginalStopsAtACopyRecordedUnverified` (the code before retired the user copy); the scenarios `replica-edited-keeping-dates-after-record.json` and `-before-record.json`. In qemu, Linux 6.12 and 7.3, `TestAWriteKeepingTheDatesOnEachDriver` on the seven drivers, beside the replicate tests and `TestADeviceThatFailsDuringACopy`, now with `placed`. `go test ./...`, race on jobs, ops, catalog and trash, `make conformance`, lint and `make model-check` pass.
- **Contract additions**: the job payload's `placed`; the crash point `placed`; `create_reprise.json`, with the vector flag `unverified`. No migration.
- **Left**: a copy recorded unverified waits for `--full` or a scrub, which reads never-verified copies first; which two drivers' renames left the change time as it was on Linux 6.12 is not measured.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the record in the job's payload, bound to the operation's id, not in `ops`, which has no payload column; committed right after the look, before the fsyncs, so that `moved` is (a)'s crash point and a new `placed` follows it; with a record and another change time found, the row takes the part's change time, not the recorded one, so an earlier change time reads `edited`, never a `conflict`; (a) whatever else moved, and for a job with no `part_look`; in (a) the file still takes the hash and the source `verified_ns`; a reprise whose own unlink of the name left in `tmp/` moved the change time reads it as a write, one more read; a `WARN` line at each copy recorded unverified; in the model, `doubt` as a field for the invariants only, and `DoubtUnverified` in every instance; the window on drivers without `RENAME_NOREPLACE` documented, not narrowed.

## The scenarios counted in the coverage, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §27)

The maintainer asked for the coverage to go up ("augmente aussi le taux de coverage"); first, the measure counts what the scenarios run, TODO.md's item. Commit `daf132b`, merged by `7c9adc9`.

- **The measure** (`make cover`, `test/ci/cover.sh`): the unit tests as before (`bin/cover/cover.out`); then the scenarios against a conformance build made with `-cover -coverpkg=./...`, each run of it writing its counters to `bin/cover/scenarios/`, which `go tool covdata textfmt` turns into `scenarios.out`; and `merged.out`, a block run when either ran it. The table gives each package's statements and the share the unit tests run, the scenarios, and either, then the total and how long each part took; a table even when a part fails, then exit 1. The CI's `cover` job keeps the three profiles, `cover.html` and `merged.html`.
- **The runner** (`conformance/runner`): `--cover-dir` gives each run of the binary `GOCOVERDIR`, made absolute (the binary runs in its sandbox), after the runner's environment and before `ATOLL_CONFORMANCE` and `TZ`; without it nothing changes (`TestCoverDir`).
- **Files one build alone holds**: the statements are those of the delivered build, as the unit tests' profile lists them, so the unit tests' column stays the measure it was. `observer_conformance.go` (79 statements) is left out, and said; `observer_delivered.go` counts with what the unit tests ran.
- **Measured**, on `0cf4ba1`: unit tests 77.3 %, scenarios 47.4 %, merged 83.0 % (29,142 statements); `cmd/atoll` 50.8 %, 74.2 %, 86.5 %. The lowest merged: `internal/view/fusefs` 0 % and `readonly` 13 % (run only in qemu), `fsx` 71 %, `ops` and `trash` 77 %, `catalog` 78 %. Locally `make cover` took 21 min on the loaded machine; on GitHub the cover and conformance jobs take about 2 min each.
- **Tests**: `TestCoverDir`; `make cover`, `make conformance`, `go test ./conformance/...`, lint, shellcheck and actionlint pass.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the statements of the delivered build as the one denominator, the conformance build's own file left out; an explicit `--cover-dir` in the runner; one target, `make cover`, running both parts in turn; the table and the summary even when a part fails, then exit 1; `cover.html` kept for the unit tests alone and `merged.html` added; the timeout kept at 45 min; `CLAUDE.md`'s line on `make cover` updated.

## The mass-modification guard over a day, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §9, §15.6, §23, §24; threat model G5)

The maintainer's answer of 2026-11-20 ("Garde sur 24 h"): a second window, a day long, for the mass-modification guard. Commit `111b291`, merged by `2023a90`.

- **The key** (`schema/config.schema.json`, an addition at format version 1): `modifies.day_guard = { files = 1000, percent = "10%", window = "24h" }`, beside `mass_guard` and in its grammar; §24.1 and `deploy/atoll.toml` show it. It trips past 1,000 files or past 10 % of a disk's copies modified within the day, as `mass_guard` does past 100 or 5 % within 10 minutes.
- **The guard**: before each `modified` decision that makes copies stale, the window and the day are both asked, the window first, with the same `rows`. Past either, the decision is not applied and the guard trips as before: every later change of that disk is held with nothing read, the other copies keep the version before, until `atoll guard confirm` or `reject --kind modifications`, or the local API, answers. A trip on the day writes `window` `day` into its `modify-guard` event, and the answer to it carries it (§9). The day counts the disk's `modified` events within its window, without `answered`, after the last answer to a hold the day tripped; an answer to a hold of the window leaves the day's count as it was. Both counts come from the catalog's events at each pass and slice, so neither a restart nor a crash starts the day again; no migration.
- **Outputs**: `atoll guard list` and `/v1/guards` give `window: "day"` with the day's count and files, the text "… within a day"; `atoll daemon --until-idle` gives it on that guard, the text "… modified within a day" (French too); the field is left out otherwise, so every other output is as it was (`api/schemas/guard-list.schema.json`, `daemon.schema.json`). The alert `guard-modifications:<id>` says it was what a day changed.
- **Tests that failed first**: 12 config vectors and the 3 keys of the complete case; `TestTheModifyGuardReadsItsDay`, `TestGuardReadsUseAnIndex` (the day's answer through `events_by_kind`); `TestTheDayStopsWhatTheWindowLetsThrough`, `TestARestartKeepsTheDaysCount`, `TestTheDayCountsWhatACrashLeft` (kill -9 after a `modified`), `TestAnAnswerToTheWindowLeavesTheDay`, `TestADryRunTellsOfTheDay`, each failing with the day's check taken out; `TestTheDayForgetsWhatLeftIt`; `TestGuardListSaysTheDayTripped`; `TestDescribe`'s day case; the scenario `daemon-modify-guard-day` (one file an hour, the third held), failing at its third file without the check. The 63 scenarios and the benches that keep the window out of the way keep the day out too. `go test ./...`, race on `jobs`, `catalog`, `daemon` and `alerts` (`jobs` past the default 10 minutes on the loaded machine, 839 s with the CI's 45), `make conformance`, lint and `make model-check` pass. §9's fingerprint moved; `Replication.tla` is unchanged, as guards are a wait there.
- **Threat model**: G5 done.
- **Left**: the daemon that runs for good logs no guard line, as before (the alert and `held` say it); no warning for a `day_guard.window` shorter than `mass_guard.window`.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the key `modifies.day_guard`, with `window` configurable (24 h by default) so tests and the soak can keep it out of the way; the defaults 1,000 files and 10 %; events counted as the window counts them (a file modified twice counts twice), not distinct files; the day's count starting again only at an answer to a hold the day tripped; the window asked first, a decision that trips both being the window's; an answer holding for `mass_guard.window` whichever window tripped; `window` written only as `day`, absent otherwise; no off switch; the alert's key unchanged; no `window` in the output of `guard confirm` or `reject`.

## The pool's polkit rule, printed by atoll polkit-rule, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §15.13, §25, §26; threat model G22)

The maintainer's answer for G22 ("Commande qui l'imprime"). Commits `4741c96` and `906c789`, merged by `681977d`.

- **The command** (`cmd/atoll/polkit.go`): `atoll polkit-rule [--json]` prints, from the configuration alone, polkit's rule for the pool, which root installs as `/etc/polkit-1/rules.d/49-atoll-pool.rules`, read by polkitd before the `.deb`'s `50-atoll.rules`. For the account `atoll`, the start of each disk's mount unit is allowed (named as the remount names it, `daemon.MountUnit`, now exported), the start of any other mount unit is refused (`polkit.Result.NO`), and anything else is left to the rules after it. A draining disk is in; a failed disk is left out, and so is a disk whose unit name would pass 255 bytes, which the daemon mounts again itself (exit 4, partial success, the rule printed all the same). Each unit is a JavaScript string with its backslashes doubled, as systemd's `\x2d` would otherwise read as `-`, another mount point's unit. The rule is ASCII, ES5, in English whatever the language, and identical to `rule` in `--json` (`api/schemas/polkit-rule.schema.json`, an addition at format 1). No catalog, no disk, no lock. When systemd refuses a start, the daemon's log now also says to print the pool's rule again. `deploy/README.md` installs the rule with the command, through a `.new` file moved only when the command exits 0.
- **Tests**: the scenarios `polkit-rule`, `polkit-rule-left-out`, `polkit-rule-usage` and `polkit-rule-reads-no-disk`, which failed first; `TestPolkitRulePrintsTheRule`, `TestPolkitRuleLeavesOutAUnitWithNoName`, `TestPolkitRuleAllowsTheListAloneAndRefusesOtherMounts` and `TestJSStringOfEachMountUnit`. In qemu, `TestThePoolPolkitRuleUnderSystemd` (polkitd 126, systemd 257) on Linux 6.12 and 7.3: with the `.deb`'s rule alone, `atoll` may start both the pool unit, whose name holds `\x2d`, and a tmpfs of `/etc/fstab` whose unit is what an unescaped rule would name instead; with the printed rule, `pkcheck` says yes for the pool unit and no for the tmpfs and `home.mount`, `atoll`'s start of the tmpfs is refused, and the remount still goes through the disk's unit. `TestTheRemountUnderSystemd` passes beside it. `go test ./...`, race on `daemon` and `cmd/atoll`, `make conformance` (638 scenarios) and lint (default, conformance, loopdev) pass.
- **Left**: the job mode, G22's second half, as polkit does not see it; the rule printed again by hand after any change of disks; the package's removal leaves `49-atoll-pool.rules` (`deploy/README.md` says to remove it); polkitd's reload of a changed rule taken from polkit(8), not measured, as the test restarts polkit.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): every disk of the configuration but a failed one, draining and cache disks included; a disk whose unit name passes 255 bytes left out with exit 4, the rule printed all the same; no flag for another account, always `atoll`; the rule answers yes for the pool's units, so it works without the `.deb`'s rule too, and no only for the start of other `.mount` units, leaving other verbs, units and accounts to the rules after it; the rule in English and ASCII whatever the language; `--json` with a new schema; no reminder from `disk add`, `remove`, `replace` or `fail`, which cannot tell whether a pool rule is installed (the reminder is in the rule's header, `deploy/README.md`, and the daemon's log line when systemd refuses a start); the install through a `.new` file, so exit 1 or 4 installs nothing.

## Plex tried unclaimed, from its own host, 2026-11-20 (specs.md §21.5, §27)

The maintainer asked whether the Plex test could run on a test Plex without his account ("tu peux pas faire avec un plex de test sans mon compte"). The Plex test of `test/mediaservers` ran only with `PLEX_CLAIM`, a claim token of his account, and was skipped without it; it now runs with no account and no token. Commits `6df2c40`, `576b797` and `3cb78ec`, merged by `8f8d520`.

- **Measured**, with `plexinc/pms-docker:latest`, Plex 1.43.5.11079, unclaimed and with no token:
  - **From another host of its Docker network**: `GET /library/sections` and `GET /:/prefs` answer 401, with `ALLOWED_NETWORKS` set to the private ranges or not; Plex's log reads the request as "Allowed Network (Subnet)" and answers 401 all the same.
  - **From its own network namespace** ("Loopback"): every request the test makes answers 2xx, a library made with `tv.plex.agents.none` and the scanner Plex Movie included (201), `refresh` and `refresh?path=` too. A library made while `/identity` still says `startingPlugins` answers 400, which the test's wait for an empty start state avoids.
  - **The scan by hand still counts**: in a library made empty, a partial scan of a new movie's folder, then one of the library's folder, found nothing in 30 s each; a scan of the whole library found it in 2 s, and a partial scan of a second movie's folder then found it in 4 s.
- **`run.sh`**: without `PLEX_CLAIM`, Plex starts unclaimed, with no `ALLOWED_NETWORKS`. The test runs in its network namespace (`--network container:`) and reaches it at `http://127.0.0.1:32400` with no token file; Jellyfin and Emby are reached by name, as before. With `PLEX_CLAIM`, nothing changes. Its cleanup now removes the volumes the images declare (`docker rm -v`), which each run had left dangling, a server's whole configuration among them.
- **The test**: scans the library once by hand, as Scan Library Files does, and waits for its seed before the notifications, as §21.5 says. Without a token, a 401 now fails the test where it skipped it, so the weekly run says when Plex stops taking its own host's requests.
- **Plex's image talks to plex.tv on its own**, unclaimed: at start it calls `servers.plex.tv` with an empty token (privacy, release channels, features every 5 minutes, and `POST /devices/<id>/unclaimed` with its private Docker address), and its crash uploader runs. The test sends nothing there; the weekly workflow now runs Plex with no secret, and so registers an unclaimed device with plex.tv each week.
- **Tests**: the Plex test passed twice in a row on `6df2c40`, listing the movie once told of it, at its new name alone after the rename, and no more after the deletion, every request in Plex's log from 127.0.0.1 with no token; all three servers passed on `3cb78ec` (Plex, Jellyfin 12.4.0, Emby 4.10.2), then Plex again alone; no volume, container or network left behind; lint with the build tag, `go vet` and shellcheck pass.
- **Docs**: `specs.md` §21.5's measured line and §27, `test/mediaservers/doc.go`, the comments of `run.sh` and of `.github/workflows/mediaservers.yml`, whose weekly run now tries Plex.
- **Left**: the claimed path has never run, nor with it the `allowed-network` subtest; whether a claimed Plex takes its own host's requests with no token is not measured; the maintainer's real Plex, its libraries on the merged view, is still to try.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-22** ("Tout confirmer"): the unclaimed server tried from its own host as the default, the claimed path kept behind `PLEX_CLAIM`; no `ALLOWED_NETWORKS` on the unclaimed server; a 401 failing the test rather than skipping it; the scan of the whole library by hand in the test before the notifications; `docker rm -v` in the cleanup.

## The maintainer's answers of 2026-11-20 (specs.md §10, §14.2, §15.6, §15.12, §21.5, §26; threat model G5, G22)

He was asked the choices of 2026-11-19 made without him and the decisions TODO.md still held, eleven questions in three rounds; he took the option put first each time.

- **Confirmed**: the choices of the eighteen entries of 2026-11-19 that listed some to confirm, each saying so now: ten of the core, nine together ("Tout confirmer"), the sample configuration outside `/etc` among them ("Garder /usr/share/doc"), and the device that left, `diskseq` first ("Confirmer"); eight of what is experimental ("Tout confirmer"), the view's strict check without `/proc`, root included, among them ("Confirmer, strict"). So does the bound of a walk's depth that TODO.md listed apart.
- **Decided, to do** (TODO.md, each with a test that fails first):
  - **Flaw 1's limit** ("Les deux"): the job records the change time right after the rename, in a commit of its own, and a reprise that finds no such record and another change time than the verified one records the copy unverified.
  - **A hibernated NTFS volume** ("Oui, readonly"): `readonly` on every driver, with a new reason, when the mount check finds a `hiberfil.sys` at the top under 4 KiB or whose first 4 KiB are not all zeros.

## An idle disk systemd unmounted itself, mounted again when its device left, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §15.13)

The maintainer's answer to the finding of the remount through systemd ("Yes, if its device left"): count `not-mounted` as a USB reset when the device the disk was mounted from left. Commit `f482efe`.

- **Why**: with udev, systemd unmounts an idle `/etc/fstab` mount within a second of its device going, and the check then found the disk `not-mounted`, no reason a USB reset gives: an idle USB disk was never mounted again, unless a pass's confirmation happened to see it first.
- **The rule** (`disks.DeviceLeft`, `conformance/vectors/device_left.json`, 12 cases): each check that finds a disk mounted keeps the device the mount table names and what that name leads to, a block device, its numbers, and the kernel's `diskseq` of its disk (its disk's for a partition), which a disk plugged again gets anew (`fsx.DeviceNodeOf`, a name under `/dev` and a file of `/sys`, no disk woken). Found `not-mounted` after that, the disk went for a USB reset when the name leads nowhere, to no block device, to other numbers, or to the same numbers with another `diskseq`, both known; the log says so, and the remount follows as for the other reasons. Its device where it was, as after an administrator's `umount`, the disk stays `missing` and nothing is tried; so too for a disk not mounted when the daemon started, a machine that cannot say (the conformance build), and a name that cannot be read. The same holds when a pass's confirmation finds it first.
- **Tests that failed first**: `TestDeviceLeftVectors`, `TestACheckGivesTheSourceOfADiskFound`, `TestDiskSeqIn`, `TestDeviceNodeOf`, `TestADiskNoLongerMountedIsResetWhenItsDeviceLeft` (seven cases through `check`), and in qemu `TestAnIdleDiskUnpluggedUnderSystemd`: ext4 and ntfs-3g mounted by systemd from `/etc/fstab` with udev's rules, their device renamed away, systemd unmounts the idle disk, the daemon finds it `not-mounted`, says its device left, waits while it is away, and has systemd mount it again once it is back; on Linux 6.12 and 7.3, beside `TestTheRemountUnderSystemd`. `go test ./...`, race on `daemon`, `disks` and `fsx`, `make conformance` (634 scenarios) and lint pass; GitHub's run `44601968714` is green in every job.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Confirmer"): `diskseq` as what tells a disk plugged anew under the same name and numbers, and a sequence number unknown on either side read as no departure; the log line at `WARN`; a name that cannot be read is no departure, said at `WARN`.

## The remount after a USB reset through systemd, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §15.13, §26)

The maintainer's answer: have systemd mount the disk again ("Remonter via systemd"), so that an ntfs-3g disk outlives a stop of the daemon. Commits `25cf996`, `deb08b6`, `aaa2baa` and `1c103dc`, merged by `63fc671`.

- **The remount** (`internal/daemon/systemd.go`): `umount -l` as before; then, where systemd is PID 1, `/usr/bin/systemctl --no-block --no-ask-password start <unit>` on the unit of the disk's `/etc/fstab` entry, named as `systemd-escape --path --suffix=mount` names it (`conformance/vectors/mount_unit.json`, 24 cases from systemd 257), and a wait for its job, reading `systemctl show`. ntfs-3g's FUSE process belongs to the mount unit, as at boot. Before the start the daemon waits up to 10 s for systemd to see the unmount, and starts nothing while the unit's device is missing, so the loop is never held for systemd's 90 s device timeout. A unit that failed is not mounted by hand after it. The daemon mounts the disk itself, as before, saying why once a run for each reason, where systemd is not PID 1, the table is not `/etc/fstab`, the unit's name would pass 255 bytes, systemd has no unit, `systemctl` cannot reach it, or systemd refuses.
- **polkit**: `deploy/50-atoll.rules` lets the account `atoll` start a `.mount` unit, and nothing else. The `.deb` installs it in `/usr/share/polkit-1/rules.d/` and recommends polkitd; the archive carries it. pkcheck on polkitd 122 (Debian 12) and 126 (Debian 13): start of a mount unit allowed; stop, restart, reload, a service, an automount, a template, another account, and systemd's other actions refused. The snapshot `.deb` installed on Debian 12 and 13, its rule read. `TestThePolkitRuleLetsAtollStartAMountUnitAlone` holds the rule. No setting of the units changed: systemctl's whole D-Bus conversation ran under the call filter set to kill, and nothing was killed.
- **Measured in qemu**, Linux 6.12 and 7.3, systemd 257 with dbus, polkit and udev (`TestTheRemountUnderSystemd`): ntfs-3g and ext4 remounted through their unit, ntfs-3g's FUSE process in the unit's cgroup, each still mounted and readable after a stop, a start and a restart of `atoll.service`; `atoll` may not stop the unit; without the rule, the daemon mounts the disk itself, and a stop takes the ntfs-3g disk with it, as before. `TestMountUnitNamesAreSystemds` compares the vectors with the guest's `systemd-escape`. The whole of `test/loopdev`, both runs, on both kernels: exit 0. On the branch, `go test -race -timeout 45m ./...`, `make conformance` (634 scenarios), lint, `go test ./conformance/models`, `goreleaser check` and shellcheck pass. §10 is unchanged, so neither the TLA+ model nor a fingerprint moved.
- **Threat model** (`docs/threat-model.md`): §8 "Elevation through polkit": code running as `atoll` may now start any mount unit of the machine, which widens little beyond `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, within what root configured (the mount helpers run as root outside the call filter, with root's device, mount point and options; a unit's start also starts what it requires or wants; polkit does not see the job mode). §4: a remounted ntfs-3g volume is now parsed as root, as at boot. `systemctl` by absolute path (G19). New low gap G22: a rule naming the pool's units alone, which `deploy/README.md` shows how to write.
- **GitHub's CI**: run `44579171805` on the merge failed in qemu, `TestTheRemountUnderSystemd` on both kernels: with KVM the disk showed mounted again before the daemon had seen the unit's job end and said so, the test read its log at once, found no line through the unit, and its stop cut the daemon's wait short. The test now waits for the daemon's line (`9b804e4`), passed in qemu on Linux 6.12 and 7.3, and run `44586241616` on it is green in every job.
- **Found**: with udev, systemd itself unmounts an idle fstab mount within 1 s of its device going (`StopPropagatedFrom=`, measured in qemu); the disk then reads `not-mounted`, which is no reset reason, so it is remounted only if a pass recorded `io-error` or `device-gone` first. A busy one stays a dead mount, reads `device-gone`, and is remounted through its unit once the device is back (measured). A decision in TODO.md.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): `umount -l` stays the daemon's own, only the mount goes through systemd, and the rule grants `start` alone (a unit's stop runs a non-lazy umount, which fails on a busy dead mount); one static rule for any `.mount` unit, with a narrowing rule documented and G22 (polkit cannot read Atoll's configuration, and a list `atoll` could write would let it widen its own rule); a JavaScript rule alone, no `.pkla`; polkitd among the `.deb`'s Recommends; any loaded unit with a `FragmentPath` used, `SourcePath=/etc/fstab` not required; the waits (the device checked before the start, 10 s for systemd to see the unmount, `--no-block` and polling so a refusal is told from a failure without reading messages, no `mount` after a failed unit); the log levels, once a run for each reason; `/usr/bin/systemctl`, and no `After=dbus.service` in the units.

## ntfs-3g's creation time read through a handle, a regression of flaw 1's fix, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §7, §14.2)

GitHub's qemu job, on the merged tree, failed on ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g: a pass found metadata on copies nothing had changed (`TestAFailedMountIsAMissingDisk`), and a conflict where a copy was to be refreshed (`TestAPassRefreshesOnEachDriver`), a modification of the user's read as both sides changed. Flaw 1's fix records a new copy's row from what its part's handle gave when verified; a handle's `Info` read no creation time where `statx` gives none, while a look by name reads it from `system.ntfs_crtime`, so the row and every later look differed, the change time the rename moved was not taken, and the copy read as edited. Measured in qemu with both looks printed: the part's had no birth, the name's had one. File, writable file and folder handles now read the attribute through `/proc`, as a look by name does (`fsx.statxBirth`); both tests pass on both FUSE drivers. Commit `405ea26`, which also gives `make test-race` 45 minutes, as `internal/jobs` passed the 10 of `go test` on GitHub's runners. GitHub's run `44556740018` on it: every job green, the qemu job's second run under systemd included.

## The threat model's G18: the systemd units hardened, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §10, §15.13, §26)

The maintainer's answer: harden the units, the group `disk` included, each setting measured in qemu. Commits `3bad015`, `c8e4b2c`, `689d52b` and `5d529ba`, merged by `5d5cb40`.

- **The units** (`deploy/atoll.service`, `deploy/atoll-mount.service`) set `SystemCallArchitectures=native`, `SystemCallFilter=@system-service @mount`, and `fanotify_init fanotify_mark open_by_handle_at` for the daemon, with `SystemCallErrorNumber=EPERM`, `RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6 AF_NETLINK`, `RestrictNamespaces`, `LockPersonality`, `MemoryDenyWriteExecute`, `RestrictRealtime`, `ProtectClock`, `ProtectHostname` and `UMask=0027`, `NoNewPrivileges` kept. **No supplementary group**: the group `disk` gave nothing `CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH` and `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` did not, measured, so no udev rule replaces it. **`CAP_FSETID` added**: without it a copied 2775 folder came out 0775, and so did one made through the view. Left out, each with its reason in the unit: `RestrictSUIDSGID` (with it nothing of a setgid folder was copied), `DevicePolicy`/`DeviceAllow` (the remount opens the device under whatever number it comes back with), and every setting that gives a mount namespace.
- **Measured under systemd**: qemu's guest has a second run with systemd 257 as PID 1 (`test/qemu/units`, `SYSTEMD_RUN`, on by default, the CI's too), where `TestTheDaemonUnderSystemd` (ext4 on a partition of a SCSI disk through device-mapper, and ntfs-3g) and `TestTheViewUnderSystemd` start both units as they ship, a call outside the filter killing the process, on Linux 6.12 and 7.3: ready, copies through fanotify, setgid kept, the socket, a reload, mergerfs's branches, a remount through `/etc/fstab` after a device-mapper rename, `SG_IO` on the whole disk, smartctl, both views mounted for the host, a stop with 0, each in the host's mount namespace with no device policy. `systemd-analyze security`: 7.3 to 4.4 and 7.1 to 4.2. `TestTheUnitsAreHardened` holds the settings.
- **Found**: an ntfs-3g disk the daemon remounted is unmounted when the daemon stops or restarts, as its FUSE process belongs to the service's cgroup (ext4 stays): written in §15.13 and `deploy/README.md`, not fixed (a decision in TODO.md). `deploy/README.md` said `/etc/atoll` belongs to `atoll`; it is root's.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"), but for a folder made with no model, which is to take 0755 whatever the umask ("Forcer 0755", TODO.md): no udev rule; `CAP_FSETID` added; `SystemCallErrorNumber=EPERM` in the units, `kill` in the tests; `UMask=0027`, so a folder §15.12 makes with no model on any disk is 0750 and `atoll`'s; the guest's second run on by default; `ProtectClock` allowed, measured to make no mount namespace.

## The two flaws TLC found, fixed, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §9, §13.4, §14, §14.1, §14.2, §15.12, §17.2)

The maintainer approved the remedies ("je ok avec tes remèdes de failles"). Commits `2441e45` and `eaa8f82`, merged by `bfc584e`.

- **Flaw 1: a new copy is recorded as its part was when verified.** A user's write into a new copy once its name was in the library, before its row was written, or while Atoll was down after a crash at `source-synced`, was recorded as the copy itself, verified against the version before: no look saw it, and a scrub would have repaired it away. The job now records with its whole-file hash what the part was, size, times, mode, owner and links (`part_look` in its payload); the row takes that, but for the change time the rename moved, which it takes from a look right after the rename, or the reprise's, only when nothing else moved (`ops.CreatedRow`, `conformance/vectors/create_row.json`). A write since reads as an edit, modified, or metadata for a `chmod`. Tests that failed first: `TestAWriteIntoANewCopyBeforeItsRowIsAnEdit`, `…WhileAtollIsDown`, `…OnADiskLostAfterItsRename`, `TestAChmodOfANewCopyWhileAtollIsDown`, and the scenario `replica-edited-before-commit.json`, now among the scenarios.
- **Flaw 2: a running job is kept until its reprise, and its file's decisions wait** (§14.1 everywhere). A copy whose operation was left planned on a disk lost had its job dropped while still running, by the plan, which cancelled a pending copy on a missing disk, or by a decision Gone; back, the reprise finished the copy with no job, and a file the user had deleted came back, or a copy of the version before counted at the file's level. The plan is told which pending copies have their job running (`Copy.Running`, `plan.json`): it counts each as an `ok` copy there, holds no room for it, and never cancels it. Every decision of the file waits, a rename included (`PropFile.Running`, `propagation.json`), as its modifications did; nothing else takes the job away (`dropFileIfEmpty`, a modification). Tests that failed first: `TestAFileWithAJobRunningWaitsForItsDeletion`, `TestAPendingCopyWithItsJobRunningIsKept`, `…WaitsForItsRename`, each through kill -9 and through EIO, `TestARunningJobKeepsItsPendingCopy`, and the scenarios `replica-of-deleted-file.json` and `replica-of-old-version.json`. rapid draws `Running` too: a running copy is never cancelled.
- **The model**: `Fixes` is empty and every instance passes, the flaws' included (3,657, 6,982 and 72,029 states); `make model-flaws` says no flaw is open. Fingerprints follow §9, §13.4, §14, §14.1, §14.2, §15.12 and §17.2.
- **Contract additions**: the job payload's `part_look`; vector fields `running` in `plan.json` and `propagation.json`; `create_row.json`. No migration.
- **Left, listed by the fix**: a write that keeps size, times, mode and owner moves only the change time, which the row then takes (right after the rename the width of a `stat`, in a reprise the whole time Atoll was down; §14.2 says so); a file renamed while a job runs reappears at its old name on that disk once the reprise finishes the copy, as before; `atoll scan` and `conflicts resolve` still touch a running job; `restore --to` records the look after its syncs, an unverified user copy.

## The view checks the disk it writes, and is tested as another account, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §21.2, §27; threat model G2, G3)

The maintainer's answers to G2 and G3 of `docs/threat-model.md`, each the option put first. Commits `bf865af` and `3af2f36`, merged by `d5f639d`.

- **G2.** The view that writes had the kernel check a caller against the catalog, then wrote with `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`, so a `chmod` or `chown` made directly on a disk was not enforced until a pass recorded it. Before it journals anything, the view now looks at what each operation acts on, on the disk it acts on, and at the folder of a name there; where the mode, owner or group differ from what it presents, it checks the caller again as Linux would there, and answers `EACCES` or `EPERM`: an open to write, a create, a folder or symbolic link made, a hard link, an unlink, an rmdir, a rename, a change of attributes, and the extended attributes. It wakes no other disk, and where the disk holds what is presented, nothing more is read. The pure check is `view.Access`, with 65 vectors in `view_access.json`; the caller's other groups and capabilities, which FUSE does not give, come from `/proc` (`proc_status.json`), and without them the caller is checked as strictly as possible. Three unit tests of the writer fail without the check.
- **G3.** `TestViewAsAnotherAccount` writes through a real mount as another account, by `setpriv`: the kernel's refusals each come back as Linux gives them (a create in a folder of root's, an unlink in a sticky folder, a `chmod` and a `chown` of another's file, setuid by a non-owner, a hard link `fs.protected_hardlinks` refuses); a setuid file written by a non-owner loses the bit on its disk; and G2's cases, a file 0600 and a folder 0755 made so on their disks, are refused before any pass. Passed in qemu on Linux 6.12, with the view's other tests on real disks.
- **Found, not fixed**: a file or folder the view makes takes the caller's group, not the group of a setgid folder above it, as Linux gives it; a write by a non-owner to a setuid file whose presented mode differs from its disk's in other bits fails with `EPERM` until a pass.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"; the caller checked strictly without `/proc`, root included: "Confirmer, strict"): no look at the disk when it holds what is presented; groups and capabilities from `/proc`, capabilities counted only in the view's user namespace, and without `/proc` (`hidepid`) the caller checked by its account and group alone, root included; only the folder that exists on the disk reached is checked, folders made on the way taking what is presented; a new name a stricter folder refuses is refused, not placed on another disk; the view's root folder checked as any; a write to a file setuid on its disk alone refused for a caller without `CAP_FSETID`; a `chmod` that only takes setuid or setgid off never refused; `fs.protected_hardlinks` taken as on when unreadable; an operation on several disks refused when any refuses; times set to now checked as `touch`, others need the owner; ACLs not checked (G14).

## The threat model's G1, G4, G5 and G6, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §17, §23, §24, §25.1, §26)

The maintainer's answers to the threat model's medium gaps (`docs/threat-model.md`), each the option put first but for G4, where he took the SMTP key too. Commits `e9cb86a`, `f37a233`, `da6c837`, `4e4015f` and `051a752`, merged by `2f134e5`.

- **G1, the token file of an answer to a guard.** Over TCP an answer needs a token file of root or the daemon's account, with no `g+w`, `o+r` or `o+w`, in folders, its own and each symbolic link's on the way, of root or that account, with no `o+w` even sticky, and `g+w` only for root's group or the daemon's. The 403 gives each reason with the `chmod` or `chown` that mends it, and the refusal is logged; questions and dry runs are unchanged. `fsx.ReadSecretFile` refuses what is not a regular file. Tests: `TestTheAPIAnswerTakesATokenOnlyItsAccountsCanSet` (14 cases), `TestTheAPIAnswerLooksAtTheFoldersOfALink`, `TestTokenDoubts` (15).
- **G4, the secrets.** A §24.4 warning at each of the eight keys that name a secret file, for a file other accounts may read or write, or that belongs to none of Atoll's accounts (root, the one running, the catalog's owner): `rules check` shows them, and the daemon now logs its configuration's warnings at its start and at each reload. Every secret file is read with a limit, 4 KiB, 16 KiB for OTLP's headers. A warning for a secret sent over `http://` to a host that is not this machine: a media server or Gotify with its token, ntfy always (its topic is its secret), any address with a user. A new key, `alerts.smtp_require_tls`, false by default so nothing changes: true, a mail goes only over TLS, or is not sent and the log says why. 33 configuration vectors, 2 scenarios, `TestSMTPRequiresTLSWhenAsked`.
- **G5, versions purged early.** A new alert, `versions-purged:<id>`, priority 4: versions a modification replaced (`overwritten`) that the daemon's purge took for room before their retention ended; it names ten paths and holds until a day passes with none more. Counted in the daemon's memory. Nothing else in the purge changed. Tests: `TestVersionsPurgedEarlyAreCounted`, `TestTheDaemonTellsOfVersionsPurgedEarly`.
- **G6, the API's limits.** A `ReadTimeout` of 15 s on the socket, `api.listen` and `metrics.listen`; each listener holds 32 connections, through Atoll's own listener and no new dependency, the next ones waiting unaccepted; an unreadable token logged once a minute at most; `/v1/disks` checks mounts one at a time, and `/v1/disks/{id}` takes the catalog's turn first. Tests: `TestASlowClientTimesOut`, `TestTheConnectionsAreCapped`, `TestTheAPIWaitsPastItsConnections`, `TestAnUnreadableTokenIsLoggedOnceAMinute`, `TestTheAPIChecksTheMountsOneAnswerAtATime`.
- **Contract additions**: the key `alerts.smtp_require_tls`; the alert key `versions-purged:<id>` (and in `atoll_alert`); §24.4 warnings; the 403's cases and text, and the 503's description.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): G1 checks the token file's folder and each link's, not the folders above; any group may still read the token file, so its members can answer; a folder that cannot be looked at gives a 403; secrets read only from regular files, everywhere; G4 warns of files others may write too; Atoll's accounts are root, the one running and the catalog's owner; the daemon logs every §24.4 warning at start and reload, the later phases' too; "this machine" is `localhost`, 127.0.0.0/8 and `[::1]` as written, no name resolved, so a Tailscale address warns; ntfy over `http://` warns even with no token; the key is `alerts.smtp_require_tls`, in the style of `[alerts]`, not `smtp.require_tls`; G5 counted in memory, a restart starting from zero; 32 connections a listener, the next ones waiting rather than refused; `/v1/disks/{id}` serialized for its checks and the catalog.

## The core first, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §14, §28)

The maintainer: "je veux un truc bullet proof (très fiable et très secure) avec peu de features dans un premier temps; on ajoutera les features une fois le noyau rock solid". Commit `1bc014f`.

- **One copy at a time by default**, on every disk: `replication.max_parallel_per_ssd` takes 1, where it took 4 that morning. Copies at once stay as they were built, to turn on knowingly, until the TLA+ model covers them and a soak on a real SSD has run them.
