The tests of copies at once ask for four (`ssdAtOnce`), so that they still test concurrency; the configuration's vectors and `TestCopyLimits` say 1. The model runs one copy at a time, so it is unchanged; §14's fingerprint follows its new text.
- **Experimental, off by default and no promise of the first release** (§28, README.md, ROADMAP.md): Atoll's own view, writing and read only, with its hard links, extended attributes and copies written in parallel; the SSD cache; the UPS through NUT; the link guard; copies at once; the export over OTLP; `balance.equalize = "percent"`; the kernel's type `ntfs`.
- **Versions**: tags plain Semantic Versioning, `v0.1.0`, `v0.1.1`… while the core is proven, `v1.0.0` once it is declared solid, no suffix `-beta`; the `.deb` with a Debian revision, `0.1.0-1` (`release: "1"` in `.goreleaser.yaml`'s nfpms), as Debian's policy gives a package that is not native. The first release, `v0.1.0`, once the core is proven.

## The soak after the merges, cut short by Docker after 3 h 30, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §26)

`TestDaemonSoak` on `af340fd`, every feature of the day merged, in qemu without KVM on Linux 6.12, `SOAK=72h SOAK_FILES=200000`, from 07:03. Docker Desktop's VM stopped at 10:32, a minute before Windows installed an update of the Claude application, and the soak with it; the maintainer, back, had asked it stopped then. Its log is `bin/soak4/soak.log`.

- **No fault**: 271 passes, none held nor partial, no error, and the one expected warning, no `smartctl` in the guest.
- **The first pass** peaked at 182.1 MiB, the Go runtime holding at most 158.5 MiB and the heap 135.1 MiB live, for 180.5 MiB on `ab9e21f`: under the 200 MB (190.7 MiB), the day's features and the OpenTelemetry SDK's larger binary costing some 1.6 MiB there. No tenth of the 72 hours was reached, so no reading of memory over time.
- **The passes**: 6.9 s at the median, 7.2 on average, as before the merges, and 0.54 hours busy of 3.33.

## The CI green on GitHub, main protected, BLAKE3 measured on real hardware, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §27, §29.7)

The first runs of GitHub Actions on `beta`, after the merges of the day. Two faults of the CI itself, fixed in `7ff4ae0`: git refused the checkout in the container jobs, which stopped `go build` from stamping the version (`GIT_CONFIG_*` set `safe.directory`); govulncheck found GO-2026-6610 to 6617 in `golang.org/x/net` v0.59.0, which the OpenTelemetry exporters bring, taken to v0.60.0 at once. Then the loop-device tests, in qemu with KVM (25 minutes for both kernels, where it took hours without), which no one had run on the day's features:

- four failures were the tests' own: files of copies at once that did not fit the bench's volume, an ambiguous column in a test's query, a prune past PATH_MAX that needs `walk.long_paths = "walk"` since the bound on depth, the name corpus on the type `ntfs` under `errors=remount-ro`;
- **one was a finding on the type `ntfs`**: without `windows_names`, a name holding `" * < > ?` can be made there, and once its file is closed the driver errs and, under `errors=remount-ro`, sets the whole volume read-only; with `windows_names` the creation is refused (`TestAForbiddenNameOnTheTypeNTFS`, §22, a decision in TODO.md);
- **one was a bug of the view's copies written in parallel**, on ntfs-3g: the part adopted as a replica on the other disk, with a time of creation where ntfs-3g gives none, became the original, and the view's create waited for the catalog to present the copy on its own disk, for good, holding back every later operation at that path. Taken in now once the catalog holds the copy the view wrote in sync at the size and time a look finds (`ca8fee9`, `TestAFileTakenInWhenItsOriginalMovedToAnotherCopy`, §21.2).

Run `44491299109` on `ca8fee9`: every job green, `race`, `integration`, `conformance`, `lint`, `govulncheck`, `qemu`, `arm64`, `tla`, `cover`, `blake3-amd64`. **`main` is protected** as the maintainer chose: a merge needs `race`, `integration`, `conformance`, `lint`, `govulncheck`, `qemu`, `arm64` and `tla` green; no force push, no deletion; an administrator may pass over it.

**BLAKE3 on real hardware** (`docs/research/blake3.md`), the vectors passing on both: arm64 Neoverse-N2 with SVE2, 909 to 916 MB/s for a read feeding both hashes and 1,934 MB/s for the chunks' alone; x86-64 AMD EPYC 9V45 with AVX-512, 2,329 to 2,443 and 5,436 to 5,518 MB/s. The item of TODO.md "Hardware not yet measured" is closed.

## The TLA+ model, checked by TLC, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §6.1, §9, §10, §13.4, §14, §14.1, §14.2, §15.4, §15.5, §15.10, §15.12, §17.1, §17.2, §20, §27, §29.4)

The maintainer chose formal verification of the most dangerous mechanism, the first gap of the test map: TLA+ checked by TLC, Java in a container only ("TLA+ et TLC"), and the model kept beside specs.md with a fingerprint against drift. Commits `45457cd`, `cdc7b77`, `b2c0121` and `c17472d`, merged by `fb3ca0a`.

- **The model** (`conformance/models/Replication.tla`): copies made, propagated and sent to the trash, step by step, each action citing its section of specs.md, with Atoll killed, the power cut, a disk lost or left an empty mount point and mounted again, and a user modifying, replacing, deleting or creating, between any two steps. Invariants by CLAUDE.md's numbers 1, 4, 7, 10, 11, 12 and 13, plus no user data lost, a replica in sync, and a liveness property.
- **The instances**: six that expect a pass, up to 2.5 million distinct states and a minute each with 2 workers; three that reproduce the flaws below; about six minutes in all (`make model-check`, `test/tla/run.sh` in `eclipse-temurin:21.0.12_8-jre-noble` with tla2tools 1.7.4, its SHA-256 checked; a job `tla` in the CI and in `test/ci/run.sh`).
- **Two flaws of specs.md, in the code too**, each replayed on the conformance build, each with a scenario in `conformance/models/flaws/` that fails until its remedy, which is the maintainer's to choose (`conformance/models/README.md`):
  - **A write into a new replica before its row is written is recorded as verified** (§14.2, §17.2): a copy at `source-synced`, at its name with every sync made and its row not written, that a user writes in place (directly, or through mergerfs whose first branch is that disk) is committed `ok`, verified against the version before, with the size and times of the new one; no look sees the edit, and a scrub then finds the copy `corrupt` and repairs the user's edit away to the trash. A crash at `source-synced` stretches the window to the whole time the daemon is down. Proposed: the row takes what the part was when verified (inode, size, time before the rename), so a later write reads as `edited`.
  - **A job running is dropped while its operation is planned** (§13.4, §14.1, §15.12): the plan cancels a pending copy on a `missing` disk with its job, whatever its state, and a decision `gone` deletes the file and its jobs by cascade; when the disk comes back, the reprise finishes the copy with no job. So a file the user deleted comes back, with no trash, read as a user's new file; or a copy of the version before is recorded `ok` and counted at the file's level. Proposed: §14.1 everywhere, a pending copy whose job runs kept and counted, and a file with a job running waiting for its decisions; or a reprise that sends such a copy to the trash.
- **Five modelling mistakes** TLC's first traces showed, fixed; seven of nine bad designs put into the model one at a time caught by the invariant they break.
- **Fingerprints** (`conformance/models/fingerprints.json`): the SHA-256 of the fourteen sections the model formalizes; a test fails when one changes, saying to reread the model, and when the model cites none of them; a rule in CLAUDE.md; a pointer from each section to the model.
- **Statecharts** (`conformance/models/states.md`, Mermaid, from `states.json`) of the disk and copy states, each transition with its section, kept to the schema's lists and §4 by a test, with the bugs of DONE.md their transitions caused and the two flaws. Found: the copy state `unavailable` is entered by no transition of specs.md and written by no code.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): the passing instances assume the two remedies proposed; the flaw scenarios kept out of `conformance/scenarios/` until a remedy; tla2tools 1.7.4 rather than the 1.8.0 prerelease; a section's fingerprint holding its subsections.

## The gaps of the test map, 2 to 5, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §26, §27)

The maintainer asked for the gaps of `testing-map.svg` to be closed in the order of their value ("ok fait par ordre de valeur 1 2 3 4 5"); the first, formal verification, is a TLA+ model of its own. Commits `578c3ff`, `49c5510`, `91aaf39` and `9a412b9`, merged by `fac78bb`.

- **Mutation tests** (gap 2): `test/ci/mutation.sh` runs gremlins v0.6.0 (Apache-2.0, a tool installed at that version, never a module dependency) on the pure core, `glob`, `value`, `names`, `rules`, `config`, the hashing of `integrity` and `solver`, every operator on, generated code left out; each package's score and the mutants that lived go to the job summary. `make mutation PKG=…`; `.github/workflows/mutation.yml` each night on `beta`, and on demand. No gate yet: proposed once the first nights are read, an efficacy of 95 % and a mutant coverage of 85 % a pure package, each mutant that lives killed by a test or named equivalent. Measured: `glob` 164 killed, none lived, 6 not covered; `value` 235, none, 24 before, then 245, none, 14 once `Percent.Exceeded` (used by the mass guards, `trash.max_size` and `cache.max_fill`; a new field `exceeded` in `percent.json`) and `Listen.String` had tests of their own. The rest are conditions of a `case`, which gremlins cannot reach.
- **Coverage** (gap 3): `make cover` makes one profile of the module with `-coverpkg=./...`, and `test/ci/cover.sh` gives the table by package and in total, and HTML; the CI's `cover` job writes the table to the run's summary and keeps the profile 30 days. No threshold. Unit tests only.
- **Fuzzing and property-based tests** (gap 4): 16 new fuzz targets, 19 in all, each against a reference or a round trip and seeded from the vectors: the value grammars (durations, sizes, percentages, windows, `listen`, NUT, `min_free`), the configuration's load and its edits by `disk add`, `remove` and `fail`, mountinfo, a forged `disk.toml`, the reprises of a repair and of an operation, and `path`/`path_b64`. `test/ci/fuzz.sh`, `make fuzz`, and `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml` each night, 5 minutes a target, the corpus kept from night to night, a crasher kept 90 days. `pgregory.net/rapid` v1.3.0 (MPL-2.0, a test dependency) checks the solver's plan properties, the capacity of §13.1, and a state machine of writes, deletions, a disk lost and back, passes and prunes against a model that counts copies on its own; fixed seeds in `go test`, a new seed and 100,000 checks each night. Four mutations of the solver made by hand, each caught. Found: `ParseNUT` keeps brackets in a host of two colons or more written without them (`0@::]`), harmless, and the grammar frozen at version 1.
- **The threat model** (gap 5): `docs/threat-model.md`, not a contract: the assets, six adversaries, ten entry points each by STRIDE with what holds it and what it leaves, read in the code, and 21 gaps, none fixed: 7 medium, a token file other accounts can write (the 403 checks only that others can read it), the view enforcing the catalog's mode and owner while writing with the right to override them, no test writing through the view as another account, other secret files with no check of mode or size and tokens that may go over `http://`, a slow rewrite under the mass-modification guard's threshold, no read timeout nor cap of connections on the API, no seccomp or address-family limits in the units; and 14 low. A pointer from §26.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): gremlins rather than go-mutesting, every operator on, one job a night; coverage a job of its own without `-race`, not in `test/ci/run.sh`; the field `exceeded` of `percent.json`; 5 minutes a fuzz target each night, crashers never committed by themselves; fixed seeds in `go test`, new ones at night; a `nolint:depguard` on `net/netip` in a fuzz test, which reads with it; the NUT round trip skipping hosts with brackets rather than narrowing the frozen grammar; the threat model's priorities, none high.

## Hard links through Atoll's own view, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §14, §15.1, §21.2)

The maintainer asked for Atoll's own view to be done before the next soak ("la vue atoll à faire avant le prochain soak"), hard links among it, as Sonarr and Radarr hard-link a download into the library and got `EXDEV`, copying and doubling the space for the time of a transfer. Commits `090b636` and `5f09774`, merged by `3cc31b4`. Vectors first: 11 cases in `view_resolve.json`; a scenario, `daemon-hard-link-replicated.json`.

- **Where**: on the disk the view presents the file from, its original, at the new path there, after the folders that disk lacks, made as for what is new (`ops.Disk.LinkUser`). A folder answers `EPERM`; `EXDEV`, so that the program copies as before, for a symbolic link, a disk the view may not write to, one mounted with the kernel's `ntfs` driver (whose `link` gives the file the time now, specs.md §22), a name that disk refuses, collides with one it holds or puts past its branch's limit, a name it holds already (a stale copy the view does not present), or a driver that makes no such name.
- **The journal**: `view_ops.kind = link`, `lpath` the name it was made from, `lpath2` the new name, `file_key` the inode, journaled before it runs; migration `0020_view_link.sql`, which makes `view_ops` again as `0006_owner.sql` made `ops`. The view presents the new name from its disk until a pass takes it in; its reprise at `atoll mount`'s start finds it done when the new name leads to that inode, undone otherwise.
- **The passes**: each name is a file of its own (§15.1): the new name is recorded and replicated as independent full copies, never as a link (§14), which the scenario pins. The link moves the change time of the file for both names, so the next pass reads the name it was made from once, as after a `touch`, and finds only metadata changed.
- **The link guard and the SSD cache with a file of two names**: each name has its link, both leading to the one file; a name deleted through the view loses its link and the other keeps its own; on the cache each name leaves as a file of its own.
- **Not done, for the maintainer**: mirroring links on the replicas. It would take link groups in the catalog, a constraint of the solver placing a group's replicas together, a job and an operation of its own with its reprise, and refreshes that link again; it saves a copy's room on each replica disk only while both names exist, and Sonarr and Radarr mostly delete the download's name after seeding. Recommended: not before a need is measured.
- **Tests**: `TestWriterLinks`, `TestWriterRepriseOfALink`, `TestAPassTakesInAHardLinkThroughTheView`, `TestTheLinkGuardWithAFileOfTwoNames`, `TestAFileOfTwoNamesLeavesTheCache`, and the loop-device `TestViewWrites`, which expected `EXDEV` and now makes a link through FUSE (to run in qemu).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): `EXDEV`, not `EEXIST`, where the disk holds something the view does not present at the new name; no hard link to a symbolic link; the link moves the change time of the name it was made from, read whole once at the next pass; through the view the two names have their own `st_ino`, as inodes are numbered by path, with `nlink` 2.

## Extended attributes through Atoll's own view, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §6.1, §9, §14, §15.4, §17.2, §21.2)

Samba keeps DOS attributes in `user.DOSATTRIB`, and macOS clients through Samba theirs in `user.com.apple.*` and `user.org.netatalk.*`. Commit `7d2a62e`, merged by `3cc31b4`. Vectors first: `xattrs.json` (19 cases), 4 cases in `view_resolve.json`, 4 in `catalog.json`; a scenario, `replicate-xattrs.json`, with a new step of the runner, `setxattr`, and `xattrs` in a tree.

- **Only `user.`**: a read of another namespace answers `ENODATA`, a list leaves it out, a write `ENOTSUP`; the root takes none, a symbolic link none (`EPERM`).
- **Read** from the copy the view presents, on its disk alone, which a read wakes as a read of data does; the catalog holds no attribute.
- **Written** on that copy alone, a folder's on every disk that holds it, journaled first, `view_ops.kind = xattr` with the file's inode.
- **Taken in**: the pass reads the set the copy of that inode holds now and gives it to each other copy `ok` or `surplus` on a disk it can write to, one operation `ops.kind = xattr` each, journaled with its event, after a look that the copy is as its row says; its row takes the change time that moved; the file's data is never copied again. Refused by a filesystem: `kept`, never stale. A copy on a disk that cannot be written keeps its set, `disk-unavailable`. The reprise undoes an `xattr` left planned, and the next pass gives what still differs.
- **The copy pipeline** gives a part its source's `user.` set before the `fsync` and the re-read; a target that refuses one still gets the copy, and an event `xattr-kept` names what it went without.
- **Changes made directly on a disk** still reach no other copy (§15.4): detecting them would read every copy's attributes at each pass.
- **Contract additions**: migration `0021_xattr.sql` (`ops.kind` and `view_ops.kind` gain `xattr`; it makes `ops` again, which takes time as the journal grows), event kinds `xattr` and `xattr-kept` (with `names`), the operation `xattr` of `api/schemas/daemon.schema.json`, the runner's `setxattr` step and `xattrs` field.
- **Tests**: `TestWriterXattrs`; in `jobs`, the pass giving and removing an attribute, a refusal (`ENOTSUP`), `EIO` then a disk back, `kill -9` at `xattrs-set`, and the pipeline with a refusal (`E2BIG`); the loop-device `TestViewXattrs` through FUSE on ext4, ntfs-3g and ntfs3 (to run in qemu).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): reads from the presented copy's disk, not the catalog, so Samba with `store dos attributes = yes` wakes disks as it browses (`= no` avoids it); a copy whose disk could not be written when the pass ran never gets the new set later; on a refusal a copy keeps what it could take; each `setxattr` journaled on its own; a folder's set on every disk at once, with no pass after; nothing given to a file in conflict or corrupt; a dry run lists no `xattr` operation.

## Copies written in parallel in immediate mode, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §12.3, §12.5, §14, §14.2, §19, §21.2)

DrivePool's real-time duplication, through the view ("copies written in parallel in `immediate` mode"). Commit `87a7409`, merged by `3cc31b4`. Vectors first: 3 cases in `catalog.json` for `view_ops.kind = part`.

- **What**: a new file the view makes whose policy, read before its size is known, says `immediate` with replicas wanted, with no file of the catalog at its path, is written, as the program writes it, to its original and to a part in `<workdir>/tmp/` of each replica's target, so that its bytes are on every target when the program closes it.
- **The targets** are the solver's (`watch.ParallelTargets`): the plan of that file, with its group for `colocate`, each disk's free space as its filesystem says it now. A target the plan chooses in `standby`, one past `max_parallel_per_disk` parts of the view, and a file that lands on the SSD cache get no part: those replicas are the ordinary job's.
- **The parts** (`internal/view/writer/parallel.go`): each journaled before it is made, an operation `create` of `ops` and the view's row `kind = part` (migration `0022_view_parts.sql`). A writer of its own writes each, never holding the program back: past 32 MiB waiting for one part or 128 MiB for all, a write that fails, or the room past `min_free` spent, the part is dropped at once and its operations undone. A failed write to the original, a truncation, a rename, a deletion or another open to write the file drops every part of it.
- **Closed**: a part that took every write and holds the original's size is synced and handed over. The pass that records the file waits for no `settle`; the job its plan queues for that disk adopts the part (`catalog.AdoptViewParts`) and runs the rest of the copy pipeline from it: the metadata, `fsync`, the re-read without cache against the original's hashes, the rename without replacing, the `fsync`s, the folders' times and the commit. The replica is the part itself, with no byte written again. A part no job takes is dropped by the pass; one `atoll mount` left mid-write, by the view's reprise at its next start.
- **A resume of any copy** now compares what its part holds past the chunks recorded with the source, and writes only where it differs, rather than truncating the part to them; the re-read without cache checks every chunk against the source's hashes either way.
- **Tests**: in `writer`, a file written in parallel, what gets no part, the parts dropped (truncated, renamed, deleted, opened again, past `min_free`, a write failing on the target, a part falling behind, a write failing on the original), the reprise of a part cut short; in `jobs`, the replica made from the part with no byte written again, a crash at each step of that copy, a file changed after it was closed, and a part no job takes; the loop-device `TestViewWritesInParallel` through FUSE on ext4, ntfs-3g and ntfs3 (to run in qemu).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): the daemon finishes the copy by adopting the part, `atoll mount` writing no row of the catalog, so the replica is recorded at the next pass; a target in `standby` gets no part rather than another disk; 32 MiB a part, 128 MiB in all, 4,096 writes queued; `min_free` measured when a disk's first part starts; `max_parallel_per_disk` counted in `atoll mount` alone; a file landing on the cache gets no part; any truncation drops the parts, a program's `ftruncate` to reserve room included; a file renamed after its close (qBittorrent's `.!qB`) loses its parts; the comparing resume for every copy.

## Copies at once, by the kind of each disk, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §14, §14.1, §15.12, §15.13, §24, §26, §29.3, §29.4)

The maintainer's decision: "c'est fonction du disque effectivement : sur HDD une à la fois, mais pour les SSD/NVMe plusieurs". Until then the daemon ran one copy at a time, and `max_parallel_per_disk` waited for its loop. Commits `880f47c` and `56c52f5`, merged by `a567188`. Vectors first: 16 cases of `config.json`.

- **The limits** (`jobs.limitsOf`, `disks.KindOf`): a copy counts against the disk it reads and the disk it writes. `replication.max_parallel_per_hdd` (1) applies to a disk with a spindle, or of a kind no one can tell; `replication.max_parallel_per_ssd` (4) to a disk with no spindle under any of its whole disks, as `/sys/block/<name>/queue/rotational` says, which wakes no disk. `max_parallel_per_disk`, when set, applies to every disk alike, as version 1 meant it; left out, it now means the limits by kind, the maintainer's exception to "a new key's default changes nothing", written in §29.3. At most 8 copies run in all.
- **The run** (`replicator.runAll`): copies start in queue order as soon as both their disks have room and no other copy of their file runs, looking at most 4,096 copies ahead; their lines come out in queue order, whatever order they end in. A cancel, `SIGTERM` or a low battery, stops every copy at its next step, and the run returns once each is over. Checked before a copy is journaled, at each chunk of the fill and of the verification, after the fill and before the rename.
- **What copies at once share** (`sharedSession`): a disk one copy loses is lost at once for all, and its handle closed once none uses it; a lock a disk around names (part, parents, rename); a lock on the link guard's map; SQLite's one writer. Room is held by the pending copies of §13.4, as one at a time. Balancing, scrub repairs and the cache's transfers stay one at a time; the conformance build runs one copy at a time, so ids and crash points stay deterministic.
- **Tests** (`internal/jobs/parallel*_test.go`, each copy in its own goroutine, the test machine recording which disks each touched and when): the limits by kind and by key, both disks counted; one copy of a file at a time; the order of the lines; EIO on the target or the source, ENOSPC, a disk gone and a cancel with copies in flight; a disk lost by one copy stopping the one beside it; kill -9 at each point of the pipeline in a subprocess, leaving several `.part` files, then the reprise; 8 random pools against the invariants. 14 mutations made, each caught. In `test/loopdev`, for qemu: `TestTheKindOfALoopDevice`, `TestCopiesAtOnceOnEachDriver`, `TestATargetFailingWithCopiesAtOnce` (dm-error under ext4 and XFS with copies in flight).
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): 4 on flash by default (a copy is sequential I/O; 4 cores on the ZimaBoard and the M920q); both disks counted; 8 copies at most in all (a 4 MiB chunk each, §26); the look-ahead of 4,096; one copy of a file at a time, even from flash; flash only when every whole disk says so, so a USB SSD that reports a spindle counts as an HDD; one copy at a time in the conformance build and wherever kinds cannot be read; a disk lost stops the copies beside it at their next step (target lost: the operation stays planned; source lost: undone, the part kept); no room check at a copy's start, the pending copies holding it; ids of operations and events follow timing; balancing, scrub repairs and transfers one at a time.

## The view's operations taken in by their names, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §15.13, §21.2)

The maintainer wanted Atoll's own view done before the next soak, its operations no longer walking their disks whole among it. Commit `4fd4a26`, merged by `a567188`. Vectors first: `view_names.json` (13 cases).

- **The names** (`watch.ViewNames`, pure): every path of each operation of the view not taken in yet is named on its disk; a folder's two paths are named and walked below on every disk whose catalog holds a folder at either (`catalog.FolderDisks`); `unlink` and `rmdir` name their path as deleted; past 10,000 names on a disk, that disk is walked whole. A pass adds these names to what it looks at, where it walked the disk whole before.
- **Still walked whole**, as before: passes with no names (start, reload, rescan, `--until-idle`, shadow mode), a disk with an operation of Atoll's own left planned, a disk with a copy moved or uncertain and no names for it, a disk past 10,000 names, and the disks the daemon asks to walk whole.
- **Measured** on 50,000 files a disk: 903 ms → 25 ms for a pass taking in a file the view made; 1.05 s → 21 ms, then 1.03 s → 25 ms, for a file it renamed; 14 to 16 ms for a pass with nothing to take in.
- **Tests**: `TestThePassTakesInTheViewByNames`, every kind of operation through the real writer, no disk walked whole; `TestTheViewsOperationsAreLookedAtByTheirNames`. 7 mutations made, each caught.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): a folder renamed now names every disk that holds it, where the operation's disk alone was walked; a name deleted for `unlink` and `rmdir` only; planned operations of the view named too; the view's names not counted as names given for a copy that moved, as before.

## OpenTelemetry: the log, and an export over OTLP, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §23.1, §23.2, §24, §26, §29.3, §29.7)

The maintainer chose option C in Go now, off by default for the metrics and the log, the memory it costs accepted, and by default the log in OpenTelemetry's data model and the metrics' names aligned on its conventions ("option C désactivé par défaut pour les metrics et logs, dès Go, c'est pas grave pour l'overhead mémoire; par défaut tu as logs JSON au modèle de données OTel et noms de métriques alignés sur ses conventions"), so that Vector, Elastic Agent or an OpenTelemetry Collector read it as the standard says. Commits `2f05e24`, `2e20ff5`, `da66906` and `1d9069e`, merged by `d527472`. Contract and vectors first: `otel_name` and `unit` for each metric of `api/metrics.json`, `conformance/vectors/otel_names.json` (21 cases), `log_record.json` (18 cases), 27 cases of `config.json`.

- **Metric names** (`internal/metrics`): the Prometheus names stay frozen at version 1; each metric gets an OpenTelemetry name, dotted under `atoll.`, no unit and no `_total` in it, its unit `By`, `s`, `Cel` or `{count}`, chosen so that the specification's compatibility translation (`PrometheusName`) gives the frozen name back exactly, which a test checks for every metric. No name is the namespace of another, as Elasticsearch maps dotted names to objects. The OpenTelemetry names are kept from now on as the Prometheus ones are (§29.3). `Points` gives the samples of `/metrics` as numbers; `/metrics` is byte for byte as it was.
- **The log** (`internal/telemetry`): one handler per process, a JSON line a record, as the data model's examples write it: `Timestamp` (Unix nanoseconds, a string), `SeverityText`, `SeverityNumber` (slog's level + 9), `Body`, `Attributes` (groups dotted; a string that is not UTF-8 as `<key>_b64`, `path_b64` among them), `Resource` (`service.name`, `service.version`, `host.name`) and `InstrumentationScope` (the Go package that logged). `[telemetry] log_format = "text"` gives slog's text again. Every command takes its configuration's format; the CLI's own lines (`atoll: …`) stay text.
- **The export**, off by default: with `otlp_endpoint` and `otlp_metrics`, the daemon sends the metrics from the recorder's snapshot every `otlp_interval` (1 min), and with `otlp_logs` its records, over OTLP/HTTP with protobuf (`/v1/metrics`, `/v1/logs`), through the official SDK v1.47.0; `atoll mount` sends its records. A ring of 2,048 records that never blocks and drops its oldest; each export bounded by `otlp_timeout` (10 s); a failure told at once, then at most once every 10 minutes, and never exported; headers from `otlp_headers_file`, read at each request, never logged, `https://` only; no redirect followed; a reload applies the new settings at once.
- **Memory** (§26): the daemon on a small pool, 19.8 MiB resident before; 25.5 to 26.2 MiB with OTLP off, the binary 38.4 MB from 26.0 as the exporters bring gRPC and protobuf, its pages 5 MiB more; 29.1 to 29.7 MiB sending both signals every second. On 200,000 files a disk, where `TestScale` peaked at 183.8 MiB, those 5 MiB come near the 190.7 MiB of the 200 MB: to measure again in qemu.
- **Tests**: `TestPrometheusNameVectors`, `TestEachOpenTelemetryNameGivesThePrometheusName`, `TestOpenTelemetryNamesFollowTheRules`, `TestPointsAreTheText`, `TestLineVectors`, the handler's tests (what slog gives, the package that logged, debug left out, text and back, the sink, whole lines), `TestExportSendsTheMetricsAndTheLog` against an in-process collector decoding the protobuf, `TestADeadCollectorHoldsNothingUp` (500 and a hang), `TestARedirectIsNotFollowed`, `TestAFullQueueDropsItsOldest`, `TestReadHeaders`, `TestReporterTellsOnceAWhile`, `TestManagerFollowsTheConfiguration`, and the daemon in a process of its own logging JSON and exporting, and logging text. Verified: the race detector on everything, lint on the three builds, the scenarios; 28 mutations made, each caught. Nothing read the daemon's text log, so nothing had to follow the new default.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): the names where bytes sit beside a count (`atoll.disk.copy`, `atoll.file`, `atoll.trash`, `atoll.copied`…), and snake_case joins where a name would be another's namespace (`atoll.trash_entries`, `atoll.files_waiting`…); count units for 0/1 and state gauges, as unit `1` would add `_ratio`; the OpenTelemetry names frozen as the Prometheus ones; the resource without `service.instance.id`; the scope the Go package; `ObservedTimestamp` left out of the line, `Attributes` when empty, every string not UTF-8 as `<key>_b64`, durations in integer nanoseconds, times RFC 3339 with nine digits; commands log JSON too; only the daemon exports metrics, `atoll mount` its log, commands nothing; one flat `[telemetry]` table, 1 min and 10 s; no user, query or fragment in the endpoint, a warning for an endpoint with no signal; the headers file `https://` only, comments, the exporter's own headers refused, 16 KiB; no gzip; a log processor of Atoll's own, dropping the oldest; the SDK's retries within `otlp_timeout`, a failed batch dropped; failures told once every 10 minutes; a stop flushes for 5 s at most; no redirect; `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` can add to the exported resource, documented rather than blocked.

## GitHub Actions and releases, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §25, §26, §27, §29.7)

The maintainer chose on 2026-11-19, each the recommended option: the checks on GitHub's hosted runners, x64 and arm64, on every push to `beta` and `main` and each pull request to `main`; a release built by GoReleaser on a tag he alone pushes, a `.tar.gz` and a `.deb` for amd64 and arm64 with `SHA256SUMS`, an SBOM per archive and GitHub's provenance attestations, drafted for him to publish; the first, `v0.1.0-beta.1`, after the soak of 24 hours; the local CI kept; Dependabot on `beta`. Commits "Say the version of the build with atoll --version", "Measure the throughput of the hashing on the CPU that runs it", "Keep what the qemu test downloads from a run to the next" and "Check every change on GitHub Actions, and build releases with GoReleaser", merged by `6b5a964`.

- **CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): `race`, `integration`, `conformance` and `lint` call the `make` targets `test/ci/run.sh` calls, in its image, `golangci/golangci-lint:v2.15.0`, with its Go 1.27.2 and as root; `govulncheck` v1.9.0 with the latest patch of go.mod's Go, the one a release is built with; `qemu`, `make test-qemu` on both kernels, which says whether the runner has `/dev/kvm`, its downloads cached by month; `arm64`, `go test ./...` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`; and the hashing's throughput, on arm64 and in `blake3-amd64`, with whether the CPU has SVE2 or AVX-512, in the run's summary. A newer push cancels the run it supersedes. Pull requests to `beta` run it too: Dependabot's.
- **Media servers** (`.github/workflows/mediaservers.yml`): `make test-mediaservers` on Mondays, on `beta`, and on demand; Plex is skipped, as no secret holds a claim.
- **Releases** (`.goreleaser.yaml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`): on a tag `v*`, the static binary (`CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath`, `-s -w`), its version the tag, which `go build` reads from git, with no time of the build in it and the commit's for each file: one commit gives the same archives and packages, byte for byte. For each architecture a `.tar.gz` with the binary, `deploy/`'s units, sample configuration and README, `LICENSE` and `README.md`, its SBOM (SPDX, syft v1.56.0), and a `.deb`; `SHA256SUMS`; a draft release, a prerelease for `-alpha`, `-beta` and `-rc`, its notes the commits since the last tag and how to check a download; then `actions/attest-build-provenance` on each archive and package, which `gh attestation verify <file> --repo peondusud/atoll` checks. `make release-snapshot` builds the same into `dist/` with only Docker, from a clone.
- **The `.deb`**: `/usr/bin/atoll`; `deploy/`'s two units in `/usr/lib/systemd/system/`, their `ExecStart` pointed at `/usr/bin/atoll` by a hook of GoReleaser's; the sample configuration in `/usr/share/doc/atoll/examples/`, as `atoll init` refuses to write where a configuration is; `postinst` makes the group and the account `atoll` when they are not there, and has systemd read its units again. Depends on `passwd`, for `useradd`; recommends mergerfs and smartmontools; suggests fuse3 and ntfs-3g. It enables, starts, restarts, stops and removes nothing.
- **`atoll --version`**: `atoll` and the version of the build, the one `atoll_build_info` gives, without reading the configuration; anything after it is a usage error. Scenario `version.json`.
- **The qemu test's downloads**: with `CACHE`, `test/qemu/run.sh` keeps Debian's packages and the mainline kernel in a folder that outlives the container (`make test-qemu QEMU_CACHE=<folder>`), and says whether qemu has KVM.
- **BLAKE3's throughput**: `BenchmarkHashReader`, one read of 64 MiB feeding both hashes, and `BenchmarkChunkSum`, in `internal/integrity`, which name the code zeebo/blake3 takes on the CPU; `test/ci/blake3.sh` runs them for the CI. On the maintainer's i7-12700K, AVX2, with the machine loaded: 1690 to 1800 MB/s, and 3570 to 3950 MB/s.
- Every third-party action pinned by its commit, each released at least two weeks before: actions/checkout v7.0.1, actions/cache v6.1.0, actions/setup-go v7.0.0, goreleaser/goreleaser-action v7.3.0 with GoReleaser v2.20.0, actions/attest-build-provenance v4.2.2, anchore/sbom-action/download-syft v0.24.3. `permissions: contents: read` at the top; the release job alone writes: `contents`, `id-token` and `attestations`. No secret but `GITHUB_TOKEN`.
- Verified: actionlint 1.7.12, with shellcheck 0.11.0, on the three workflows, and shellcheck on the scripts: nothing found. `goreleaser check`. A snapshot of `6f40bcf` from a clone: two `.tar.gz` of 7.4 and 6.8 MB, two `.deb` of the same sizes, two SBOMs of 24 KB; built twice from one commit, the archives and packages are the same bytes, the SBOMs not (syft writes the time and a random namespace). In a `debian:13` container, `apt install ./atoll_…_amd64.deb` brings mergerfs, smartmontools and fuse3, makes `atoll` (uid 999, home `/nonexistent`, shell `nologin`) and enables nothing; `atoll --version` gives `v0.0.0-20261119044801-6f40bcf77118`; `atoll init --pool-id p --view-mount /srv/pool` works; installed again, the account is kept; removed, then purged, the account, `/etc/atoll` and `/var/lib/atoll` stay; with the group made before, the account takes it. Under systemd in a container: both units `disabled` and `inactive` after the install, `systemd-analyze verify` finds nothing, and after `atoll init` the daemon starts as `atoll` and runs a pass. The arm64 `.deb` installs and its binary runs under emulation. On a tag `v0.1.0-beta.1`, in a repository of its own and short of publishing: `atoll_0.1.0-beta.1_linux_amd64.tar.gz`, `atoll_0.1.0~beta.1_amd64.deb`, `atoll v0.1.0-beta.1`. The qemu cache, twice on the mainline kernel with no test: 141 s, then 97 s with 125 packages and the kernel from the cache. The unit tests of `cmd/atoll` and `internal/integrity`, lint, `go vet` for arm64, and the 618 scenarios.
- Only GitHub's first run can tell: KVM on `ubuntu-latest`, container jobs on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, the unit tests on real arm64, BLAKE3's SVE2 code there, the caches, and a release from end to end.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"; the sample configuration in `/usr/share/doc/atoll/examples/`: "Garder /usr/share/doc"): the sample configuration goes to `/usr/share/doc/atoll/examples/atoll.toml`, not to `/etc/atoll/atoll.toml` as a conffile as the choice said, as `atoll init` refuses to write where a configuration is, the sample names four made-up disks, and dpkg would ask at every upgrade that changed it (one entry of `.goreleaser.yaml` to change otherwise); `atoll --version` added, `atoll <version>`; the version Go's from git, not `-X`, so a snapshot prints Go's pseudo-version; the packaged units' `ExecStart` pointed at `/usr/bin/atoll` by a hook, `deploy/` and the tarball keeping `/usr/local/bin`; `postinst` makes the account only when it is not there, no `prerm`, nothing removed on purge; Depends `passwd`, Recommends mergerfs and smartmontools, Suggests fuse3 and ntfs-3g; the Maintainer field a GitHub noreply address; the tests as root in the CI's image, as `run.sh` runs them; the CI on pull requests to `beta` too, for Dependabot's; the media servers' schedule on `beta`; a newer run cancels an older one on `main` too; the release notes from git, merge commits left out, about 340 commits for the first; attestations on archives and packages, SBOMs per archive; GoReleaser v2.20.0, the newest two weeks old; the snapshot's version `<next patch>-snapshot.<commit>`, the package section `admin`.

## How deep a walk goes, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §15.8, §15.10, §15.12, §23, §24, §26)

The maintainer chose B by default, with C through the configuration ("profondeur option B par défaut avec C possible via config"): a walk goes no further than the 4095 bytes of a mergerfs branch, and `walk.long_paths = "walk"` walks past it with a bounded number of folders open. Commits `fc7bf66` and `7415f13`, merged by `5ae2647`.

- **The bound.** No walk records or goes below a name whose `<mount>/<root>/<logical path>` is past 4095 bytes, the formula of `names.FitsBranch` and of `branch-path-too-long`, per disk: what mergerfs opens, so a copy past it protects nothing. That covers a scan, a pass, a look at the names the watcher gave, `atoll catalog verify`, a watch folder by folder, and the walks of the work folder's `tmp/` and `trash/` (58 bytes further). The shortest such name on each branch is reported, in `branch_path_too_long` (`atoll scan`, `catalog verify`, `catalog rebuild`; left out when empty) and by the alert `branch-path-too-long:<id>`; `catalog verify` exits 4 then. A walk holds 2,048 folders and some 4 MiB of paths at most.
- **Nothing read as deleted.** A row at or below such a name, or carried there by a folder renamed, is `unknown`. While one is on a disk, a copy found nowhere is `uncertain`, so its file is kept and made whole again, with nothing in the trash; an overwriting move is still `gone`.
- **`walk.long_paths = "walk"`.** At most `walk.max_open` folders are open at once (64 by default, at least 3). A folder let go of is opened again from the deepest one held and checked by device, inode and mount; one renamed, replaced or removed meanwhile is left aside in `unreadable`, with nothing of what was below it. Measured by a counting wrapper: at most 3 and 4 on a 40-level, 4.8 KB tree. `max_open` with `"skip"` gets a warning that it does nothing (§24.4).
- **Costs of depth.** `addParents` and the names check stop at the first parent they hold, the square of the depth for a chain where it was the cube. `OpenFileAt` and a look at names hold one folder at a time. The watcher marks no folder past the limit, and with `"walk"` holds two descriptors a level for `max_open/2` levels, marking deeper folders later from the root.
- **Tests**: unit tests in `internal/watch` (the boundary at 4094, 4095 and 4096 bytes in both modes; no deletion after an ancestor renamed, a folder or the copy moved past the limit; descriptors counted; an ancestor renamed, replaced or removed in the middle of a walk by fault injection), `internal/jobs` (a pass trashes and deletes nothing: the other copy would have gone to the trash on the old code), `internal/ops` and `internal/alerts`; 13 configuration vectors; the scenario `walk-past-the-branch-limit`, whose tree is deeper than `PATH_MAX`, which the runner now makes and reads through `os.Root`. Verified: the race detector on every package, integration, lint, and the scenarios.
- **To run as root**: the watchers folder by folder (fanotify on ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g, inotify) in both modes, a tree past `PATH_MAX` on each driver, and an ancestor renamed in the middle of a walk on btrfs.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): the bound counts the whole branch path, per disk; while a name past the limit is on a disk, a copy found nowhere there is `uncertain`, so a deletion made straight on that disk is undone, and a copy moved past the limit made again, until the long name is fixed (`unknown`, tried first, left a row and a trash job that failed); each crossing name reported on its own; the work folder's walks 58 bytes further; `max_open` at least 3, unused in mode B; a look at names reports such a name only when it is there; the reason in the trash listing in English; with `"walk"`, an operation's chain of handles and the solver's checks of parents not bounded.

## The local API answers a guard, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §15.6, §25, §25.1, §26)

Of the local API's writes that TODO.md left to decide, the maintainer chose option C on 2026-11-19 ("api local option C"): one write, an answer to a guard, the most useful from afar, from a phone, a web page or Home Assistant, with the smallest surface. Moving and deleting stay with the CLI. Commits `33f21f2` and `18dfbca`, merged by `0768756`.

- **`POST /v1/guards/{id}`** answers a guard of a disk by the code of `atoll guard confirm` and `reject`, under the catalog's lock, with the same events in the transaction that records the answer, and gives their output byte for byte (`api/schemas/guard-answer.schema.json`). Its body holds `answer`, `kind` (`deletions` by default), `dry_run`, `yes` and `up_to`.
- **Invariant 17.** Nothing is answered without `"yes": true`. `"dry_run": true` says whether a guard of that kind stands, the time an answer would go up to, and what the guard holds, as `guard list` says it, and writes nothing; both, or neither, is 400. `atoll guard confirm` and `reject` take `--dry-run` for the same. The output of an answer is as it was, so the scenarios and scripts that read it are unchanged.
- **The guard shown.** `up_to`, which a dry run gives, names the guard an answer is for. A guard that ended and tripped again since, or was answered and tripped again, is not answered (409): a confirm tapped on an old notification never lets go of what tripped after it.
- **Through the daemon's loop.** The loop gives the answer between two of its steps, under the lock as for a pass (`daemon.Hand`, `Options.Catalog`), and is then told as SIGHUP would tell it, so that its next pass takes the answer. Taking the lock from the API's own goroutine would open and close a descriptor of the catalog's file beside a pass that writes, which drops every lock SQLite holds on that file in the process. An answer the loop does not take within the request's 60 s, or that a daemon parked on a low battery refuses, is 503 with nothing answered; sent again, an answer answers once.
- **Refused, with nothing written**: a body of another type than `application/json` (415), which a browser cannot send to another site unasked; one over 4 KiB (413); one that is not one object of those fields, each once and named exactly, or holds an unknown answer or kind, or an `up_to` written otherwise (400); a disk the configuration does not list (404); and, with `yes`, a daemon in `--dry-run` (409), and over TCP a token file other accounts can read (403).
- **Tests**: `cmd/atoll/apiguard_test.go` builds twin pools of folder disks, which a real pass holds, and answers one by the CLI and one by the API, for a confirm and a reject. The outputs are the same, the dry run's included; so are the rows and events, and the next pass, which lets the three deletions go after a confirm and keeps the files after a reject. The mass-modification guard is tried likewise. The test also tries each refusal above, with the catalog unchanged; `up_to`; the token missing, wrong, and in a file of mode 0644; a daemon in `--dry-run`; and the daemon's loop taking the answer, then passing because of the reload, and 503 with no loop. `internal/daemon/work_test.go`: the work waits for a command that holds the lock and runs under it; work whose request is gone never runs; a parked daemon refuses it. Two scenarios, `guard-answer-dry-run.json` and `guard-answer-dry-run-modifications.json`: the dry runs write nothing, the guard holds as before, and the answer that follows is as without them. Verified: the race detector on every package, integration, lint, and the scenarios.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): one path with a JSON body, `yes` and `dry_run` named after the flags, 400 when both or neither; `up_to` optional, 409 on a mismatch; the CLI gains `--dry-run` and still no `--yes`; the dry run's `paths` are what `guard list` shows; the dry run holds the lock; reads and answers share the socket and the token, but an answer over TCP refuses a token file other accounts can read (403); `application/json` required and no CORS headers; answers through the daemon's loop, 503 after 60 s; a daemon in `--dry-run` refuses answers (409); no event field says an answer came through the API, the log does with the remote address; the daemon told through its reload; an unknown disk 404.

## The openmediavault plugin's form, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §21.1, §24, §25)

The maintainer chose, on the research of the same day (`docs/research/omv-mergerfs.md`): an output of its own and an object in `--json` both; the plugin's one `minfreespace` chosen by a variable, the largest of the disks' own by default, or one `min_free` for every disk; and a warning on `view.mount` ("pour omv 1. A et B 2. via une variable par défaut choix le plus grand mais l'option la même min_free doit être possible 3. avertir oui"). Commit `1547417`, merged by `d22f983`. Scenarios and vectors first: `mergerfs-config-omv-form`, `-largest`, `-unit` and `-refused`, and five cases of `view.omv_min_free` in `config.json`.

- **`atoll mergerfs-config --omv`** prints each field of the plugin's form, a line each, named as the form names them: Name, Paths (a branch a line, `<path>=<mode>`), Create policy, Minimum free space and Unit, Options; then the mount point the pool will have. In French, the labels the French form shows: openmediavault 8 translates Name and Unit, the plugin 8.0.5 none of its own. With `--json` too, the output of the line gains `omv`: `rpc`, what `omv-rpc -u admin MergerFS set` takes (`name`, `paths`, `createpolicy`, `minfreespace`, `minfreespaceunit`, `options`; a script adds the `uuid`), `mount`, and `warnings`, a closed list. `--json` alone is as it was.
- **The one minimum free space**, `view.omv_min_free`: `largest`, the default, the largest of the branches' own; `same`, none when they differ, the disks and their values named, exit 4. Only the branches that take new files count, as mergerfs reads `minfreespace` only to place one; every branch when none does. A branch with no value of its own exits with 4, as the line does.
- **The unit**: the largest of G, M and K that holds the value exactly in a count of at most 99999, the most the form and the RPC take (read in the plugin's source); otherwise rounded up, in the smallest unit whose count holds it, with a warning: 5% of 4 TB is 195,312,500K exactly, past 99999, so 187G. Past 99999G, exit 1.
- **The name**: the last component of `view.mount` when it is `/srv/mergerfs/<name>` and the form takes it; otherwise that component still, or `pool_id`, and a warning to set `view.mount` to `/srv/mergerfs/<name>`.
- **Refused**, exit 1 and nothing on standard output, naming the disk: a branch path holding `"`, `'`, `` ` ``, `$`, `\`, `;`, `|`, `&`, `<`, `>` or a control character, line breaks included. Without `--omv`, the line has it, as mergerfs takes it.
- With `view.engine = "atoll"`, the form is the pool that breaks the glass, as the line is, the cache disks included.
- **Tests**: seven in `internal/view` (`TestOMVSize`, `TestOMVRefused`, `TestOMVName`, `TestOMVForm`, `TestOMVSame`, `TestOMVNoneTakesNewFiles`, `TestOMVRefuses`); `TestMergerfsConfigOMV`, `TestMergerfsConfigOMVRefuses` and `TestMergerfsConfigOMVWarnsOfTheLargestFile` for the human output, its French and standard error, which no scenario judges. Verified: the race detector on every package, lint on the default and conformance builds, and 621 scenarios.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): a configuration key, not a flag, for "une variable"; `omv` only with `--omv --json`, `--json` alone unchanged; the value taken from the branches that take new files; the count capped at 99999 by the plugin, so the smallest unit that holds the value rounded up; the name from `view.mount`, or `pool_id`; every control character refused; no `uuid` in `omv.rpc` (a script adds openmediavault's uuid of a new object, `fa4b1c66-ef79-11e5-87a0-0002b3a176b4`, as the documentation says); the French labels as openmediavault's own catalog gives them, not seen in a browser; under `same` with values that differ, no Minimum free space line and `null` in JSON; the largest-file warning on standard error only; the paths unindented, to paste into the form; the menu named "Storage, mergerfs" in `deploy/README.md`, not checked.

## The ntfs driver of Linux 7.1 supported, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §10, §15.8, §17, §17.1, §22)

The maintainer chose option C of `docs/research/ntfs-driver.md`, with its author's suggestion ("pilote NTFS option C"). Commits `7c6971b` and `630ed0f`, merged by `4079a7e`. Vectors and scenarios first: 18 new cases in `disk_check.json`, 2 changed, and the scenarios `prune-name-refused-ntfs` and `link-guard-make-ntfs`.

- **Supported as ntfs3** (§10): a disk mounted with the type `ntfs` is `online` when its superblock options hold `iocharset=utf8` and not `nocase`; `readonly` with `nocase` or `iocharset` otherwise, `nocase` refused at `disk add` as on ntfs3. `unsupported-driver` stays in the closed list and in the schemas, as version 1 keeps every value, and no check gives it any more.
- **`errors=remount-ro` required**: without it the disk is `readonly` with the new reason `errors-continue`, in §10 and the `reason` enums of `api/schemas/daemon`, `disk-list` and `scan`; `disk add` takes such a disk `readonly`, with a warning. With it, a volume Windows left hibernated, or one marked dirty, is mounted read-only, which `read-only-mount` reads. Order: `ads`, `nocase`, `iocharset`, `errors-continue`, then the rest.
- **`EINVAL` from `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` is a name refused on that type** (§17.1, §15.12): `name-refused`, nothing moved, neither `link` nor, for a folder, a plain `rename` tried; a file a reprise would put back stays, its operation `planned`. The type is the one the mount table gives when an NTFS disk is opened. Before, a prune of such a name ended `io-error` with both names; the scenario fails so without the change.
- **The link guard makes no link on that type** (§17): its `link` moves the file's modification time, so a link of the guard would change the copy it guards.
- **§22**: `mount -i -t ntfs` past a `mount.ntfs` helper, `umask=022` recommended, the `link` bug and what follows from it (§15.4), `windows_names` and `link`, the names ntfs-3g wrote that it cannot rename (`ERANGE`, `io-error`), no journal of its own. Reporting the bug upstream is the maintainer's.
- **Conformance**: the control file takes `driver` (`ntfs3` or `ntfs`) on a disk of `fs` `ntfs`: its mount shows the type `ntfs` with `iocharset=utf8` and `errors=remount-ro`, and a name of `refuses` is refused to `unlink` and `rmdir`, not to `link`.
- **Tests**: `TestCheck` on the new vectors, `TestWhatTheTypeNTFSDoesOtherwise`, `TestARenameRefusedOnTheTypeNTFS`, `TestAPruneOfANameTheTypeNTFSRefuses`, `TestTheGuardMakesNoLinkOnTheTypeNTFS`, and `disk add` on that type. In `test/loopdev`, `ntfs` joins every list of drivers that has ntfs3, mounted with `mount -i -t ntfs -o errors=remount-ro,iocharset=utf8` and skipped on a kernel without it, with ntfs3's exemptions for names that disagree, and `ntfsfix -d` before a volume a dead device left dirty is mounted again. New: the mount check on that type, the name corpus with and without `windows_names`, `TestPruneOfARefusedName` on it, `TestALinkOnTheTypeNTFSMovesItsTime` and `TestTheLinkGuardOnTheTypeNTFS`.
- **Verified**: the race detector on every package, lint on the default, conformance and loop-device builds, and 619 scenarios. The loop-device tests on `ntfs` wait for a run in qemu on the mainline kernel.
- **Not decided**: point 3, a check of `hiberfil.sys` on every driver.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-20** ("Tout confirmer"): the reason's name, given for any `errors=` but `remount-ro` and for none; no link of the guard on the type `ntfs`; `disk add` taking an `errors-continue` disk `readonly`, as `iocharset`, rather than refusing it; a folder's `EINVAL` read as the name refused too; `ENOSYS` still falling back to `link`; `ntfsfix -d` in the loop-device tests.

## The third soak of 12 hours, stopped after 9 hours, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §26)

`TestDaemonSoak` on `ab9e21f`, the first pass held apart (`931c71b`), in qemu without KVM on Linux 6.12, `SOAK=12h SOAK_FILES=200000`, from 2026-11-18 at 19:53. The maintainer had it stopped after 9 hours 17 minutes, on 2026-11-19 at 05:10, to run a soak again once the features he decided that day are merged ("enlève le soak … on en relancera un après les merges de toutes les nouvelles fonctionnalités"). Its log is `bin/soak12c/soak-12h.log`.

- **No fault**: 672 passes, none held nor partial, no error, and one warning, expected, no `smartctl` in the guest.
- **Memory, at each of the seven tenths it reached**: 3.5 to 4.0 MiB of heap in use, 9 goroutines, 14 open files, with no growth; 37 to 44 MiB resident; a peak of 170.8 to 172.6 MiB, under the 200 MB (190.7 MiB) at every tenth, for 172.8 to 180.7 MiB on `2d016b3`. The first pass peaked at 180.5 MiB, for 195.2 MiB on `2d016b3`: the fix of `931c71b` holds in the soak too, as `TestScale` had measured. The catalog grew from 76.8 to 100.4 MiB, events and journal, as before.
- **The passes**: the one that starts took 7 min 4 s, for 5 min 56 s. Those after it took 7.1 s at the median for six hours, as on `2d016b3`, then 12 to 20 s from 02:00 to 05:00, with 130 to 160 operations a pass for 92, and 9.5 s again by 05:00. The machine ran the containers of the media servers and of openmediavault from about 02:00 (the entry below), and a pass that takes longer gathers more of the soak's changes, so it runs more operations. That it was the load alone is not shown: the next soak, with nothing else on the machine, is to tell.

## Media servers and openmediavault tried in containers, 2026-11-19 (specs.md §21.1, §21.5)

The maintainer asked for the try of the notifications in a container, with Plex or an alternative, and for openmediavault the same ("pour plex je veux que tu fasse le test dans un container avec un plex/alternative", "pour openmediavault pareil dans un container").

- **`test/mediaservers`** (`make test-mediaservers`): `run.sh` starts Plex, Jellyfin and Emby from their vendors' latest images on a network of their own, and runs a test that sets each up from scratch, makes a library of movies holding one movie, then writes, renames and deletes another below it, telling the server of each change through `internal/notify`, as the daemon does after a pass: the folder of a new file, both folders of a rename, and the folder of a file sent to the trash, which is no longer there. The servers read the library at `/data` and the test writes it at `/pool`, so `path_map` is part of what is tried. The test runs on a commit, as `test/ci/run.sh` does, and the lint job of the CI lints it.
- **Jellyfin 12.4.0 and Emby 4.10.2 pass**, twice in a row on `4d4eb6f`: each lists the movie once told of it, at its new name alone after the rename, and no more after the deletion, a folder that is gone naming the item that stood there. Jellyfin acts after `LibraryMonitorDelay`, which the test sets to 5 s from its 60; Emby, which has no such setting, after some 90 s a step.
- **Plex 1.43.5 takes `refresh?path=`**: tried by hand, a partial scan of a new movie's folder lists it within 2 s, with "Run a partial scan when changes are detected" on or off.
  - **It ignores one in a library none of whose scans found a file yet**: a partial scan of a new folder, and one of the library's own folder, found nothing in a library made empty, until a scan of the whole library found the movie; from then on each partial scan worked. Jellyfin did the same in the first run, its library made empty ("Library folder … is inaccessible or empty, skipping"); Emby did not. So the test makes each library with a movie in it, as a library in use has.
  - **Unclaimed, it answers 401 to every other host**, `allowedNetworks` or not, from its start: one first server took the requests of its network, no later one did, its own address and the loopback always. So `run.sh` takes `PLEX_CLAIM`, a claim token of the maintainer's account, has the server claimed, and gives its token to the test through a volume only the test reads, which sends it as `token_file` does; without it, the Plex test is skipped. Not run yet with a claim.
- **Jellyfin left a library made during its first scan out of it**, and dropped the scan the library's creation asked for: the test asks for a scan of the whole library until the seed is listed.
- **openmediavault** (`docs/research/omv-mergerfs.md`): in a Debian 13 container running systemd, openmediavault 8.6.1, its mergerfs plugin 8.0.5 and mergerfs 2.42.0. mergerfs 2.42.0 takes Atoll's fstab line as it is, the change of branches at runtime included. The plugin takes Atoll's branches with their modes and its options, but one `minfreespace` for the pool, in K, M or G (a branch's own is dropped with no error), and mounts at `/srv/mergerfs/<name>`. The form of Atoll's output for it waits for the maintainer.

## The first pass back under the 200 MB, 2026-11-18 (specs.md §26)

The maintainer asked why the soak's first pass peaked at 195.2 MiB ("Oui, cherche").

- **Not the daemon: the pass.** `TestScale`'s first pass, which runs the pass alone, peaked at 198.5 MiB on `a101246`, its heap 148.4 MiB live, where `47ab5f6` peaks at 182.7 MiB with 135.5 MiB live, measured the same way the same day. The two runs were in qemu, from copies of the tests that stop after the first pass and take a heap profile at the most live heap. The soak's own first pass on `a101246` held 150.4 MiB live.
- **What grew.** The profiles hold all of it in the plan of the whole pool (`watch.filesForPlan`, `solver.newPlanner`), 22 MiB more than on `47ab5f6`. Phase 2b had made what the plan holds for every file larger:
  - each file's `planFile`, by 48 bytes for the counts of the SSD cache, 224 bytes once the allocator rounds it, for 176;
  - its `File`, by 16 bytes, for its residency;
  - what the settling remembers of it, by 8 bytes, for a conflict held.
  On 200,000 files, that is about 14 MiB.
- **The fix.** What only a file on the SSD cache, or with copies misplaced in a plan of `atoll balance`, needs is held apart, and made for that file alone. A `planFile` is 160 bytes, 64 fewer for each file once rounded, about 12 MiB on 200,000 files. A test holds `planFile`, `File`, `Copy` and what the settling remembers to their sizes: a field more for every file is then a choice, not something nobody saw.
- **Measured in qemu on the fix**, in the same copies of the tests:
  - `TestScale`'s first pass peaks at 180.5 MiB, its heap 134.6 MiB live;
  - the soak's first pass peaks at 181.7 MiB, its heap 136.0 MiB live;
  - both are under the 200 MB (190.7 MiB), where `47ab5f6` was.
  The copies sampled the heap every 64 KiB, which costs a little.
- **Left:** `File`'s residency could be a byte, 3 MiB on 200,000 files. It stays as it is: the first pass is back where it was.

## The soak of 12 hours on the passes that look at names, 2026-11-18 (specs.md §26)

The maintainer asked for it once phase 2b was done, 12 hours ahead of the 24 ("lance le soak 12h deja"): `TestDaemonSoak` on `2d016b3`, the code of the passes that look at what changed (`5a6586e`), in qemu without KVM on Linux 6.12, `SOAK=12h SOAK_FILES=200000`, the run made on `f438a3a` on 2026-11-14. Its log is `bin/soak12b/soak-12h.log`.

- **It passed**: 911 passes, none held nor partial, no error, and one warning, expected, no `smartctl` in the guest.
- **The passes.** The one that starts took 5 min 56 s, for 6 min 21 s. Every pass after it looked at the disk the writer changes alone, by names, and took 7.0 s at the median, 8.0 s on average and 15.9 s at most, for 105 s at the median on `f438a3a`. Its time is that of the operations it runs: about 92 a pass, and 112 to 116 in the second and third tenths, where a pass took 10 to 11 s. The daemon spent 2.0 hours of the 12 in its passes, for 8.9 hours, and ran as many operations a minute as before, about 116, in three times as many passes.
- **At each tenth**: 74 to 99 passes, for 24 to 31; 3.3 to 3.6 MiB of heap in use, 9 goroutines, 14 open files, with no growth; 38 to 110 MiB resident, for 174 to 184; a peak of 172.8 to 180.7 MiB, under the 200 MB (190.7 MiB) at every tenth, for 212.1 to 220.4 MiB, the Go runtime holding at most 155.0 to 163.8 MiB of it and the heap 126.0 to 141.1 MiB live. The catalog grew from 73.3 to 113.1 MiB, as on `f438a3a`: events and journal under a change every second.
- **The first pass is still past the 200 MB**: 195.2 MiB, the Go runtime holding at most 173.9 MiB, 19 past its soft limit of 155, and the heap 147.5 MiB live. Read at its most, 193.9 MiB: the runtime 171.7, SQLite's allocator 10.2, the binary's pages 16.0, and 4.0 of it all in transparent huge pages. It is the pass that finds the 400,000 copies of a catalog that knows none, once; `TestScale` measured every step at 183.8 MiB at most on `47ab5f6`, and the soak on `f438a3a` had its first pass at 192.4 MiB. Brought back under the same day (the entry above).
- The soak's comments on how many passes a tenth holds dated from passes that walked both disks whole; they say so now.

## Passes that look at what changed, 2026-11-17 (specs.md §15.13)

Commits "Look at some names of a disk alone, as a pass is given them", "Plan only what may have changed after a look limited to names" and "Look at the names the watcher heard, not the whole disk", on `beta`, the last piece of phase 2b. Vectors first: `named_verdict.json` (12 cases) and `heard_names.json` (8).

- **The maintainer's answers** (2026-11-17, the recommended ones): a copy a look limited to names no longer finds is gone when an event said its name, or a folder above it, was deleted, or when its row says it went already; renamed when its inode is found under a name the look read; and an absence only a `moved-from` explains waits for a walk of the whole disk. A file a name gives is looked at alone, a folder walked below it, and the folder that holds a name read for its entry. Then, once measured that the plan of the whole pool was half of each pass: the plan too takes only what may have changed. Rejected: a whole walk before any absence, which would have left the soak's load as it was; deciding as a whole walk does, where a lost event could make a move a deletion; listing the folder of each name, or the folder below the root.
- **The look** (`watch.lookNamed`): each name reached from the root a name at a time, following no link; the rows at or below the names read through the indexes, by the name and by the range below it apart, as their OR read every row; a file found where no row is, whose inode a row elsewhere holds, brings that name in, and so does a folder's. What the look leaves waiting (a new name, a changed copy, a folder renamed) the next pass reads again (`Outcome.Again`); what it concluded nothing of, the next pass walks whole (`Outcome.Whole`). Only the entries at or below its names can go. A disk with copies that went has their names looked at again; one with a copy that moved, walked whole unless names were given.
- **The plan** (`watch.SettleOnly`, `catalog.PlanSet`), when the pass walked no disk whole and every disk is online or in standby: the files at the names looked at, every file not `ok`, every file on the SSD cache or with a copy to make or transfer, those a copy ran for, their groups of colocate and the files above them. The guard counts a disk's copies only when something counts against them, less what the look added; `DueFiles` goes through `copies_gone`.
- **The daemon**: each ripe group someone other than Atoll made gives its name (`watch.HeardNames`), a group at the root, an overflow, or past 10,000 names a walk of the whole disk; names add up until a pass takes them, with those the last pass left to see again.
- **Measured** (a container, 50,000 files a disk, 2026-11-17): a pass that walks and plans everything with nothing to do, about 1 s; a pass given one name, 12 ms.
- Tests: `TestNamedVerdictVectors`, `TestHeardNamesVectors`, `TestHeardAddsUp`; in `jobs`, the named looks (a new file, a deletion by its event and without one, a rename by its inode, a link, an overwrite, folders walked, renamed, deleted, emptied, a name below a link, an edit, a move a walk found) and the plans of some files (left as they were, every file after a walk or with a disk away, the room of a pending copy, a group of colocate, a file above, the guard's count); in the daemon, `TestTheScopeOfAPassForEvents` and `TestTheDaemonLooksAtTheNamesItHeard`, a daemon on two disks that records a file, decides a deletion and a rename, and walks no disk whole after its start. Verified: the unit tests, the race detector on watch, daemon, jobs, catalog and `cmd/atoll`, lint on the default and loop-device builds, and 617 scenarios, on each of the three commits. Made to fail, 30 of 31 mutations were caught (12 of the look, 8 of the plan, 10 of the daemon's names); the one left: a name below a symbolic link is refused twice, by the look and by the opening of a folder, which follows no link, so taking away the first changes nothing.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-17** ("Oui, confirmés"):
  - past 10,000 names on a disk, a pass walks it whole, as so many looks cost more;
  - a look limited to names keeps the links of the guard (§17) in step only once the pass opened the disk, as on a disk spared: the link of a copy deleted there waits for a later walk, or for a pass that writes to that disk, and holds its data meanwhile;
  - a disk with a copy that moved, found by a walk of the whole disk, is walked whole again for it, as the catalog does not hold the name it was found at; the operations of Atoll's own view still walk their disks whole, as before (a follow-up in TODO.md);
  - the plan takes every file not `ok` at each pass, a manual library's included, and every file on the SSD cache, rather than a schema change for an index on the states;
  - with a disk in any state but online or standby, every pass plans every file.

## Prometheus metrics, 2026-11-17 (specs.md §23.1, §25.1, §29.3, §29.5)

Commit "Give the daemon's state as Prometheus metrics on /metrics", on `beta`, the seventh piece of phase 2b. Contract and vectors first: `api/metrics.json`, the three cases of `conformance/vectors/metrics.json`, nine cases of `config.json`.

- **The maintainer's answers** (2026-11-17): `/metrics` on the local API, on its socket and over TCP with its token, and also on `metrics.listen`, a new key, with no token ("Les deux"); the counts of the catalog taken at the end of each pass and kept in memory, the state of the disks, the UPS, sleep and free space as the daemon holds them (recommended); all four families, the pool and its disks, activity, health, and sleep, cache, trash and scrub; the names, types and labels pinned in a language-neutral `api/metrics.json` (recommended). Rejected: a recount of the whole catalog at each scrape, as `/v1/status` makes, which would break the 5 % of CPU with a scrape every 15 s; names free to change.
- **The text** (`internal/metrics`, pure): `Render` writes a `Snapshot` as the text format 0.0.4, each metric of the contract that has a sample, in the contract's order, its samples by label values in byte order. The binary embeds `api/metrics.json` (`api/api.go`), so the help and the order are the file's. A `Recorder` holds the snapshot between the daemon's steps and the requests.
- **The daemon** records: the disks of the configuration at the start and each reload; at each mount check, each disk's state, whether it sleeps, and its size and free bytes through a statfs; at the end of each pass, under the catalog's lock, the files by state, each disk's copies, trash and copies due for the scrub (`catalog.CountForMetrics`, one grouped read each, the scrub's own condition), each disk's reading of SMART when newer, what the pass did (operations by kind and outcome, the bytes of its replicas, `Outcome.Copied`), and the conditions of the alerts; each reading of SMART as it comes; each change of the UPS; what each slice of the scrub read. A pass that fails is counted.
- **The API**: `/metrics` beside `/v1`, `text/plain; version=0.0.4`, made from memory alone; `metrics.listen` serves it alone, any other path 404. `api/openapi.yaml` lists it.
- Tests: the vectors and the contract's rules (`TestMetricsVectors`, `TestEveryMetricHasAVector`, `TestFamiliesAreTheContract`, `TestStatesAreTheSchemas`), `TestCountForMetrics`, `TestTheMetricsFollowTheDaemon` (a daemon on two disks: a scrub, a copy, a disk gone and its alert with no channel, a pass that fails), `TestTheMetricsTellOfTheUPS`, `TestTheMetricsTakeEachReadingOfSmart`, `TestAPassKeepsTheNewerReadingOfSmart`, `TestTheAPIGivesTheMetrics`, `TestMetricsListenGivesTheMetricsAlone`, and the slice's operations in `TestTheDaemonReadsChangedCopiesAfterItsPass`. Verified: the unit tests, the race detector on metrics, daemon, jobs, catalog and `cmd/atoll`, lint on the default and loop-device builds, and 617 scenarios. Made to fail, each of 27 mutations was caught: the samples unsorted, a label unescaped, seconds with six digits, a temperature of 0 written, counts before a pass, sleep inverted, an alert's key whole, a metric the contract lacks written, a disk that forgets its counts, `metrics.listen` beside `api.listen`, the trash of a planned operation, another condition for the scrub, a replica's bytes, the scrub due from now, no space at the check, standby before draining, no alert without a channel, a failed pass, the operations of a pass or of a slice, a reading of SMART, the catalog's older reading, a failed disk's reading, the scrub, the UPS, every path on `metrics.listen`, and the metrics as JSON.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-17** ("Oui, confirmés"):
  - the metrics themselves, 33 of them: their names, their labels, and what each counts (`api/metrics.json`); a disk's state among the six a disk of the configuration can be in, `retired` left out as such a disk has left it; a condition of an alert as `atoll_alert{alert, disk}`, its key cut at its first colon;
  - the conditions of the alerts are worked out after each pass even with no channel set, for the metrics alone, so that Alertmanager can do without Atoll's channels; the table `alerts` stays as it was;
  - `metrics.listen` on the address and port of `api.listen`, however written, is refused when the configuration loads;
  - the free bytes come from a statfs at each mount check, every 30 s, as a pass makes one; a disk it cannot measure keeps what was measured;
  - a metric with nothing to say has no sample: no size before a check, no count before a pass, no SMART for a failed disk, no UPS without `ups.enabled`; a value in seconds is written from integer nanoseconds with nine digits, the same bytes in both languages;
  - `atoll_copied_bytes_total` counts the replicas the passes made, those of files on the SSD cache among them: a transfer off the cache copies nothing, as it hands the user's part to a copy made before;
  - no new dependency: the text format is written by hand, rather than with `prometheus/client_golang`.

## balance.equalize, 2026-11-17 (specs.md §12, §12.4, §13.4, §24.4)

Commit "Place Atoll's copies by the most free share with balance.equalize", on `beta`, the sixth piece of phase 2b. Vectors first: five cases of `plan.json` and three of `config.json`.

- **The maintainer's answer** (2026-11-17, the recommended one): with `balance.equalize = "percent"`, the copies Atoll places itself go to the disk with the most free share of capacity, whatever `placement.new_files` says, which then decides only where a new file first lands; nothing is ever moved to even the disks, "at placement time only", as the spec's line had it. Rejected: moving Atoll's copies to even the fill, as DrivePool's equalizer does, and a key of no effect.
- **The plan** (`solver.Input.Equalize`): `percent` takes the place of `new_files` in the best candidate's order, after the listed disks, spreading and `colocate`, before `online` and the position (§13.4). Every plan reads it: the passes' copies, those of a file leaving the SSD cache, balancing's, `atoll replicate`'s, and `atoll capacity`, which plans as Atoll would place. Atoll's own view and the mergerfs line keep `new_files` for what is new.
- **The configuration**: `balance.equalize` no longer warns of a later phase; with `new_files = "percent"` it warns of no effect. The example of §24.1 and `deploy/atoll.toml` say what it does; the DrivePool table of §12 too.
- Tests: the vectors, which put `equalize` after `colocate` and spreading and before `new_files`; `TestEqualizePlacesAtollsCopies`, a pass whose copy goes to the most free share with `ordered`, and as `ordered` says when off; `TestCapacityPlansWithEqualize`; `TestTheViewLandsAsNewFilesSaysWhateverEqualize`. Verified: the unit tests, the race detector on solver, jobs, watch and catalog, lint on the default and loop-device builds, and 617 scenarios. Made to fail, each of 7 mutations was caught: the plan or the pass or `atoll capacity` that reads no `equalize`, the view that equalizes, `equalize` before `colocate`, and the warning missing or wrong.
- **Decided without the maintainer, confirmed on 2026-11-17** ("Oui, confirmés"):
  - every plan reads it, `atoll capacity` and `atoll balance` among them, as each says what Atoll would do; only the landing of what is new, through the view or mergerfs, keeps `new_files`, the SSD cache's included;
  - with `percent`, `fill_to` no longer bounds Atoll's copies, as with `new_files = "percent"`: a copy goes to a disk past it while it has room past `min_free`;
  - `balance.equalize = "percent"` with `new_files = "percent"` loads with a warning of no effect, where the file sets it, as `read.classes` with `oldest` does.

## The UPS through NUT, 2026-11-17 (specs.md §15.13, §20, §23, §24.4)

Commit "Read the UPS through NUT, and stop on its battery", on `beta`, the fifth piece of phase 2b (R-27). Vectors first: `ups_address.json`, `ups_answer.json` and `ups_power.json`, new, ten cases of `daemon_wake.json` and three of `config.json`.

- **The maintainer's four answers** (2026-11-17, each the recommended one): Atoll speaks NUT's own protocol to `upsd`, `GET VAR <ups> ups.status` every 5 s as upsmon polls, with no NUT program and no login; on battery, nothing that moves data starts, nor the hashes, the scrub, the catalog backup or the rescan, while passes for events go on and what runs finishes; with the battery low, the daemon stops its work at its next step as on `SIGTERM`, writes out each disk with `syncfs`, then only reads the UPS until line power is back, when it takes its work up again by itself; and a UPS that goes unheard is read as upsmon reads it, critical on battery and harmless on line power, with an alert.
